# Netrunner Workspace Roadmap

Canonical single source of truth for engine mechanics, client-server infrastructure, single-player card data, and AI bot development across `netrunner_core`, `netrunner_bots`, `netrunner_single_player`, `netrunner_server`, `netrunner_cli`, `netrunner_gym`, `netrunner_selfplay`, and `netrunner_card_sync`.

**Current goal:** a solid single-player Netrunner, then expansion into network play.

**Working order:** Phase 1 §2 (UI legibility — **done**) → Phase 1.5 (Session Unification — **done**) → Phase 1 §1 (Saved Decks — **done**) → the Rules Audit (Tier 1 and Tier 2 — **done**) → Phase 1.75 (Learn to Play — **done**) → Phase 2 §5's measurement repair (`determinize` made reproducible — **done**, September 2026) → Phase 4 §1's per-viewer masking of the action log (**done**, September 2026 — it turned out to be a live leak, not a future feature). → Phase 2 §2's JSON-Lines recorder and replay viewer (**done**, September 2026) → Phase 4 §2's reconnection handshake (**done**, September 2026). → Phase 2 §5's training-data volume (**engineering done**, September 2026 — sparse trajectories, unique seeds per iteration, a replay window, and the 96 → 2,400-game scaling measurement; the first 2,400-game run was stopped after five refused iterations) → Phase 2 §5's search and trainer repairs (**done**, 2 September 2026 — a cycle break for every `Seat::Agent` search, negamax at opponent nodes in both searches, and a mixed outcome/root-value target with a lighter value loss; the second 2,400-game run **finished nine of sixteen iterations, refused every candidate, and was killed by its own harness** — see Phase 2 §5). → Phase 4 §3's match registry and lobby queue (**done**, 2 September 2026 — concurrent matches as a first-class thing, rooms, a cap, a per-match seed) → Phase 4 §3's per-decision clock (**done**, 2 September 2026) → Phase 4 §3's spectators (**done**, 2 September 2026 — a `Viewer::Spectator` perspective at the masking boundary, the intersection of the two seats). Phase 4 §3 is closed. → Phase 2 §4's benchmark suite and Phase 3 §2's rating engine (**done**, 2 September 2026 — `netrunner_rating`, Glicko-2 per track and role; `netrunner_cli bench` rates every seating of a set of bots in parallel; the daemon rates human matches into `--ratings-file`). → Phase 3 §1's bot personalities (**done**, 2 September 2026 — `Weights` profiles on the shared evaluator, five archetypes, measured on the benchmark ladder). → Phase 1 §8's *Elevation*, deck by deck (**COMPLETE**, 3 September 2026 — all ten stages, all 82 cards, all 28 published sample decklists, 16 Corp × 12 Runner = 192 matchups; the set gate, the deck-covering sweep schedule, hosted cards, identity flips on both sides, run redirection and reversal, sabotage, run-end prevention, bypass, identity and score-area paid abilities, Dividends, play-from-Archives, `Sequence` continuations, the Region limit, purpose-restricted credits, forfeit, faceup agenda installs, threat level, counters on an identity, and an ability only the opponent may use). → Phase 4 §3's daemon deck pool (**done**, 3 September 2026 — `--serve` deals published sample matchups instead of a filler-padded fixture, so the games it rates are on the same pool every bot is measured on; the mirrored filler registry in `netrunner_cli` went with it). → the search baseline for those blindnesses (**measured**, 3 September 2026, Phase 3 §1 — Dividends is a leaf-valuation gap, not a horizon one). → `feat/eval-dividends` (**done**, 3 September 2026 — the leaf valuation exists and is pinned at the search budget it needs; play is unchanged at 32 iterations, and the barrier is now the search, not the weight). → the ambush lever (**done**, 3 September 2026 — an ambush's advancement tokens were worth zero, Corp flatlines roughly double on every seed, and `Trap` has the recogniser it was missing) → the paid-choice decline reflex (**done**, 3 September 2026, Phase 3 §1 — the Corp accepted 0 of 332 paid choices at every seed because accepting parks a further prompt and declining does not; a bounded upside on a parked decision makes it 11–15, and the three cards still declined are each blocked on a *different* missing term). Next: the damage/pressure term the Corp does not have — it is what *Phật Gioan*, *Measured Response* and the standing `opponent_grip_weight` question all reduce to — and the Corp's missing counterpart to `ACTIVE_RUN_WEIGHT`, without which *Anoetic Void* values the run it ends at zero; *Measured Response* itself the baseline showed search can already see past, so that half is a priors question rather than a missing term; then Phase 1 §5's format support; on the training side, the value head cannot learn until self-play is strong enough that positions decide games (Phase 2 §5), and the ONNX per-call cost is what makes a network-in-the-loop iteration ~90 minutes. → the training loop pins its engine (**done**, 3 September 2026 — a run builds once and runs that binary, every trajectory records the card-and-deck fingerprint that produced it, a corpus mixing engines is refused, `set_rank` appends a new set instead of inserting it, and stalled games — 24% of every recorded decision — are dropped from the window; the third 2,400-game-equivalent run is in flight at `-g 1200 -s 128`, read `checkpoints/iterations.log`). **Retrain before trusting any policy**: the audit changed the rules and the `ActionSpace` layout.

> Two items used to each claim primacy — §1 was labelled "top priority" while Phase 1.5 was "the highest-value architectural work in the repo right now." Both were accurate about their own axis and useless as an ordering. They are now scoped explicitly: §1 is the top **user-facing** gap, Phase 1.5 the highest-value **architectural** one, and the line above is the actual sequence.

**Health as of the last full review:** 1,181 tests passing, 0 failing, 2 ignored (network-gated live sync). `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets` completely silent. The engine's purity boundary, determinism model, and masking layer all hold, and — after the Rules Audit below — so do the twelve core rules that section found non-functional in ordinary play, every one of which had been invisible to the 844 tests green at the time. Both agent-driven sweeps now carry a rules-coverage gate and a card-conservation invariant, so "green" means every action class, every sample-deck card and every load-bearing event was actually reached, not merely that games ended.

---

## 🚀 Shipped & Verified Milestones

### Core Engine & Rules Primitives (`netrunner_core`)
- [x] **Phase & Priority State Machines:** `GamePhase` (`Mulligan`, `StartOfTurn`, `Action`, `Discard`, `GameOver`) and priority-based `PaidAbilityWindow` (ICE approach/encounter, pre-access, per-card access decisions, start/end of turn).
- [x] **ICE Stack & Jack-out Windows:** Dynamic `RunIce` resolution from installed Corp cards and four Netrunner-compliant jack-out legality windows.
- [x] **Access Phase Plumbing:** Multi-card access selection (`SelectNextCard`), post-access decisions (`StealAgenda`, `TrashAccessedCard`, `PassAccessedCard`), automatic on-access triggers, and interactive triggers (`PayToAvoidAccessTrigger`/`DeclineAccessTrigger`, e.g. *Fetal AI*).
- [x] **Deck-Out & Victory Resolution:** Start-of-turn deck-out checks, agenda point victory via `CardRegistry`, public Heap/Archives tracking.
- [x] **Icebreaker & Economy Primitives:** Rig state with `Encounter`/`Turn` strength buff tracking, subtype-gated subroutine breaking (`restrict_to`), `OnPlay` economy operations.
- [x] **Self-Reference Card Triggers:** `CardTarget::ThisCard` / `Cost::TrashSelf` dynamic resolution.
- [x] **Deterministic replay foundation:** `seed` + `rng_step` live inside `GameState`, so `apply_action` is pure and any recorded action history replays bit-identically (asserted by `single_player_test.rs`). This underpins replay, MCTS, and server authority alike.
- [x] **Legality by construction:** `rules::legal_actions` generates candidates and keeps only those `apply_action` actually accepts, so legality can never drift from enforcement. Costs a clone per candidate — do not "optimize" without a profile proving it matters.

### Network, Masking & Client Architecture (`netrunner_server` & `netrunner_cli`)
- [x] **Masked per-seat state (`ClientView`)** — perspective masking and fog of war for Corp/Runner hidden information, enforced at the engine boundary.
- [x] **Transport-Agnostic Channel Layer** — `ClientMessage`/`ServerMessage` decoupled from transport, uniform `MatchSession` execution.
- [x] **Real WebSocket Transport (`tokio-tungstenite`)** — `netrunner_server --serve` daemon, `netrunner_cli --mode remote` with `Connect` handshakes and two-human lobby pairing, verified live TUI socket driving.

> **Known shape, recorded deliberately:** the server sends **full masked `ClientView` snapshots** after every action, not deltas. That is the current design, not an oversight. See Phase 4 for when deltas become worth it.

---

## 📇 Phase 1: Single-Player Completeness (Immediate Focus)

Ordered by what stands between the engine and a person playing a full, satisfying game of their own deck.

### 1. Saved Decks End-to-End — DONE

**The framing changed, deliberately.** This was scoped as *deck import*: parse a NetrunnerDB JSON export into a playable `rules::Deck`. What shipped instead is a **saved deck format** — deckbuilding happens against the cards this engine actually implements, not against an external file a user can get wrong. `deck::Decklist` and `deck::validator` are kept, but reached by *converting* a saved deck into their numeric-keyed shape; nothing parses user-supplied NetrunnerDB JSON. Importing a decklist someone else built is a separate feature, and not one this bullet ever really described.

- [x] **`decks::DeckFile` is the one authored deck format — DONE.** The old `SampleDeck` grew `category`, `description` and `how_to_play` (markdown) and learned to load from disk, so the seven published NSG decks became ordinary decks that happen to ship embedded. One parser, one validator, one lister. `netrunner_core` still performs no I/O: it exposes `from_json`/`to_json` and the CLI's `deck_store` owns directories, reads and writes.
  - **`DeckCategory` defaults to `Custom`, not `Sample`, and that is load-bearing.** `decks::matchups()` now filters to `Sample`, and its consumers are all training or verification harnesses (`netrunner_selfplay`, `netrunner_gym`, both agent-driven sweeps). A deck file that forgets to state its category is therefore excluded from training rather than silently added to it — the safe direction. This lands Phase 1.75 §3's planned `kind` field early, under a different name.
- [x] **The three deck types and two validators are reconciled — DONE.** They are *not* merged, because they answer genuinely different questions; `decks::DeckFile::validate` is the single seam that runs both, so no caller has to know which to invoke. Gameplay executability is checked first — "references a card the engine cannot play" is more fundamental than "two influence over". The pipeline is documented in `deck/mod.rs`'s module doc. `agenda_point_range` stays the only shared rule; `MAX_COPIES_PER_CARD` stays duplicated on purpose.
  - **The deckbuilding validator had never run on anything.** `ValidationReport` had no caller in the workspace. It is now checked against all seven published decks, which spend 14-15 of their 15 influence — tight enough that an error in the influence model would show up as an over-budget deck rather than passing unnoticed.
- [x] **Wired to the CLI — DONE.** `--corp-deck`/`--runner-deck` resolve a built-in id, then a saved deck name, then a path, and validate with both validators before `GameState::setup`. **Play-time validation is a hard failure**: `setup` would catch an unplayable deck by itself but not an illegal one, so without this "legal" would mean something different depending on how it was checked. A saved deck may not reuse a built-in id — an error naming both, never a silent shadow.
  - New `deck` subcommand: `list`, `show` (description, how-to-play prose, cards grouped by printed type, legality), `validate`, `new`, `add`, `remove`. Editing re-validates and reports, but a failure after an edit is a **note, not a rejection** — a deck under construction is legitimately illegal, and refusing would make it impossible to build one card at a time.
  - Decks live in `dirs::data_dir()/netrunner/decks`, overridable by `--decks-dir` then `NETRUNNER_DECKS_DIR`. Writes are temp-file-plus-rename, same reasoning as `netrunner_card_sync`'s cache.
  - **Watch out when adding a CLI module:** `netrunner_cli` has no `lib.rs`, so `tests/onnx_opponent.rs` reaches source through `#[path = "../src/..."]` and the test binary *is* the crate root. Every module a path-included module reaches for must be declared there too.

### 2. Make Game State Legible to a UI — DONE
- [x] **Card counters reach `ClientView` — DONE.** `PublicInstalledCard::counters` is `Option<u32>`, masked on **exactly the same condition as the card's identity** (`identity_visible = owner_view || rezzed`) — `mask_installed_card` reuses that existing local rather than restating the rule, so the two cannot drift apart. `PublicInstalledRunnerCard::counters` is a bare `u32`: the rig is never masked, so there is no unrezzed state to leak from and no rule to express.
  - **`Option` rather than a `u32` defaulting to `0`,** so the view never collapses "concealed" into "rezzed and genuinely empty". The current TUI renders both as no badge, but the masking layer knows the difference and shouldn't lie about it; `zero_counters_on_a_rezzed_card_is_some_zero_not_none` pins that.
  - **The kind of counter is deliberately not in the view.** `counter_kind` is static `CardDefinition` data and every client already holds a `CardRegistry`; duplicating it would be two sources of truth. `netrunner_cli`'s `counter_label` resolves it registry-side and renders `", 3 virus"`.
  - **The doc comments were the real bug.** Both `counters` fields claimed *"no card uses this yet, so there's no visibility question to answer until one does."* **Twelve cards use counters** — verified end-to-end: a real System Gateway match reaches a rig holding `smartware_distributor` with 44 credit counters that its own owner previously could not see.
  - `PublicRunIce` still carries no counters field — no ICE uses them. Add it when one does.
- [x] **`GameState::turn` — DONE.** Counts **each side's turn separately** (`0` through both mulligans, `1` = Corp's opening turn), incremented at the single point a turn begins — `turn::enter_start_of_turn`, the only site emitting `GameEvent::TurnStarted`. Placed *after* that function's Corp deck-out return, so a Corp that cannot make its mandatory draw never counts the turn it failed to start; `turn` and `TurnStarted` can therefore never disagree.
  - **`netrunner_single_player` no longer reconstructs it.** `MatchHistory` records `self.state.turn` read from the **pre-action** state, which is what preserves the existing convention that a turn-ending action is logged under the turn it ended rather than the one it started. Recorded numbers are unchanged; the session-local counter and its `TurnStarted` watch are deleted.
  - `determinize` copies `view.turn` straight through rather than resampling — it is public information, and a search tree that disagreed with reality about the turn number would mis-evaluate any future "on turn N" effect.

> **Test-fixture cleanup landed alongside this.** `GameState`'s `Default` impl exists precisely so adding a field "fails to compile in exactly one place instead of across ~43 test literals" — but the literals had never been converted, so a new field broke 18 of them. They now use `..Default::default()`, per AGENTS.md's Testing Rule. The `Default` impl itself stays exhaustive, which is the whole point of it.

### 3. Rules Gaps Worth Closing
- [x] **Purge virus counters as a basic Corp action — DONE.** `PlayerAction::PurgeVirusCounters`: 3 clicks (the Corp's whole turn, since `CORP_CLICKS_PER_TURN` is exactly 3), zeroing `counters` on every installed/rigged card whose registry `counter_kind` is `CounterKind::Virus`. Scans **both** sides — the rule is about the counter kind, not who controls the card — though only Runner Programs qualify in the current pool. Closes the hole where the Corp had no counterplay to *Botulus*/*Leech*/*Fermenter*/*Conduit*/*Tranquilizer*.
  - Deliberately has no "nothing to purge" error, unlike `RemoveTag`'s `RunnerNotTagged`: purging an empty board is legal, just pointless. `legal_actions` therefore offers it whenever the clicks are there, keeping legality a mirror of the rules; if self-play ever suffers from bots burning turns on it, the fix belongs in `netrunner_bots`' evaluation, not here. (Measured: the System Gateway sweep went 5.95s → 6.26s, i.e. no step-count blowup.)
  - `ActionSpace::SIZE` 1024 → **1025**. The slot was **appended** as its own trailing segment rather than added to the payload-free `UNIT` block where it naturally belongs, so every pre-existing index still decodes to the same action — an exported policy's first 1024 outputs stay meaningful and only the head width changes. Same append-never-shift principle as `observation::CARD_VOCAB`. **Keep that property for future additions.**
  - Guarded at the data end by `purge_clears_counters_on_every_real_system_gateway_virus`, which pins the full virus roster against the real embedded cards — the engine-level tests use synthetic fixtures and would still pass if a shipped virus card were missing `counter_kind`.
- [x] **Rename `Cost::PurgeTags` → `Cost::ClearTags` — DONE.** Also `GameEvent::TagsPurged` → `TagsCleared`, so "purge" now means exactly one thing in the codebase (virus counters) with no near-miss neighbour. Rust-only; no card JSON referenced either name.
- [x] **Dynamic discard re-checks — DONE (defensive).** `discard_card` no longer decrements the count stored in `GamePhase::Discard`; both it and `finish_end_turn` now call a single `turn::cards_over_hand_limit`, re-derived from live hand size and max hand size every step. The stored `required` became a *report* rather than the authority, so `require_discard_phase` no longer returns it.
  - **Not a bug that was reachable.** `GameEvent::CardDiscarded` has no `dispatcher::dispatch_event` arm, so nothing fires between discards and the count cannot currently go stale. This is insurance for the first card that draws, changes max hand size, or deals brain damage mid-discard.
- [x] **Simultaneous trigger order — cross-side consistency DONE (defensive).** `both_sides_candidates` now tags each candidate with its side and routes through `order_active_first` via a new `dispatcher::turn_active_side` (reads `GamePhase`, which is total — unlike `current_actor`, which is `None` during `StartOfTurn`). Previously it emitted Corp-before-Runner regardless of whose turn it was, at the only two dispatch sites whose audience spans both sides.
  - **Not reachable either:** **zero** cards declare `Trigger::OnDamageAboutToResolve`/`OnTrashAboutToResolve`.
  - **Deferred-trigger queue — DONE, and it fixed a live bug.** Trigger dispatch had no equivalent of `Effect::Sequence`'s "stop if something parked" guard, so when a trigger parked a decision the rest of the dispatch resolved *underneath* it. Reachable in ordinary play: *Clearinghouse*'s `OnTurnStart` is an `Effect::PresentChoice`, so with any second Corp `OnTurnStart` asset installed (*PAD Campaign*, *Nico Campaign*), that second card resolved during Clearinghouse's pending choice.
    - `GameState::deferred_triggers` now queues the untouched remainder, drained by `dispatcher::drain_deferred_triggers` from **one** choke point: `engine::apply_action`, after every handler. Centralized for the same reason the `active_trace` guard is — and it covers trace and prevention-window resolution too, which a `pending_choice`-only drain would miss.
    - Every dispatch site funnels through one guarded primitive, `dispatcher::fire_plan`, over a flat `(card, trigger, target)` plan. Flat rather than a card list because `RunSucceeded` fires up to four triggers per card and a blockage can land between two of them.
    - `GameState::is_resolution_blocked()` is now the **single** definition of "something is parked" — it was previously spelled out inline in two places and, by omission, missing from the dispatch loops entirely, which was the bug.
    - **This is the engine's only continuation mechanism, and it is trigger-level only.** `Effect::Sequence` still abandons effects after a parking one; its doc comment's "don't chain two independently-parking effects" rule still stands for card authors. *Closed at Elevation Stage 5 (3 September 2026): the rest of a `Sequence` is now queued on the same deferred queue as `DeferredTrigger::continuation` — see Phase 1 §8.*
  - **Player-chosen ordering among your own simultaneous triggers — DONE.** `PendingDecision::ChooseTriggerOrder` + `PlayerAction::ChooseTriggerToResolve`: when 2 or more of one side's own cards react to the same event, their controller picks which resolves next, repeatedly, until one is left (which fires automatically — N triggers cost N-1 decisions, not N). Cross-side order is deliberately excluded: it's fixed by rule via `order_active_first`, not the player's to choose.
    - **Cost guard:** a decision is parked only when the order is genuinely contestable. Candidates are pre-filtered by `dispatcher::declares_trigger` — a cheap registry lookup, deliberately *not* a dry run of `TriggeredEffect::requirement` — so a lone reacting card fires directly. Over-counting (offering a choice where one option turns out to no-op) is the safe direction; silently picking an order the player was entitled to choose is not. Measured: the System Gateway sweep went 5.90s → 6.45s.
    - `ActionSpace::SIZE` 1025 → **1045** (`MAX_INSTALLED_PER_SIDE` slots), appended — same append-never-shift rule as the purge slot.

> **Deadlock found and fixed while landing the above** — pre-existing on `main`, not introduced by it. *Red Team*'s run-initiating paid ability was activatable during the Runner's **end-of-turn window**, which deliberately keeps `phase == Action(Runner)`. The run started, the window closed, and `finish_end_turn` handed the turn over with `active_run` still set — leaving the Corp with **no legal action at all**: `EndTurn` rejected by `CannotEndTurnWhileRunActive`, and the run not theirs to advance. Confirmed pre-existing by reproducing it at the previous commit (seed 9 of a widened sweep); the committed sweep's seeds 0..8 simply never reached it.
> - Fixed by `run::check_run_may_begin` — now the **single** definition of "may a run start", shared by `start_run` and `Effect::PromptChooseServer`'s park-time check. Those two must agree: `PromptChooseServer` parks a decision only `start_run` can resolve, so a narrower copy in one of them parks an unresolvable decision and deadlocks outright. That is exactly how the original bug arose (the park-time check tested only `active_run`), and it recurred mid-fix when the two briefly disagreed again.
> - **Sweep seed range widened 8 → 32 — DONE.** Two of the three deadlocks in this area were only visible outside seeds 0..8, so coverage was the binding constraint, not the test's design. 32 costs ~31s in a debug build (measured; ~1s/seed, linear), taking the whole workspace suite from **27s to 40s** — less than the naive sum, since other crates' tests run alongside it. Cheap enough to keep running constantly, which is what matters given the repo has no CI and `cargo test --workspace` is the only gate.
>   - Depth is `NETRUNNER_SWEEP_SEEDS`-overridable on the same test body, rather than a second `#[ignore]`d deep copy that would never run. `NETRUNNER_SWEEP_SEEDS=256 cargo test -p netrunner_single_player --release` verified clean at 87s — the first coverage beyond the 60 seeds checked while fixing the Red Team bug. See AGENTS.md's Testing Rule for when to run it deep.
- [x] **Post-action paid-ability window — DONE.** `WindowCheckpoint::PostAction { side }` opens after a basic click action so the **non-active** player can respond. Only their half was missing: `activate_ability` already permits the acting player's own paid abilities throughout `Action(side)`, so a window nobody but them could use would be pure overhead.
  - **Gated on the opponent actually having something usable** (`paid_ability::has_usable_paid_ability` — requirement met and cost affordable, via the new non-mutating `ability::cost_is_affordable`). Deliberately not implemented by probing `legal_actions`, which would recurse through `apply_action` without bound.
  - **Which actions count is an exhaustive `match` on `PlayerAction`**, not a check for a `ClickSpent` event — exhaustive so a new action fails to compile until classified, explicit so a click-*costing* paid ability isn't mistaken for an action. That is also what prevents a cascade: closing a window is a `PassPriority`, which is not an action.
  - **Measured, since step cost was the whole risk: none.** Sweep 31.11s → 30.79s; 256-seed deep sweep 87s → 85s; 40 headless games unchanged. `post_action_windows_occur_but_stay_rare` pins both failure modes with generous bounds — 63 openings across 8,956 steps in 16 games, i.e. real but ~0.7% of steps.
- [x] **Icebreaker abilities require an encounter — DONE, and it is what made the above affordable.** Real Netrunner only permits an icebreaker's abilities while encountering ICE; the engine offered Cleaver's "2[c]: +1 strength" as a legal action on the Corp's turn — affordable, permitted, and pointless. With a rig of breakers always answering "yes", *any* opponent-has-something gate fired on essentially every action.
  - New `EffectRequirement::DuringEncounter` on the `Paid` abilities of all 10 breakers (Botulus keeps the stricter `EncounteringHostIce`; Leech was caught by sweeping every card rather than a hand-written list). `Effect::BoostStrength` also now errors `NotInEncounter` at resolution, matching what `BreakSubroutines` and `ModifyStrength` already did — the requirement gates *offering*, the effect gates *doing*.

- [x] **Unspent clicks are lost at end of turn — DONE.** `end_turn` used to leave the ending side's clicks in place, reasoning that "every click-spending action is already gated by `engine::require_phase`, so leftover clicks are inert." True for *actions*, false for *paid abilities*: `activate_ability` resolves the acting side from card ownership whenever a window is open, deliberately bypassing phase — so *Regolith Mining License*'s `[click]: take 3[c]` was payable on the opponent's turn out of clicks that should no longer exist. Now zeroed in `end_turn`, before its own `EndOfTurn` window, since that window is part of the turn ending rather than more action phase.

> **Two things this surfaced, both worth knowing.**
> - **The post-action window's apparent activity was mostly this bug.** Its bounds test recorded 63 openings across ~9,000 steps; with clicks correctly cleared, *Spin Doctor* (cost `RemoveSelfFromGame`, no requirement) is the **only** System Gateway card either side can use on the opponent's turn — one copy, which must be drawn, installed and rezzed. The test now asserts only the upper bound (a too-loose gate would tax every action); "does it fire when someone qualifies" is covered deterministically by an engine unit test instead, because in-game occurrence is genuine luck.
> - **`MAX_INSTALLED_PER_SIDE` was too small: raised 20 → 32.** Real System Gateway matchups reach 23-24 installed Corp cards, past which `RezIce` had no `ActionSpace` index — and a legal action with no index is invisible to `get_action_mask`, so when it is the *only* legal action the mask is empty and `netrunner_bots`' index adapter panics. Pre-existing and confirmed reachable on `main` at other seeds; this change only altered trajectories enough for the committed seeds to find it. `action_mask.rs`'s claim that "real games essentially never reach these caps" was simply wrong, and its own advice — widen the constant — is what was followed.
>   - **`ActionSpace::SIZE` 1045 → 1357, and unlike every previous growth this one SHIFTS indices rather than appending** — the constant sizes 26 segments spread through the space. An exported policy needs **retraining**, not just a wider head. Keep appending wherever there is a choice; there wasn't one here.

- [x] **Every playable card carries its catalog join key — DONE, and it corrected four cards.** 19 baseline Core Set cards had no `numeric_id`, and since faction, influence cost, deck limit and set code all join from the catalog on that key, those cards carried *none* of it — playable, but silently neutral/0-influence/no-set to any deckbuilding check. The Core Set catalog is now embedded (`data/cards/core.json`) and all 19 are joined, pinned by `every_playable_card_carries_a_numeric_id`. That is what makes the slug → numeric mapping behind `DeckFile::to_decklist` total.
  - **The parity test then ran against those cards for the first time and found four real bugs:** *Account Siphon* cost 2 → 0, *Gordian Blade* cost 2 → 4, and both *Corroder* and *Gordian Blade* were missing their 1 MU entirely. Same class as the *Malapert Data Vault* bug the test caught on its first run.
  - **`Data Mine` (01076) cannot be represented and is excluded explicitly.** It is ICE with keywords `"Trap - AP"` and no Barrier/Code Gate/Sentry subtype, while `CardType::Ice` carries a mandatory `IceType`. Filtered by a reasoned `CATALOG_UNMODELABLE` list *before* conversion, so an unexpected failure still aborts loudly — the same discipline `SG_UNIMPLEMENTED` applies to coverage.
  - **The observation vocabulary needed defending.** Core Set's `01xxx` codes sort below System Gateway's `30xxx`, so a plain `numeric_id` sort would have pushed all 75 SG cards 19 slots along, invalidating every exported policy for a change that added no new card. `observation::set_rank` keeps whole sets from interleaving; verified that **zero System Gateway slots moved**. Keep new sets appending.
- [x] **Snare! models its printed text — DONE, and it gave `interactive_on_access` its first user.** The card had `cost: 4` (its pay-to-fire cost misfiled as a *rez* cost), an invented `trash_cost: 3`, and an unconditional on-access trigger. Real text: rez 0, trash 0, "when the Runner accesses this asset anywhere except in Archives, you may pay 4[c]. If you do, give the Runner 1 tag and do 3 net damage."
  - **`InteractiveOnAccess` was dead code before this** — fully implemented and tested, used by **zero cards**, exactly like the prevention subsystem below. Its semantics were also only half the story: Runner-pays-to-*avoid* (Fetal AI). Snare! is Corp-pays-to-*apply*.
  - New `dsl::AccessInteraction` captures both as mirror images — opposite payer, opposite polarity — so `resolve_access_trigger` is **one** function taking `paid` rather than two that could drift apart. `AccessPhase::PendingInteractiveTrigger` carries a `decider` because `current_actor` takes no `CardRegistry` and must still name the right player; `current_actor` gained a step for it, after the paid-ability window and before the phase (which is `Action(Runner)` throughout a run and would otherwise hand the Corp's decision to the Runner).
  - The "except in Archives" clause is a new `InteractiveOnAccess.requirement` plus an `AccessingArchives` requirement, spelled `Not(AccessingArchives)` with the existing combinator. Checked at *presentation*, not resolution: parking a decision that resolves to nothing would announce a trap that cannot fire. The "must reveal it in R&D" clause needs nothing — access already reveals.
  - `PlayerAction::PayToAvoidAccessTrigger` → `PayAccessTrigger`, since paying no longer implies avoiding. Same "one word, one meaning" cleanup as `PurgeTags` → `ClearTags`; no `ActionSpace` index moved.
- [x] **Basic click actions were legal in the middle of a run — DONE.** `engine::draw_card_click` (and its siblings) guarded on `GamePhase` and open paid-ability windows but never on `active_run`, so the Runner could draw cards and take credits while parked mid-run — including while the Corp owed a decision. Pre-existing and unrelated to the access work above; found by a Snare! test asserting the Runner had nothing to do. Now one central guard in `apply_action` returning `RulesError::ActionBlockedByActiveRun`.
  - **The decision the entry was waiting on: a run suspends *actions*, not click-spending.** Exactly the `ActionKind::BasicClickAction` set is blocked. Run sub-actions, `RezIce`, `PassPriority`, trace bids and parked-decision resolutions are untouched — and so is `ActivateAbility`, **including a paid ability that costs a click** (*Regolith Mining License*). The rule being modeled is "you cannot begin a new action while the run action is in progress", not "no clicks during a run": `BreakSubroutineWithClick` is the standing counterexample, and it is *why* bioroids can't be click-broken on a run made outside your own action phase. Blocking click-cost abilities would also have needed a `Cost` inspection instead of the exhaustive classifier.
  - **Guarded centrally, not per handler**, for the reason already written above the `active_trace` guard: one site beats threading a check through ~14 handlers. It reuses `classify_action`, whose exhaustive `match` means a new `PlayerAction` cannot compile until someone decides whether a run suspends it — the guard cannot be silently forgotten the way 14 call sites can. It sits *after* the trace/paid-choice/decision guards, which are the more specific "nothing but this resolves" states and own their own rejections.
  - **The bug lived in the three run phases that are deliberately not checkpoints.** `require_no_window` was incidentally covering for the missing guard everywhere `paid_ability::open_window_if_at_checkpoint` opens a window; `RunPhase::Initiation`, `RunPhase::Success` and `AccessPhase::SelectNextCard` are not among them. `no_basic_action_is_legal_at_a_runs_non_checkpoint_phases` walks a real run through all three and asserts no window is open at each, so it pins the guard rather than a window standing in for it.
  - **`open_post_action_window`'s `active_run` bail is now unreachable** — no `BasicClickAction` can resolve with a run active. Kept: the three conditions read as one invariant and only that half is redundant. Its doc comment says so.
  - The Snare! test that found this now asserts what it originally wanted — `legal_actions_for(Runner)` is **empty** while the Corp owes the decision, not merely "the Runner isn't offered it". No `ActionSpace` index moved, so no exported policy needs retraining.
- [x] **`PuctAgent::search` could return no actions at all, and self-play died on it — DONE.** `run_iteration_loop.py` panicked on iteration 1 at `PuctAgent::search always returns at least one action`. `search` states its contract over the caller's `view.legal_actions` (real state, side-filtered) but expanded its root from `get_action_mask` over the **determinized sample**, then reported the *intersection* of the two. Nothing made that intersection non-empty, and its `assert!` only checked the input. Now the root is seeded from `view.legal_actions` — `MctsAgent::new_root` has always done this, which is exactly why MCTS never had the bug — and `search` returns one `ActionStat` per legal action, zero-visit if the search never descended it.
  - **The observed trigger is *Tāo Salonga*, and it is unrelated to the click-action guard above.** Every empty intersection found by instrumenting a 400-game sweep (10-11 per ~52,000 root determinizations) is the same state: Tāo's `PendingDecision::ChooseCards { source: OpponentInstalled, filter: Ice(Barrier), min: 2, max: 2 }` where at least one target ICE is **unrezzed**. The Runner's view masks an unrezzed Corp ICE (`identity_visible = owner_view || rezzed`), `determinize_installed` resamples it to a random ICE, and the caller's `ToggleCardSelection { ballista }` then encodes against nothing while `ConfirmCardSelection` is rejected because `selected` names cards the sample lacks. Disjoint sets, empty result, panic.
    - **The repro, for anyone who needs to see it again:** `netrunner_selfplay -s 200` with the uniform evaluator (**no ONNX model needed**), **game index 175** — which is also its seed — matchup `hyper_velocity_vs_planning_ahead`, **ply 64**, determinization seed 231. Games 0-119 are clean; 175 is the first failure. Only `game_index % 12 ∈ {1, 4, 7, 10}` can reach it at all: those are the brackets pairing a Corp deck with `planning_ahead`, whose identity is Tāo. Those coordinates come from a replay harness reproducing `PuctAgent`'s exact seed sequence, whose `choose_action` copy nested the temperature and cycle-break conditions as `else if` where the real one is `||` — the two can diverge only after a ≥8-long repeat streak, so **the ply is likely rather than bit-guaranteed**; the state and the deck bracket are direct observation.
    - **Both targets were unrezzed there, which is the worst case:** both were resampled, so `legal_actions(sample)` came back **empty** — the determinized root was not merely disjoint from the caller's three actions, it was a dead state. `expand` produced no edges, `simulate` took its "expanded but stuck" branch on all 200 iterations, and the search did nothing whatsoever before the panic.
    - The first-guess explanation — that suspending basic click actions mid-run removed an accidental safety net — was **checked and is wrong**. Game 175's decision is on **turn 4, with `active_run: None`, no paid-ability window and no trace**; its parked `PendingDecision` means `ActionBlockedByPendingDecision` had already made `GainCreditClick`/`DrawCardClick` illegal there, independent of the guard. Two further states dumped from the sweep agree (one during the *Corp's* turn), and the binary that panicked predated the guard commit by ~9 hours. The guard does thin out mid-run legal sets and so raises the general risk of a narrow intersection, but it is not this trigger.
  - **It had already happened once and been patched at the wrong altitude.** `reseat_selectable_cards`'s doc comment records the same panic and repairs it for `(Corp, OwnRAndD)` and `(Runner, OwnStack)`, with `_ => return` for every other zone/side — including the `OpponentInstalled` that Tāo actually uses. That patch stays; it treated one instance of a class, and this treats the class. `search_reports_every_legal_action_even_when_the_sample_disagrees` pins it: reverting the root seeding makes it report **0** of 2 actions.
  - **`determinize` was discarding public information, which is what made divergence common.** `InstalledRunnerCard.counters` and rezzed `InstalledCard.counters` are carried in the view and were simply dropped, making every counter-costed ability illegal in the sample; `hosted_on_ice` was dropped too, which fails `EncounteringHostIce` **unconditionally** and put every Trojan's ability permanently beyond the search. `bad_publicity_credits`, `bonus_run_credits`, `runner_cannot_steal_or_trash` and the Corp's `recurring_credits` were zeroed because the view did not carry them; it does now. An *unrezzed* Corp card's counters stay `0` — the view masks them precisely so they cannot leak its identity, and that is honest ignorance rather than a dropped field.
  - **`ClientView` grew five fields and no observation slot moved.** Each is genuinely public in the real game (a Trojan sits physically on its host; recurring credits are visible tokens) and carries a doc comment saying why, matching the existing "never masked" entries. `observation.rs` is deliberately untouched, so the vocabulary is unchanged and **no policy needs retraining**.
  - **A second defect, found while diagnosing: the policy targets were mislabelled.** `ActionStat::index`/`visit_counts` are indices in the *determinized* state's encoding, but `netrunner_selfplay` recorded them beside an observation encoded from the **real** state, and `netrunner_gym` decodes against the real state. `determinize_installed` rebuilds `corp.installed` in view order rather than install order, so the same action generally lands on a different slot in the two spaces. Targets are now re-keyed against `session.state()` — in the harness, not inside `PuctAgent`, because an agent seeing anything but its `ClientView` would breach the fog-of-war boundary. `ActionStat::index` is now `Option<usize>` and documents which space it is in.
  - `choose_action` returns `Option` rather than `expect`-ing. The contract makes an empty list unreachable, but a training run is unattended for millions of games and killing it over one decision is the wrong trade; the ply is played and simply not recorded.
  - **Left open deliberately at the time — now closed from the other end.** The search *tolerated* the Tāo divergence rather than modelling it: a `ToggleCardSelection` naming a card the sample lacked became a dead branch valued `0.0`, so the swap was never meaningfully searched. The plan was to extend `reseat_selectable_cards` to re-seat every revealed target. Instead the `InstallId` work below removed the cause — a positional `ToggleCardSelection` always addresses *some* card in the sample — and `reseat_selectable_cards` is gone. The residue is filter mismatch only (the sampled card at that position may not match the decision's `CardFilter`), which needs a `CardRegistry` and a fresh measurement before anything is built for it. The earlier figures, for whoever takes that on: the field-preservation fixes alone moved empty intersections 11 → 10 and dropped `AcceptPendingPaidChoice` misses 4 → 0, but left 442 `ToggleCardSelection`, 196 `SelectCardToAccess` and 136 `PlayOperation` drops.
- [x] **`legal_actions_for(Runner)` leaked an unrezzed Corp ICE's identity, and `ActionSpace` aliased duplicate card ids — BOTH DONE, by one handle.** An installed card is now named by `state::InstallId`, a public per-instance handle allocated at install time, and every action that targets an install carries it instead of a `CardId`.
  - **The leak.** `pending_choice::zone_card_ids(OpponentInstalled)` read `corp.installed` off the real state, so *Tāo Salonga*'s parked `ChooseCards` offered the Runner `ToggleCardSelection { card_id }` naming ICE their own `ClientView` masks to `card: None`. Fog of war is meant to be structural at the `ClientView` boundary (AGENTS.md §2) and this routed around it. It is also *why* the sampled and real action sets diverged in the PUCT crash above.
  - **Withdrawing the action was never the fix, and that framing is what shaped everything else.** Real Netrunner lets the Runner swap ICE they cannot identify and host a Trojan on unrezzed ICE — they can see it on the table, they just don't know what it is. The bug was that the action named the card's *identity* where the rules give only its *position*. Filtering those actions out would have been a rules regression wearing a security fix's clothes.
  - **A second leak of the same shape, not previously listed: `InstallProgramOnIce`.** `install_program_on_ice_candidates` pairs every Trojan with every installed ICE — "rezzed or not", by its own doc comment — and carried the host's real `CardId`. **Latent rather than live**, and the reason is worth keeping: `legal_actions` keeps only candidates `apply_action` accepts, the candidate is built with `memory_cost: 0`, and the handler demands the registry's declared value (`1` for both *Botulus* and *Tranquilizer*), so it was filtered out before reaching any view. See the open bullet below — that filter is its own bug, and fixing it must not reopen this hole.
  - **`ToggleCardSelection` takes a zone *position*, not an `InstallId`** — its referent is a zone slot, often not an install (HQ, grip, stack), so one uniform representation beats a payload that varies by zone. `PendingDecision::ChooseCards::selected` became `Vec<usize>` with it, which is what makes `PublicGameState::pending_decision`'s "carries no hidden information" pass-through honest again rather than merely unchecked.
    - This **subsumed the copy-cycling hack**: `resolve_toggle_card_selection` cycled through copy counts because a `CardId` could not name the second of two identical cards (the *Carnivore* deadlock at §3's note below). Distinct positions are distinct, so the machinery is gone and the regression test still passes.
  - **`ActionSpace::SIZE` is unchanged at 1357 and no segment moved, so no policy needs retraining.** Every affected action was *already* encoded by position into `corp.installed`/`runner.rig`/the selection's zone — only the `PlayerAction` payloads changed, to carry the position the encoding always used. `bounded_position_installed`/`bounded_position_rig` now search by id, which is the whole of the aliasing fix: slots that were previously **unreachable** (the second and third copy of a card) now are, so an exported policy stays valid but has never been trained on them.
  - **`SwapInstalledIce` and `RezInstalledIgnoringCost` take `InstallId`s too**, which fixes a silent no-op nobody had noticed: with two Barriers of the same title against Tāo, both `CardId` placeholders resolved to the same install and the swap exchanged a card with itself. `instance_matches_filter` is likewise keyed by position now — `UnrezzedIce` previously answered for the *first* copy, so one rezzed and one unrezzed *Tithe* reported the wrong one.
  - **`determinize` has to agree with the real state about install order, and did not — this cost a livelock.** `ServerView` groups installs by server, so rebuilding `corp.installed` by chaining those groups produced a different ordering than the state the view came from. With positional actions that is fatal rather than untidy: at sweep seed 40 (`discretion_advised vs planning_ahead`, on Tāo's swap) *both* of the Runner's `ToggleCardSelection` positions decoded to non-Barriers in the sample, so `HeuristicAgent` scored neither, fell through to its `legal_actions[0]` fallback, and toggled the same position on and off until the 10,000-step budget ran out. `PublicInstalledCard` now carries `position` and `determinize` reassembles in it. That fallback's comment called the divergence "hypothetical, not an expected case"; it was routine.
  - **`reseat_selectable_cards` is deleted, not merely unused.** It existed to repair `CardId`-keyed selection targets against a resampled hidden zone. Zone *counts* are public and preserved, so a position the caller may submit always addresses some card in the sample and there is nothing left to re-seat. What remains is narrower and needs a `CardRegistry` that function never took: the card sampled *at* that position may not match the decision's `CardFilter`. That is search quality, not a crash — this closes line 127's "worth doing next" by removing the problem rather than extending the repair.
  - **The gate this was really for:** `no_client_view_or_log_entry_ever_names_a_card_it_conceals` (`netrunner_session`'s sweep) asserts that no card a view hides appears in its `legal_actions` or `pending_decision`, across every seed and both seats. It is checked over the `Debug` rendering rather than a match on all 40-odd `PlayerAction` variants, so a new variant carrying a `CardId` is covered the day it is added. Verified non-vacuous by feeding it a known leak, which it caught at seed 0.
- [x] **No program could be installed through `legal_actions` at all — DONE.** Not just Trojans: **all 14 playable programs declare a memory cost** (13 at 1 MU, *Mayfly* at 2), and both candidate generators built the install action with `memory_cost: 0` while the handlers treated `CardDefinition::memory_cost` as authoritative. `legal_actions` keeps only candidates `apply_action` accepts, so every one was discarded. Measured before and after on 24 heuristic-vs-heuristic games: **0 program installs → 3**. No bot Runner had ever fielded an icebreaker, and every self-play game recorded before this had a Runner who could not install one.
  - **How it arose, because the shape recurs.** The comment justifying the `0` — *"No data-driven memory-unit stat exists on `CardDefinition`"* — was **true when written** (`4bc279e`). `8aca72a` added the `memory_cost` field and the handler's check five days later and *edited that very comment*, renaming `Card` → `CardDefinition` without re-checking its claim. A comment updated mechanically is a comment nobody re-read.
  - **The field is gone from the action rather than populated, and that was forced.** `ActionSpace::index_of` ignores `memory_cost` and `action_at` takes **no `CardRegistry`**, so it could only ever synthesise `0`: populating the candidates correctly would have left the index/RL path just as broken and made `assert_roundtrips` fail. `InstallProgram { card_id }` / `InstallProgramOnIce { card_id, host }` now carry no cost, the handlers read the registry they already hold, and `RulesError::MismatchedMemoryCost` is deleted as unconstructible. `ActionSpace::SIZE` is unchanged — the field was never in the index space.
  - **Fixing it woke a dormant second bug, so memory is now derived rather than spent.** `MemoryUnits::spend` was called only by the two install handlers and refunded by **none** of the five paths a rig card can leave play by; nothing noticed because nothing was ever installed. New `rules::memory::available_memory` derives the budget as base 4 + every installed card's `memory_bonus` − every installed card's `memory_cost`, and `RunnerState::memory_units` became a cached **report** refreshed at one choke point in `engine::apply_action`, beside `dispatcher::drain_deferred_triggers`. Same move, and the same rationale, as `GamePhase::Discard`'s `required` becoming a report once `turn::cards_over_hand_limit` re-derived it: five refund sites are five chances for a sixth to be added without one.
    - **A console's `+1[mu]` is now removed when the console is trashed**, which `install_hardware` had recorded as a deliberate shortcut ("threading a `CardRegistry` through every trash path would be a much larger refactor"). Deriving made it free. `max_hand_size_bonus` deliberately stays one-way: Agendas and identities contribute to it as well as Hardware, so summing the rig would not reproduce it.
  - **The gap that hid all of this:** nothing exercised a memory-cost-bearing program through candidate generation → mask → decode. `a_program_with_a_memory_cost_is_offered_and_round_trips` does, and was confirmed to fail before the fix. `trashing_a_program_frees_its_memory`, `a_trashed_console_takes_its_memory_bonus_with_it` and `a_full_rig_stops_offering_further_program_installs` cover the derived budget — the last asserting the limit is visible through `legal_actions`, since a bot never submits an action it was not offered. All three were checked against a probe on the pre-fix commit that pinned the old behaviour and passed there.
  - **`hosting_a_trojan_on_unrezzed_ice_resolves_without_naming_the_ice` stopped being vacuous.** `InstallProgramOnIce` now reaches a `ClientView` for the first time, so the fog-of-war assertion it carries does real work.
  - **No `ActionSpace` or observation change, but a distribution shift.** MU already had an observation slot (`observation.rs`, normalised by `MAX_MEMORY_UNITS`); it was simply constant. An exported policy stays structurally valid and now sees a feature that varies.
  - **Two consequences are tracked as open work in Phase 2 §5:** `HeuristicAgent` still almost never installs a program (its evaluator cannot value an icebreaker), and every self-play game recorded before this predates working icebreakers entirely.

> **Note — the prevention subsystem is currently dead code.** `Effect::PreventDamage`/`PreventTrash`, `state::PendingPrevention`, and `WindowCheckpoint::Prevention` are fully implemented, wired into `evaluate_effect`, and tested, but **zero cards use them**, so no real game reaches any of it. Worth knowing before extending it — and worth a card to exercise it before trusting it.

### 4. Engine Hygiene Before the Next Set
- [x] **Effect resolution context — DONE.** `ability::ResolutionContext` is threaded through `evaluate_effect` and `check_requirement`, carrying the acting card (absorbed from the old `acting_card` parameter, so arity is unchanged), the triggering event, and any cards a `DealDamage` discarded earlier in the same `Sequence`. Built at the top of a resolution, dropped when it ends, never serialized. See AGENTS.md's State Hygiene Rule for the current guidance.
  - **Only two of the three fields were scratchpad.** `last_discarded_cards` and `last_advancement_was_first` are gone. **`last_completed_run` stays on `GameState` and is not debt:** `Trigger::OnRunEnded` can be deferred into `deferred_triggers` and fire on a *later* `PlayerAction`, by which point no resolution context exists — and it is the dispatcher's only handle on `persistent_after_trash` cards the Runner trashed mid-run, since `active_run` is already cleared. Moving it would have broken *Zahya Sadeghi* and *AMAZE Amusements* in exactly the cases the deferred-trigger queue was built to fix.
  - **`WasFirstAdvancementThisCard` needed no context field at all.** `GameEvent::CardAdvanced` already carries `advancement_tokens`, and `== 1` *is* "this was the first" — the `GameState` field held nothing the event didn't. Making the triggering event visible deleted it outright and left a general mechanism behind: the next trigger needing its own event's payload reads `ctx.triggering_event`.
  - **`DeferredTrigger` gained `event: Option<GameEvent>`**, so a deferred trigger rebuilds the context it would have had. Without it, deferring an `OnAdvance` would silently report "not first" for an advancement that was — the same stale-read class this refactor removed, reappearing at the defer boundary. Guarded by `a_deferred_trigger_still_sees_the_event_that_fired_it`.
  - `damage::apply_damage` now returns `(Vec<GameEvent>, Vec<CardId>)` rather than writing discards to state. Returned explicitly, not derived from the events: the flatline path empties the grip without emitting a `CardDiscarded` per card.
  - Pure refactor — 774 tests passing before and after, with no test's expectations changed.
- [ ] **Per-card recurring credits — deliberately deferred, not forgotten.** `CorpState::recurring_credits` is a single Corp-wide pool sourced only from the identity. Real recurring credits are per-card (*Cyberfeeder*, *Net Mercur*), and the pool would need to move onto `InstalledCard`/`InstalledRunnerCard`. **Do not build this until a card needs it:**
  - **No card needs it.** `recurring_credits` is declared by exactly one card — *NBN: Making News*, an identity — and spent in exactly one place, the Corp's trace bid.
  - **The design is not knowable yet.** Real per-card recurring credits are *purpose-restricted* (*Cyberfeeder*: install/icebreaker costs; *Net Mercur*: stealth). That needs a restriction vocabulary in the DSL, and inventing one with no card to drive it is a guess — against §6's own "built on demand against real cards" principle.
  - **The precedent is right here in this document:** the prevention subsystem is fully implemented, wired into `evaluate_effect`, and tested, and *zero cards use it*. This would be the second such dead subsystem.
  - **The seam is already located** for whenever a card arrives: `Cost::Credits` in `ability.rs` resolves a waterfall (bad publicity → bonus run → recurring → wallet); per-card pools slot in as a list at the recurring step. Recorded so the analysis isn't redone.

### 5. Format Support
- [ ] **Null Signal Games Format Support.** Enforce the official [supported formats](https://nullsignal.games/players/supported-formats/) (Startup, Standard, Eternal, Snapshot) — rotation tracking, banlists, points restrictions, legality checks before match start. `format.rs` currently holds a deliberately small illustrative seed scoped to the embedded sets, and says so honestly; it is not a claim of NSG's authoritative current rotation.

### 6. Card Data & Ingestion — DONE
- [x] **Unified Card Model:** the two previously-parallel card systems collapsed into one `dsl::CardDefinition` + `CardRegistry`, cross-referenceable by registry `id` or `numeric_id`. `rules::deck::validate_deck` rejects any deck referencing an `is_playable: false` card, so `GameState::setup` can never receive a catalog-only card.
- [x] **JSON Card Loading:** cards authored one-per-file under `data/{corp,runner}/`, concatenated by `build.rs` and embedded via `include_str!`. `cards::register_playable_cards` serves every playable card to every consumer with no feature flag and no runtime I/O. The hardcoded Rust card builders are deleted — JSON is the single source of truth. Printed metadata is joined from the embedded NetrunnerDB catalog on `numeric_id` rather than restated per card (this parity test caught a real bug on first run: *Malapert Data Vault* rezzing for 0 instead of 1).
- [x] **Bundled Gateway & Elevation sets**, embedded for offline, zero-I/O availability.
- [x] **Dedicated Sync & Cache Crate (`netrunner_card_sync`):** async NetrunnerDB API v2 sync and cross-platform disk caching (`dirs::cache_dir()`), wired into `netrunner_cli cards {list-sets,sync}`, without polluting `netrunner_core`.
- [x] **Schema additions, all built on demand against real cards:** memory cost, generic counters (`Virus`/`Power`/`Credit` + `AddCounters`/`RemoveCounters`/`Cost::RemoveCounters`), and hosting (`InstalledRunnerCard.hosted_on_ice`, `PlayerAction::InstallProgramOnIce`, cascade-trash-on-unhost) for Trojan programs. Upgrades hosted on ICE remain unmodeled — no card needs it.

### 7. System Gateway Card Set — DONE
All 77 printed *System Gateway* cards are implemented, tested, and `is_playable: true`, authored JSON-only — the first set in the repo with genuinely complete coverage, since Phase 1.75 §2 landed the two starter identities (September 2026). Coverage is enforced by `every_system_gateway_card_is_implemented_or_explicitly_excluded`, which requires each of the 77 printed cards to have an implementation or an `SG_UNIMPLEMENTED` entry stating why; that list is empty. **That test is the gate for calling any future set complete.**
- [x] **DSL primitives added, in dependency order:** foundational fixes (`CardDefinition::validate()`'s agenda-field bug, Upgrade installability, generalized `OncePerTurn`); new triggers (`OnRez`, `OnApproachServer`, `OnRunEnded`, `OnBasicDrawAction`, `OnAdvance`, `OnDiscardPhaseEnd`); decision primitives (`OfferPaidChoice`/`PresentChoice`/`PromptChooseCards`/`PromptChooseServer` parking a `PendingPaidChoice`/`PendingDecision`); `dsl::zone::{CardZoneRef,CardFilter}`; hosted credit pools; MU and max-hand-size bonuses; console-singleton enforcement; conditional cost discounts; hosting for Trojans; bioroid click-to-break; dynamic amounts and conditional strength; persistent-after-trash upgrades; facedown Archives tracking (with its own masking rule — orientation and count public, a facedown card's identity hidden from the Runner); and remove-from-game. `ActionSpace::SIZE` grew 724 → 1024.
- [x] **Two identities were out of scope *for competitive play*:** *The Catalyst: Convention Breaker* and *The Syndicate: Profit over Principle* carry `stripped_text: "Starter game only."` — no rules text exists to implement. They were the only two `SG_UNIMPLEMENTED` entries until Phase 1.75 §2 implemented them as blank, `unlimited_influence` identities.
  - **This was recorded as permanent; Phase 1.75 reverses it, deliberately.** The decision was correct while no tutorial mode existed — a blank identity legal in Standard is a deckbuilding hole, not a feature. But these are exactly the identities Null Signal's *Learn to Play* starter decks are built on, and "no rules text to implement" is precisely what makes them **trivial** to implement once there is a use for them. See Phase 1.75 §2.
- [x] **Three reprints share ids with baseline cards:** *Sure Gamble*, *Hedge Fund*, *Cleaver* — handled by keeping the single existing definition. *Cleaver*'s pre-existing definition had transposed paid-ability costs; fixed as a data-correctness bug.
- [x] **Engine bugs found by the bot-driven sweep** (all reachable in ordinary play, none by any per-card test):
  - `current_actor` ignored `pending_paid_choice`/`pending_decision`, so a decision parked *while a paid-ability window was open* named the window's priority holder instead of the decision's chooser — leaving a player with no legal action at all. Its precedence now mirrors `apply_action`'s blocking guards exactly (trace → paid choice → decision → window → phase). **These two must stay in sync; that is how this was found.**
  - `Effect::PromptChooseServer` parked a choice resolvable only by `run::start_run` without checking no run was active — an unresolvable decision blocking every action.
  - `ToggleCardSelection` enforced eligibility but not `max`, so a selection could grow past the bound `ConfirmCardSelection` requires.

**DSL growth baseline:** at System Gateway completion, **14 of 66 `Effect` variants are used by exactly one card**, against a healthily reused core (`PromptChooseCards` 17 cards, `PresentChoice` 16, `EffectIf` 12). Measure the next set against this ratio — see AGENTS.md's DSL Growth Rule.

### 8. Elevation Card Set — deck by deck — DONE (2–3 September 2026, ten stages)

The 21 remaining decklists on Null Signal Games' [sample decklists page](https://nullsignal.games/players/getting-started-sample-decklists/) are System Gateway + *Elevation*, and every one of *Elevation*'s 82 cards appears in at least one of them — so "embed the published decks as training decks" *is* "implement Elevation". The set lands one or two published decks at a time, in the order that needs the fewest new cards per deck (Runner decks first: they need the fewest cards and the fewest new primitives), and **the unit of merge is "a deck is buildable"**, never "some cards landed". Every stage extends the `Sample` pool, which `decks::matchups()` returns and every harness — self-play, the gym, `bench`, `--all-matchups`, both sweeps — rotates with no code change; `CARD_VOCAB = 192` and `set_rank("elev") = 1` were sized for this, so no policy is reshaped (the 19 Core-set slots move along by 82; no sample deck plays a Core card).

- **The gate is System Gateway's gate**, generalised: `every_elevation_card_is_implemented_or_explicitly_excluded` shares `assert_set_accounted_for` with the SG test and checks `ELEV_UNIMPLEMENTED`, a list that names each missing card's stage and deck and **only ever shrinks** — a card file cannot land without deleting its entry, an entry whose card landed fails as stale, and the set cannot be called complete while one remains. 73 entries after Stage 1.
- **The published lists are pinned in one table** (`decks::tests::PUBLISHED`: id, side, size, influence spent, agenda points), so the `Sample` pool is exactly the lists the table names and nothing else.
- **Sweeps play a deck-covering schedule, not the cross product.** `netrunner_session::sweep_decks_for_seed(seed)` pairs the `seed`th Corp deck with the `seed`th Runner deck, each modulo its own list, so every deck is played within `max(C, R)` seeds; at 16 × 12 the cross product would have spent the default 32 seeds on the first three Corp decks. Self-play, `bench`, the gym and `--all-matchups` keep the cross product: they want the pairing distribution and play thousands of games. `every_sample_deck_matchup_finishes` walks the same schedule four seeds deep (identical 48 games at the SG pool; 768 debug-mode games avoided at the full one).
- **The card-coverage gate scales with the pool** (`played_pool_card_ids`): at a given seed count it demands every card of every deck the sweep played in at least six games — two seeds × three seatings, the density the SG pool has been passing at — so a card in a deck the sweep never played is not a false failure. At 256 seeds every deck qualifies and the deep run demands every card, exactly as before. Rejected: raising the default seed count with the pool (16× by the end).
- **Measurement rule for a set expansion:** adding a deck re-rolls the matchup rotation, so the 96-game random-vs-random report is not byte-comparable across stages and is not diffed as one. Each stage quotes instead: the new cards' `cards` counters (seen/installed/played/accessed), the events its mechanics emit, the pool's end-reason split against the previous stage, and the bench ladder on the new pool.

**Status: complete.** `ELEV_UNIMPLEMENTED` is empty and `every_elevation_card_is_implemented_or_explicitly_excluded` passes over all 82 codes, which is what "the set is done" means here. What remains open is bot behaviour and the daemon's deck pool, listed under Still open below — not card coverage.

**Unlock order** (cumulative Elevation cards): 1 Flow and Ebb + Sabbatical (9) → 2 Enthusiasm + Tickets, please (18) → 3 Bowel Movements + Dashing Mad (26) → 4 Prick Thyself + Shootin' 'n' Lootin' + Professional Opportunities (34) → 5 Brick Stack (42) → 6 Brutal Efficiency + Agency (51) → 7 Fashion Lab + Pork Chops (61) → 8 Quick Returns + Glyph of Warding (70) → 9 Hidden Funds + Peculiarity (74) → 10 Fine Print + Gimbatul + Not so subtle (82). Mechanics the Corp half still needs, and which stage first needs them: cards hosted on a console as a pseudo-hand (2, *Madani*); identity flip (2, *Dewi*); sabotage as a Corp decision on the Runner's turn (3, *Cacophony*); "prevent the run from ending unless the Corp pays" (3, *Shred* — the unused prevention subsystem's first card); bypass (4, *Fransofia Ward*); Dividends and agenda counters spent at discard-phase end (5, *Off the Books*); play an operation from Archives then remove it (5, *Petty Cash*); advanceable ICE (5, *Syailendra*); the Region limit at `install_card` (6, *Mahkota Langit Grid* — see the Rules Audit's Forfeit and Region entry); forfeit (7, *Biawak*, generalised at 8 for *Plutus* and *Greenmail*); faceup agenda installs (7, *BANGUN*); threat level as a derived requirement (7, *Measured Response*); counters hosted on an identity (8, *AU Co.*); moving the Runner to another server's outermost position (9, *Proprionegation*, which also needed abilities activated from the score area); installing from the Runner's score area (10, *IP Enforcement*).

  - **Engine widenings the cards exposed:** `Trigger::OnRunStart` reached only the Runner identity (Side Hustle needs the rig); `HardwareInstalled` was never dispatched at all, so no hardware could react to its own install (GAMEDRAGON™ Pro). Both are the same "fire on the card and the identity" widening Programs and Resources already had.
  - **Engine bug found by the deep sweep's spectator fog gate, on the new schedule:** trashing an upgrade installed in the root of Archives on access was a no-op — `run::access::move_to_archives` skipped every Archives access as "already in the pile", so the trash cost was paid and `CardTrashedFromAccess` emitted while *Malapert Data Vault* stayed installed, unrezzed, in Archives' root (seed 55, Quick and Dirty vs Flow and Ebb). Reachable on the System Gateway pool too; the old `seed % 12` rotation never produced the sequence. Fixed to remove the pinned install and land it faceup; pinned by `trashing_an_upgrade_installed_in_the_root_of_archives_removes_it_from_the_table`.
  - **Known fidelity limits, recorded rather than hidden:** Scrounge's optional "add 1 program to the bottom of your stack" is chained through the mandatory install's `then` (two parking effects cannot share a `Sequence`, and one card's several `TriggeredEffect`s fire in one loop), so when no program in the heap is installable the optional half is skipped too. Scrounge and Magdalene cannot install a Trojan (its host is a choice no parked install models). GAMEDRAGON™ Pro's "non-AI" clause is unmodelled — no AI breaker exists in the pool. A Magdalene decision parks *after* the discard phase ends, so it resolves during the Corp's start-of-turn window; the turn still hands over cleanly (`magdalene_may_install_a_program_she_discarded_to_hand_size_and_the_turn_still_hands_over`).
  - **DSL ratio re-measured** over the 105 card files: 23 of 53 `Effect` variants are single-use (was 21 of 48), 5 unused (unchanged). Four of the five new variants are single-use for nine cards — a rate the Growth Rule says to watch; two of the four (`RefillCountersTo`, `InstallRunnerCardFromHeap`) already have a second card in the unlock order, and Stage 1 front-loaded the Runner side's mechanics.

Both 256-seed sweeps clean after the Archives-root fix (the view-path sweep's card gate now demands the whole 20-matchup pool at 32 seeds, and passes).
  - **Measured** (96 games, seed 1, `target/coverage/elev-1-*.json`, not byte-comparable with `trk-*`/`noise-hh-seed1.json` because the rotation changed): random-vs-random reaches every new card — Ritual played 16, Scrounge 6, Side Hustle installed 9 / cashed out 6, Principia installed 11 and its abilities used 19 times, Chromatophores 12, GAMEDRAGON™ Pro 10 (hosted 32 times counting turn-start re-hosts), Azimat 5 (39 turn-start refills), Devadatta Drone 8 (5 paid R&D accesses); Magdalene's discard-phase trigger fired 258 times. End reasons 40/38/18 → 47/37/12 (Corp flatline / Runner agendas / Runner deck-out) — the two new Runner decks are 45- and 40-card Shaper lists, so fewer deck-outs is the expected direction. Scrounge's optional "bottom of the stack" half never fired in 96 random games (six plays, each needing an installable program in the heap and then the offer taken). **Heuristic-vs-heuristic exposes a bot blindness, not an engine gap:** the heuristic installs Principia (10) and plays Ritual once, and never touches Side Hustle, Chromatophores, GAMEDRAGON™ Pro, Azimat, Devadatta Drone or Scrounge — its evaluator has no term for hosted credits, hosted hardware or a heap install; end reasons 42/7/47 → 40/10/46. Bench, `heuristic,puct@32`, 48 games on the 20-matchup pool: heuristic 1598 ± 198 Corp / 1465 ± 195 Runner, `puct@32` 1463 / 1425, no stalls — within the pre-Elevation band.
  - **Still open:** the heuristic evaluator's blindness to the six cards above (a heap install, hosted credits and hosted hardware need terms in `eval::Weights`); the `--serve` daemon still seats every match on the synthetic Kate-vs-HB filler fixture (`serve.rs`), not a sample deck, so rated human games are on a different pool from everything the bots are measured on; neither bot ever over-advances an agenda, so Dividends (Off the Books, Stage 5) is never paid in agent play — the heuristic scores the moment it can, and the random Corp scores seven agendas in 96 games. The heuristic Corp never activates an identity paid ability (LEO Construction) or a multi-click asset ability (Humanoid Resources) and never plays Nanomanagement (Stage 6) — its ability scoring knows credit-and-counter abilities only, so both Stage 6 deck engines run in random play alone. *Measured Response* (Stage 7) is never played by either bot in 96 games: threat 4, a successful Runner run last turn and 5 credits is a narrow window in games that end at 7 points, so its damage clause is reached by its per-card test only. Neither bot scores or steals often enough for the heuristic to value an agenda-counter engine, so *Sericulture Expansion*'s dividend (Stage 8) joins Off the Books' as reached by per-card tests alone. With the set complete, the standing list is: **the heuristic's card-specific blindnesses** (a heap install, hosted credits, hosted hardware, identity and multi-click abilities, over-advancing for Dividends — all `eval::Weights` terms it does not have) and **three cards reached only by their own tests** (*Measured Response*, *Off the Books*, *Sericulture Expansion*). "The bots do not play the long game their conditions describe" was the guess; the `puct@32` baseline under Phase 3 §1 (3 September 2026) splits it in two — search *does* play Measured Response, and never over-advances for Dividends, because the evaluator zeroes the over-advanced token at every depth. The `--serve` daemon's filler fixture was the third item and is closed (Phase 4 §3, 3 September 2026): the daemon deals the pool. None of what is left is card coverage; the gate is empty.

Reached through one new zone, `CardZoneRef::HostedOnSource` — the parking card's own hosted cards, a destination for "host any number of programs from your grip" and a source for "install 1 hosted program" — which is why `zone_card_ids`/`eligible_positions`/`plain_zone_mut` now take the decision's `source_install`. The same plumbing gives `CardFilter::NotSourceCard` ("Knickknack" O'Brian's "1 of your *other* installed cards", compared by install so a second copy stays eligible). `Effect::InstallRunnerCardFromHost` is the third source of `install_runner_card_from_zone_with_discount` (`RunnerCardSource::Hosted`).
  - **Identity flip** (Dewi; Nebula Talent Management reuses it at Stage 10): a `RunnerState::identity_flipped` flag with `Effect::FlipIdentity`, `EffectRequirement::IdentityFlipped` gating each side's `TriggeredEffect`, and `EffectRequirement::MemoryFull` over `memory::available_memory`. One identity card, two sides, no card swap. Both sides declare `OnSuccessfulRun`; the back side's requirement is checked in the same loop right after the front side parks its "you may", so it sees the still-unflipped state and stays quiet — pinned by `dewi_subrotoputri_flips_on_a_successful_run_when_memory_is_full_and_back_when_it_is_not`.
  - **Run redirection** (`RunState::redirect_on_approach`, `Effect::RedirectRunOnApproach`, Maintenance Access): applied by `run::engine::apply_approach_redirect` at both places a run step's events are dispatched, before dispatch, so `OnApproachServer` fires for HQ's root and never Archives'. HQ's ice becomes the run's ice list with every piece counted as passed — the printed text says *approach* HQ, and a later ice resync must not walk the run back to HQ's outermost ice. Public on `PublicRunState` (the event was played face-up) and carried by `determinize`. Emits `GameEvent::RunRedirected` before the approach it changed.
  - **Discounted effect installs** (`Effect::InstallRunnerCardFromGripWithDiscount` + `CardFilter::InstallableRunnerCardWithDiscount`, Illumination): the offer and the price share one discount so a card affordable only with it is offered. Illumination's "up to 3, one at a time" is three nested `PresentChoice`/`PromptChooseCards` levels chained through `then` — data, not a counter.
  - **Hosted credits for resource installs** (`HostedCreditUse::ResourceInstalls { subtypes }`, Open Market): `engine::install_resource` drains matching pools through the new `ability::drain_hosted_credit_pools`, which `resolve_trash` now shares, and `CardDefinition::trash_when_empty` trashes a pool the drain empties (the card's own turn-start take handles the other way it empties with an `EffectIf`, as Telework Contract does). `CardSubtype` gained `Fracter`, `Job` and `Connection`, authored on the eight cards that carry them, for Open Market's subtype test and for `StrengthModifier::PerFracterInHeap` (Rising Tide) — the closed subtype set rather than the catalog's descriptive `keywords`, per that field's own doc. `Amount::PrintedInstallCost` ("Knickknack" O'Brian). *Clean Getaway* and *Lie Low* are pure composition (`PromptChooseServer` with an `on_success`, a `PresentChoice` over `DrawCards`/`RemoveTags` — the first card to use `RemoveTags`).
  - **Engine bug found by the deep sweep (seed 126, Hyper Velocity vs Enthusiasm, random seats): a nested-parking deadlock.** `resolve_choose_trigger_to_resolve` re-parked the remaining triggers as a fresh `ChooseTriggerOrder` *before* firing the chosen one; when the chosen trigger itself parked a paid choice (Devadatta Drone's counter), two blockers sat side by side and each rejected the other's resolving action — the Runner had no legal action at all. Reachable since Stage 1 with three simultaneous Runner triggers (Dewi Subrotoputri plus two Drones was the first deck to produce them). The remainder is now placed after firing and goes to `deferred_triggers` when the chosen trigger left something parked, at the cost of the player's ordering of that remainder. Pinned by `a_chosen_trigger_that_parks_a_paid_choice_does_not_deadlock_the_remaining_triggers`.
  - **Known fidelity limits:** Madani cannot host or install a Trojan (no parked install models its host); an effect install's affordability check (`can_install_runner_card_from_zone`) does not count Open Market's credits, so a click install of a Job pays from the market but Illumination's offer of one prices it from the wallet; Illumination cannot install a Trojan either. "Knickknack" O'Brian's once-per-turn is consumed when the offer is made, declined or not — which is what "the first time each turn a run begins" means.
  - **DSL ratio re-measured** over 114 card files: 28 of 57 `Effect` variants single-use (was 23 of 53), 4 unused (Lie Low is `RemoveTags`' first card). Stage 2's four new variants are all single-use today; `InstallRunnerCardFromHeap` gained its second card.

  - **Sabotage as a Corp decision on the Runner's turn** (`Effect::Sabotage(n)`): parks a Corp `ChooseCards` over HQ whose bounds come from the two zones' sizes (at least what R&D cannot cover, at most what HQ holds), with `MillRnDAmount(Amount::RemainingAfterSelection(n))` as the `then` — `ResolutionContext::selected_count` is what makes "the rest" resolvable there. Nothing in HQ mills R&D outright. Both halves land facedown.
  - **The prevention subsystem's first card, by a different door.** Shred's "prevent the run from ending unless the Corp reveals and trashes X cards" is not a `PendingPrevention`: `Effect::EndTheRun` consults `RunState::end_run_prevention` (`Effect::ArmRunEndPrevention`, `EndRunPrevention::UnlessCorpTrashesRootCountFromHq`) and, the first time, parks a Corp `OfferPaidChoice` whose cost is the new `Cost::TrashRandomFromHq(X)` and whose acceptance ends the run — an empty root means nothing to pay and the run just ends; fewer than X cards in HQ means the Corp cannot pay and the run goes on. Armed by `PromptChooseServer::on_start`, the run-start twin of `on_success` (a `Sequence` after the prompt never runs: the prompt parks).
  - **Run-end riders** (`Effect::SetRunEndedEffect`, `RunState::on_end_*` carried into `CompletedRun` and taken by the `OnRunEnded` dispatch, Charm Offensive) with `CompletedRun::accessed_cards` behind `CardFilter::AccessedDuringLastRun`, plus `CardFilter::Rezzed`. **Random HQ hosting** (`Effect::HostRandomHqCardOnThisCard`, Detente — a Corp card on a Runner card's `hosted_cards`, which the conservation invariant now tallies by deck). `EffectRequirement::SubroutineResolvedThisRun` over `RunState::subroutine_resolved`, set at both sites a subroutine resolves (Phoenix); `CurrentlyAccessingNonAgenda` (Gourmand). `OnTrashedFromAccess` reaches the rig (Cacophony's count). *Rent Rioters*, *Hantu* and Phoenix's HQ trash are composition.

(2) The fog gate did not count a card hosted faceup on a rig card as visible, so Detente's hosting read as a leak; it was the gate, not the mask.
  - **The card gate's default density is now the pre-Elevation one:** `played_pool_card_ids` demands a deck's cards only after eight seeds (24 games), the density every System Gateway deck had at 32 seeds — at nine Runner decks Party Hard drew four seeds and *Wildcat Strike* went unplayed in twelve games, which is sampling. At the default count the gate therefore covers the four Corp decks (eight seeds each) and the deep run covers everything; the alternative, a default seed count that grows with the pool, was rejected for the inner loop's cost.
