- [x] **Stage 4 — Prick Thyself (Topan), Shootin' 'n' Lootin' (MuslihaT) and Professional Opportunities (Barry "Baz" Wong) — DONE** (`feat/elevation-stage-4-runner-trio`, 2 September 2026). Eight cards: *Bling*, *Transfer of Wealth*, *Maglectric Rapid (748 Mod)*, *Sang Kancil*, *Fransofia Ward* and the *Topan*, *MuslihaT* and *Barry "Baz" Wong* identities. **The Runner half of the set is complete**: every published Runner decklist is in the pool, 4 Corp × 12 Runner = 48 matchups; `ELEV_UNIMPLEMENTED` holds 48, all Corp.
  - **Bypass** (`Effect::BypassEncounteredIce`, `RunState::ice_bypassed`, `GameEvent::IceBypassed`, Fransofia Ward): marks the encountered ice's pending subroutines `Broken` and raises the flag; the ice is *not* passed on the spot, because the bypass resolves inside a parked paid choice while the encounter's own window is open — `resolve_encounter_ice` then finds nothing to fire and takes the `Continue`, exactly as a fully broken encounter ends. The dispatcher now fires the Runner's `OnEncounter` reactions first (the active player's) and withholds the ice's own when the flag is up, and `still_applies` stands down an ice reaction that was deferred behind the offer — "no further 'when encountered' abilities resolve". `Broken` rather than a fourth `SubroutineStatus`: every consumer already reads `Broken` as "will not fire".
  - **Identity paid abilities** (Topan's "[click]: install 1 card, paying 2[c] less"): identities are never installed and had no `InstallId`, so `PlayerAction::ActivateAbility` could not name them. `InstallId::CORP_IDENTITY`/`RUNNER_IDENTITY` are reserved ids `allocate_install_id` never reaches; `engine::activate_ability` resolves them to the identity card with a context pinned to the card alone (no rig position to go looking for), `legal_actions` offers them, and `action_mask` gives each side's identity the last slot of its `ActivateAbility` segment (`IDENTITY_ABILITY_SLOT`, capping installs one short) so `ActionSpace::SIZE` is unchanged.
  - **"As if they were in your grip"** (Bling): `CardDefinition::hosted_cards_playable_from_grip` seeds `InstalledRunnerCard::hosted_cards_playable`, and `RunnerState::playable_hand` — the grip, then those hosted cards — is the one list `engine::take_from_grip`, `legal_actions` and the action mask's hand numbering all read. Hand-size discards read the grip alone. Ability-driven installs (`InstallRunnerCardFromGrip`, Baz and Topan) still read the grip only — recorded below.
  - **"Whenever you install a card"** (`Trigger::OnCardInstalled`, fired for every Runner install against the whole rig and the identity with the reacting card as the acting one): distinct from `OnInstall`, which reaches the just-installed card and could not be widened without every "when you install this" card firing on every install. The three install events now carry `credits_paid` (what was actually paid after every discount; `serde(default)` for old histories), which `EffectRequirement::InstalledWithoutSpendingCredits` reads the way `WasFirstAdvancementThisCard` reads `CardAdvanced`.
  - **Runner-side audiences widened:** `OnRez` reaches the rig and identity ("whenever the Corp rezzes" — Baz narrows it to ice with `EffectRequirement::TriggeringCardMatches(CardFilter)`, since `rez_ice` emits `IceRezzed` for assets too); `OnEncounter` reaches them (above); `OnTurnStart` reaches the identity (MuslihaT); `OnDiscardPhaseEnd` reaches the Runner's rig (Bling). `Effect::HostRandomHqCardOnThisCard` became `HostCardOnThisCard(HostedCardOrigin::{RandomFromHq, TopOfStack})` — Detente and Bling share the hosting half — and `CardTarget::HostedOnThisCard` lets `TrashCard` send every hosted card home to its owner's pile.
  - **"If a run event is active"** (Sang Kancil): `RunState::initiated_by` records the card whose `InitiateRun`/`PromptChooseServer` started the run, `EffectRequirement::RunEventActive` asks whether it is a `CardSubtype::Run` event, and the existing `AbilityDef::cost_discount_if` does the rest. `Run` is now tagged on all nine run events (Tread Lightly, Jailbreak, Overclock, Charm Offensive, Shred, Clean Getaway, Maintenance Access, Illumination, Transfer of Wealth), the way `Fracter` was tagged for Rising Tide. `CardDefinition::ice_rez_cost_modifier` (Fransofia's "+1[c] to rez each piece of ice") is summed over the rig in `engine::rez_ice`, ice only, beside Tread Lightly's run-scoped modifier. `CardZoneRef::TopOfOwnStack` (a one-card zone, whose confirm takes the *last* copy), `CardFilter::HasSubtype`/`AnyOf`, `ZoneHasAtLeast { filter }` (so an offer with nothing to pick is never parked — Maglectric Rapid, MuslihaT, Baz) and `CorpCreditsAtLeast`. `resolve_corp_installed_target` resolves `ThisCard` through the acting install, so a `then` over the Corp's installs can say `DerezCard(ThisCard)` (Maglectric Rapid). *Transfer of Wealth* is composition over `CreditsLostThisResolution`.
  - **Known fidelity limits:** MuslihaT's "look at the top card" is only shown when the card qualifies — there is no private-look primitive, so a non-matching top card stays unseen; Bling's "as if in your grip" covers the four basic play/install actions, not ability installs; Topan's meat damage lands after the installed card's own install reactions rather than simultaneously with the install; Maglectric Rapid is offered only while a rezzed Corp card exists (the print allows a pointless trash); a determinized run carries neither `initiated_by` nor `ice_bypassed`, so the searcher prices Sang Kancil's boost at full and expects bypassed subroutines to fire.
  - **DSL ratio re-measured** over 130 card files: 29 of 63 `Effect` variants single-use, 5 unused (unchanged set). This stage added one variant (`BypassEncounteredIce`, single-use) and generalised one (`HostCardOnThisCard`, now two cards); everything else was a requirement, filter, zone, trigger or card field.
  - **Tests:** nine per-card tests (`topan_installs_one_card_a_turn_for_a_click_two_cheaper_and_suffers_a_meat_damage` … `fransofia_ward_taxes_every_ice_rez_and_bypasses_ice_when_the_corp_is_rich`, two for Bling), the pinned table (all three decks at 45 cards, 15 influence) and the set gate. Both 256-seed sweeps clean on the 48-matchup pool, first run — the first stage to find no engine bug in the deep sweeps.
  - **Measured** (96 games, seed 1, `target/coverage/elev-4-*.json`; three of twelve Runner slots are this stage's decks, so each new deck sees 8 random games): random-vs-random activates Topan 72 times (the first identity paid ability ever taken), installs Sang Kancil 9 (19 boosts/breaks), Fransofia Ward 4 (2 bypass offers, both taken — the first `IceBypassed` events), Bling 2 (4 cards hosted off free installs, 25 discard-phase trashes), plays Transfer of Wealth 4; MuslihaT's turn-start look fired 131 times and Baz's rez reaction 23; Maglectric Rapid was never drawn into play in its 8 games and is reached by the 256-seed run, where the card gate demands it. Heuristic-vs-heuristic installs Bling 3 and Sang Kancil 6 (4 uses), sees Baz 43 and MuslihaT 97 times, and never plays the rest — the evaluator blindness every stage has recorded. End reasons, random-vs-random, 42/44/10 → 40/44/12 (Corp flatline / Runner agendas / Runner deck-out); heuristic 34/10/52 → 34/9/53. Bench, `heuristic,puct@32`, 48 games on the 48-matchup pool: heuristic 1528 ± 183 Corp / 1617 ± 180 Runner, `puct@32` 1345 ± 164 / 1345 ± 166, no stalls — PUCT's Corp chair recovered 1252 → 1345 (trajectory drift on a re-rolled rotation, not a change to the search), its Runner chair 1375 → 1345.
- [x] **Stage 5 — Brick Stack (Weyland Consortium: Built to Last) — DONE** (`feat/elevation-stage-5-brick-stack`, 3 September 2026). Eight cards, the first Corp deck: *Bumi 1.0*, *Idiosyncresis*, *Off the Books*, *Kessleroid*, *Syailendra*, *Key Performance Indicators*, *Flyswatter*, *Petty Cash*. The pool is 5 Corp × 12 Runner = 60 matchups; `ELEV_UNIMPLEMENTED` holds 40.
  - **Dividends and a score area that holds state** (Off the Books): `CorpState::scored_agendas` is now `Vec<ScoredAgenda>` — the card, the `InstallId` it had while installed, and `agenda_counters` — instead of bare `CardId`s, because a scored agenda can carry counters and act from the score area. `CardDefinition::dividends` is read once, by `engine::score_agenda`, which turns every advancement past the requirement into counters (a field, not an `OnAgendaScored` effect, because the excess is only known at the moment of scoring). `Cost::RemoveCounters`/`Amount::HostedCounters` resolve through the score area by install handle (`acting_scored_position`, never by card — two scored copies each spend their own), the Corp's `OnDiscardPhaseEnd` audience is the identity then the score area, and `still_applies` counts a scored install as present. The Runner's stolen agendas stay `CardId`s: nothing acts from there. The view and masking carry the struct unmasked; `determinize` copies it.
  - **Play from Archives** (Petty Cash): `CardDefinition::playable_from_archives` makes the ordinary `PlayOperation` reach into Archives through `CorpState::playable_hand` — HQ, then the Archives cards the deck's list allows — the one list `legal_actions`, `engine::take_operation` and the action mask read, the Corp twin of Bling's `RunnerState::playable_hand`. The allowed ids live on `CorpState::playable_from_archives`, seeded once by `GameState::setup` from the decklist: the mask has no registry, and there is no single seam where a card enters Archives (forty-odd `ArchivedCard` pushes), so the flag could not be seeded per entry the way Bling's is. A card played out of Archives leaves the game (`CardRemovedFromGame`, `removed_from_game`) instead of returning; `OperationPlayed` records `from_archives` for `EffectRequirement::PlayedFromArchives`. "Play only if you have not finished an action yet this turn" is `GameState::actions_taken_this_turn` (`engine::counts_as_turn_action`: every basic click action and a run, not scoring, not a click paid as a cost) behind `EffectRequirement::NoActionTakenThisTurn` — on `GameState` because it must survive across actions, and not derived from clicks because Petty Cash itself refunds the click it cost.
  - **`Sequence` continues past a park** (`DeferredTrigger::continuation`): the rest of a `Sequence` whose effect parked a decision is queued on `deferred_triggers`, pinned to the acting card with its triggering event, and drains once the decision resolves — closing the "trigger-level only" limitation recorded under Phase 1 §3 (the "don't chain two independently-parking effects" rule for authors is withdrawn; no existing card had relied on the drop, checked by scanning every `Sequence`). Needed by **`Effect::ResolveSomeOf`** (Key Performance Indicators' "resolve 2 of the following in any order"), the composition primitive that rewrites itself into a `PresentChoice` of "this option, then the same offer over the rest with one fewer" — written out, four-choose-two is twelve duplicated leaves, and a first pick that prompts would have lost the second without the continuation. The per-resolution scratch (`credits_lost`, `damage_discarded`, `selected_count`) does not survive the park, and a continuation queued from inside a subroutine would drain after the encounter's remaining subroutines — no card has that shape yet.
  - **Smaller primitives:** `Effect::PurgeVirusCounters` (Flyswatter) shares `engine::purge_all_virus_counters` with the basic action; `Effect::LoseCreditsAmount` (Idiosyncresis, authored as two over `HostedAdvancementTokens` the way Account Siphon doubles); `EffectRequirement::AmountAtLeast(Amount, u32)` (Syailendra's "3 or more hosted advancement counters" — the comparison primitive over the `Amount` vocabulary, so the next threshold is an `Amount` at most); `CardFilter::Advanceable` (the same test `legal_actions` uses to offer `AdvanceCard`; Pharos already made ice advanceable); `PromptInstallCorpCard::ignore_costs` (Key Performance Indicators and Off the Books, offering every destination since affordability no longer prunes any); `CardSubtype::Trojan` tagged on Botulus, Chromatophores and Tranquilizer for Bumi's filter. A `PromptChooseCards` with nothing eligible now no-ops even at `min: 0` rather than parking an empty confirmation (Bumi with no trojan in the rig).
  - **Masking, two fixes found by the session sweep's invariant on the re-rolled schedule** (both pre-existing): `removed_from_game` is now projected into `PublicCorpState`/`CorpClientView` — with it absent, a log entry naming Spin Doctor's self-removal read as a leak whenever a second copy sat hidden — and a parked decision's `source_card` is concealed from the Runner when the card is (Longevity Serum's "shuffle up to 3 cards from Archives into R&D" runs as the card its first prompt just discarded facedown, and named it; seed 20, `advanced_yomi` vs `sabbatical`). `ClientView::actions_taken_this_turn` is new, public like `turn`, so a determinized search agrees with the real state about Petty Cash.
  - **Known fidelity limits:** Kessleroid's "the Runner cannot trash this ice" is not enforced — no Runner card in the pool trashes ice, so there is nothing to build the flag against; Bumi 1.0's and Flyswatter's "during a run against this server" is authored (`RezzedDuringRunAgainstThisServer`) but cannot be false here, since the engine only rezzes ice while it is approached; Off the Books adds the found card to HQ before offering the free install (equivalent, the card is revealed either way); Petty Cash is playable from Archives facedown or faceup.
  - **DSL ratio re-measured** over 138 card files: 28 of 66 `Effect` variants single-use, 5 unused (unchanged set). This stage added three variants — `LoseCreditsAmount`, `PurgeVirusCounters`, `ResolveSomeOf` — each with one card so far; `ResolveSomeOf` is the composition primitive the Growth Rule asks for, and Plutus (Stage 8) and Mavirus-style purges have second cards coming for the others.
  - **Tests:** eight per-card tests (`bumi_1_0_may_trash_a_trojan_when_rezzed_mid_run_and_its_subroutines_trash_a_program_and_deal_core_damage` … `petty_cash_plays_only_as_the_first_action_and_refunds_its_click_when_played_from_archives`; the Key Performance Indicators test exercises the continuation by prompting first), the pinned table (44 cards, 15 influence, 18 agenda points) and the set gate. Both 256-seed sweeps clean on the 60-matchup pool, first run.
  - **Measured** (96 games, seed 1, `target/coverage/elev-5-*.json`; one of five Corp slots is this deck, so it sees 19 or 20 random games): random-vs-random rezzes Bumi 1.0 17 times (16 mid-run rez reactions, 11 click-breaks — the first bioroid break of a Stage-5 card), Flyswatter 10 (10 purges by rez next to 111 by the basic action), Syailendra 13 (9 encounter reactions, 90 subroutines fired), Kessleroid 16; Idiosyncresis fires its turn-start choice 24 times and is trashed 23; Key Performance Indicators is played 22 times, Petty Cash 15, and 84 cards leave the game (Petty Cash from Archives and Spin Doctor together). **Off the Books never paid a dividend in either seating**: the random Corp scored 7 agendas in 96 games, and the heuristic scored Off the Books 17 times but never over-advances — Dividends is reached by its per-card test only, a bot blindness recorded in this section's still-open list. End reasons random: 46 flatline / 40 Runner agenda / 10 deckout; heuristic-vs-heuristic: 43 Corp agenda / 7 flatline / 45 Runner agenda / 1 deckout (Stage 4: 34 / 9 / 53 — the Corp half of the pool got a real deck). Bench (`heuristic,puct@32`, 12 games per pairing, seed 1): heuristic 1547 ± 182 Corp / 1477 ± 176 Runner, puct@32 1470 ± 161 / 1463 ± 164, no stalls.

- [x] **Stage 6 — Brutal Efficiency (Haas-Bioroid: Precision Design) and Agency (LEO Construction: Labor Solutions) — DONE** (`feat/elevation-stage-6-brutal-efficiency-agency`, 3 September 2026). Nine cards: *LEO Construction: Labor Solutions*, *Project Ingatan*, *Humanoid Resources*, *Otto Campaign*, *Scatter Field*, *Nanomanagement*, *Mercia B4LL4RD*, *Semak-samun*, *Mahkota Langit Grid*. The pool is 7 Corp × 12 Runner = 84 matchups; `ELEV_UNIMPLEMENTED` holds 31.
  - **The Region limit** (Mahkota Langit Grid — the Rules Audit's "Forfeit and Region" entry, closed on the Region half): `CardSubtype::Region` is the printed tag, `engine::place_corp_card` rejects a second region into a root that holds one (`RulesError::RegionLimitExceeded`, rezzed or not) and `engine::corp_install_destinations` prunes such servers out of every parked install offer. A rejection, not an install-over — the printed limit restricts installing, unlike the one-agenda-or-asset-per-remote rule beside it.
  - **Purpose-restricted credits on the Corp's table** (Mahkota Langit Grid): `HostedCreditUse::RezInThisServer` is the first Corp-side purpose — the grid's 2 recurring credits (`RefillCountersTo` on rez and at turn start, as Azimat does) pay to rez assets in its root and ice protecting its server, drained by `ability::drain_corp_hosted_credit_pools` from `engine::rez_ice`, the one rez path, before the wallet, and counted towards affordability there. Upgrades in the root are not covered, as printed. The grid's "Persistent → trash cost +2 for assets in this root" is `CardDefinition::root_asset_trash_cost_bonus`, read when an accessed asset's `PendingChoice` is fixed, from the server's rezzed root upgrades *and* from `RunState::persistent_trashed_upgrades` — the flag AMAZE Amusements introduced now has a second reader.
  - **An identity that trashes its own bioroids** (LEO Construction): `CardSubtype::Bioroid` is authored on Ansel 1.0, Brân 1.0, Bumi 1.0 and Mercia B4LL4RD (`click_breakable` stays the mechanic; Mercia is a bioroid with nothing to click through), and `CardFilter::InAttackedServer` matches an install in the root of or protecting the run's server — empty outside a run, which is what makes the `Paid` identity ability legal only during one with no "during a run" requirement. The trash *is* the cost: a `PromptChooseCards` over `OwnInstalled` into `OwnArchives` whose `then` ends the run, gated `OncePerTurn` and on a bioroid being there at all.
  - **A conjunctive cost** (`Cost::AllOf`, Humanoid Resources' "[click][click][click], [trash]:"): `AnyOf`'s twin — affordable when every part is, paid part by part straight through `pay_cost`. Its effect runs on after the card has paid itself into Archives: `dispatcher::still_applies` now exempts a `Sequence` continuation from the "install has left play" stand-down, because a continuation is the rest of an ability that already started resolving, not a trigger waiting to fire (the Side Hustle case that check exists for).
  - **Playing an operation as an effect** (`Effect::PlayOperationFromHq`, Humanoid Resources' "You may play 1 operation from HQ"): `engine::play_operation` is split into the click action and `engine::play_operation_card`, which the effect shares — `OnPlay`, the Transaction reaction, `play_requirement` and Archives filing all resolve identically, no click spent. `CardFilter::PlayableOperation` is the offer half, paired with `engine::can_play_operation_from_hq` the way the Runner's install offers pair with their checks.
  - **A rider on a parked install** (`PromptInstallCorpCard::then`, Mercia B4LL4RD's "If you do, move this upgrade to the root of the server that piece of ice is protecting"): evaluated after the install lands, as the *parking* install, with the chosen server substituted in by `pending_choice::substitute_chosen_server` — the same placeholder convention a run offer's `AddAdditionalAccess` uses. `Effect::MoveThisCardToRoot` is the move: not an install (no cost, no `CardInstalled`, rez state and counters kept), emitting `GameEvent::CardMoved`. `PromptInstallCorpCard::discount` carries "paying 1[c] less" into `place_corp_card`'s ice tax. A `Sequence` continuation could not express either half — it resumes as the *selected* card and never learns the server.
  - **A subroutine only a subtype may break** (`SubroutineDef::only_breakable_by`, Semak-samun's "except using a fracter"): every break path consults it — `BreakSubroutines`, `BreakSubroutinesUnconditionally` and the bioroid click break (not offered, `RulesError::SubroutineNotBreakableByThis` if submitted). Per subroutine, because the text says "the printed subroutine". A breaker left with nothing it can break now errors `NoBreakableSubroutine` instead of charging its cost for nothing.
  - **Smaller primitives:** `StrengthModifier::WhileOnlyIceProtectingServer` (Scatter Field, baked at encounter like its siblings — nothing in the pool installs or trashes ice mid-run); Otto Campaign, Nanomanagement and Project Ingatan are pure composition (Ingatan is Off the Books with `OwnArchives` as the source and `ignore_costs` on the install).
  - **Known fidelity limits:** Mahkota Langit Grid's rez credits are spent whenever the Corp rezzes an eligible card — the printed "you can" is a choice the engine makes for the Corp (the hosted credits are worthless otherwise); Semak-samun's `only_breakable_by` also blocks Botulus hosted on it, which the printed text agrees with; Scatter Field's bonus is fixed at encounter; the region limit rejects rather than offering to trash the standing region, which the Comprehensive Rules also forbid.
  - **DSL ratio re-measured** over 147 card files: 30 of 68 `Effect` variants single-use, 5 unused (unchanged set). This stage added two variants — `MoveThisCardToRoot` and `PlayOperationFromHq` — each for one card, and grew `PromptInstallCorpCard` by two fields rather than adding a third. The rest of the stage is fields (`only_breakable_by`, `root_asset_trash_cost_bonus`, a `Cost`, a `StrengthModifier`, a `HostedCreditUse`, two `CardFilter`s, two `CardSubtype`s).
  - **Tests:** nine per-card tests (`leo_construction_trashes_a_rezzed_bioroid_on_the_attacked_server_once_per_turn_to_end_the_run` … `mahkota_langit_grid_is_one_per_server_pays_for_rezzes_in_its_server_and_taxes_its_assets_trash_cost_persistently`; the Humanoid Resources test exercises the continuation past a self-trashed source), the pinned table (Brutal Efficiency 44 cards / 15 influence / 18 points; Agency 49 / 15 / 20 — the first 49-card deck, so `every_corp_deck_carries_the_points_its_size_requires` is no longer trivially 18) and the set gate at 31.
  - **Measured** (96 games, seed 1, `target/coverage/elev-6-*.json`; two of seven Corp slots are these decks, so each sees 13 or 14 random games): random-vs-random activates LEO Construction's identity 8 times and Humanoid Resources 10 (12 rezzed — nearly every rez is spent), Mercia B4LL4RD's action-phase-end offer fires 98 times and moves her 10 (`CardMoved`), Mahkota Langit Grid is rezzed 16 times and refills 79 (its credits are spent as part of 1,547 installs' worth of Corp play — the drain is not separately counted), Otto Campaign is rezzed 7 times and pays out 15, Scatter Field is rezzed 5 times and fires 26 subroutines, Project Ingatan is installed 32 times and stolen 19; Semak-samun is installed 19 times and never rezzed by the random Corp (Brân 1.0 is rezzed 2 of 43 — random Corps rarely hold 3[c] at the right moment), and Nanomanagement is played once (one copy in one deck). **Ingatan never paid a dividend in either seating**, the Off the Books blindness again. Heuristic-vs-heuristic: Mercia fires 156 times but moves once (the heuristic installs ice where it already has ice), Semak-samun is rezzed 8 times with 7 subroutines fired and 2 broken, Mahkota refills 134 times, Otto pays out 32 — and **the heuristic never activates LEO Construction or Humanoid Resources** (0 activations in 96 games; identity paid abilities and multi-click abilities are outside its ability scoring), and never plays Nanomanagement. End reasons random: 37 flatline / 47 Runner agenda / 12 deckout; heuristic-vs-heuristic: 27 Corp agenda / 6 flatline / 63 Runner agenda (Stage 5: 43 / 7 / 45 — two Corp decks whose engines the heuristic cannot drive dilute the Corp half). Bench (`heuristic,puct@32`, 12 games per pairing, seed 1): heuristic 1617 ± 201 Corp / 1632 ± 212 Runner, puct@32 1349 ± 171 / 1281 ± 166, no stalls.

- [x] **Stage 7 — Fashion Lab (Poétrï Luxury Brands: All the Rage) and Pork Chops (BANGUN: When Disaster Strikes) — DONE** (`feat/elevation-stage-7-fashion-lab-pork-chops`, 3 September 2026). Ten cards: *Poétrï Luxury Brands: All the Rage*, *Aggressive Trendsetting*, *Top-Down Solutions*, *Byte!*, *Mycoweb*, *Touch-ups*, *BANGUN: When Disaster Strikes*, *Anthill Excavation Contract*, *Biawak*, *Measured Response*. The pool is 9 Corp × 12 Runner = 108 matchups; `ELEV_UNIMPLEMENTED` holds 21.
  - **Forfeit** (Biawak — the Rules Audit's "Forfeit and Region" entry, now closed on both halves): `Effect::ForfeitAgendas` takes agendas out of the Corp's score area and out of the game, counters and all, so its score drops. An `Effect` and not a `Cost` because *which* agenda goes must be decided and only an effect carries the `CardRegistry` that knows what each is worth — `ability::pay_cost` has none, across forty call sites. The Corp is asked whether to forfeit, not which: `engine::rez_ice` parks a two-option `PresentChoice` (forfeit and rez 10[c] cheaper, or just rez) whenever the ice prints `CardDefinition::rez_forfeit_discount` and the score area is not empty, and the lowest-scoring agenda goes, ties to the fewest agenda counters. Points decide the game and Dividends counters are the only other thing a scored agenda carries here, so the cheapest is the dominant answer in every position this pool produces.
  - **One rez path, shared** (`Effect::RezInstalled`, replacing `RezInstalledIgnoringCost`): the old variant hand-rolled the flip and the `OnRez` dispatch under a comment saying the duplication was deliberate, since only the click action paid. Mycoweb prints a *discounted* rez, so the two had to converge: `engine::rez_install` is the whole transition — the run's and the rig's rez-cost modifiers, the region's hosted rez credits before the wallet, the `IceRezzed` dispatch — and `rez_ice` keeps only what is about *when* a rez is allowed. The effect swallows `NotEnoughCredits` and resolves to nothing, which is what keeps declining Biawak's forfeit at 4 credits from failing the action and stranding the decision that offered it.
  - **Agendas installed faceup** (BANGUN): `CardDefinition::may_install_agendas_faceup` is permission to *rez* an installed agenda, not a flag on `PlayerAction::InstallCard` — rez is already the engine's one "flip a Corp install faceup" action, already optional, and already in the `ActionSpace`. The difference is timing only: this Corp may wait and flip later. It also closed a latent hole in the other direction — nothing rejected rezzing an agenda before, and an agenda's printed `cost` is 0, so *any* Corp could turn one faceup for nothing. The punishment half is an identity `OnAccessed` trigger, which needed the Corp's identity added to `GameEvent::CardAccessed`'s audience (the same "also fire the owning identity" widening `AgendaScored` and `CardTrashedFromAccess` already carry) and one requirement, `CurrentlyAccessingInstalledCard { rezzed_only }`, read off `AccessState::pending_install`; "an agenda" composes from the existing `Not(CurrentlyAccessingNonAgenda)`.
  - **Threat level** (Measured Response) as `Amount::ThreatLevel` — the greater of the two scores in agenda points — read through the existing `EffectRequirement::AmountAtLeast`, so the derived-quantity vocabulary grew and the requirement vocabulary did not. *N-Pot* and *Public Access Plaza* print the same clause at Stage 10.
  - **Resolving another ice's subroutine** (Mycoweb): `Effect::ResolveSubroutineOfSelectedIce` turns the chosen ice's own subroutines into a `PresentChoice` and resolves the pick *as that ice*, so a sentry's "trash 1 installed program" targets through the sentry. Composition could not reach it — `PresentChoice` needs its options in the JSON, and these are whatever the other card prints. "**Another** rezzed code gate" needed a second fix: a firing subroutine now resolves with `ResolutionContext::for_install`, carrying the encountered ice's install, so `CardFilter::NotSourceCard` can exclude Mycoweb itself. It resolved as a bare `CardId` before, which also meant every "this card" lookup inside a subroutine found the first install sharing the name — the per-install exactness the Rules Audit gave activated abilities, arriving late for subroutines. `CardFilter::IceOfType` is the sentry/code-gate half, which `CardType(Ice(_))` explicitly cannot express (its payload is a documented don't-care).
  - **The Runner's grip and stack as targets** (Touch-ups): `CardZoneRef::OpponentHand` and `OpponentDeck`, so a Corp selection can read the grip and shuffle what it took into the stack — the destination and `shuffle_after` machinery already handled both once `owning_side` knew them. The named card type is a four-option `PresentChoice`, one per Runner card type. It is also the Corp's first Double: `additional_play_cost` was paid only by `play_event`, so `engine::play_operation_card` now pays it (with the credits, before `OnPlay`) and `can_play_operation_from_hq` checks it, keeping the offer and the resolution in agreement.
  - **A click banked for next turn** (Aggressive Trendsetting): `Effect::GainClicksNextTurn` adds to `CorpState::extra_clicks_next_turn`, which `turn::enter_start_of_turn` spends into the allotment — `GainClicks` would have been written over, since the assignment *sets* the allotment and this resolves during the Runner's turn. On `GameState` rather than the resolution context by AGENTS.md's State Hygiene test: it has to outlive the resolution by a whole turn. The trigger itself fires from the score area, which `GameEvent::CardTrashedFromAccess` now reaches (pinned per scored copy, exactly as `DiscardPhaseEnded` does for Off the Books), and "an **installed** Corp card" is the same `CurrentlyAccessingInstalledCard` BANGUN reads, checked while the access that trashed it is still pending.
  - **The top of R&D** (Poétrï): `CardFilter::TopOfZone` narrows a selection to a zone's last N cards (the top of R&D and of the stack is the end of the `Vec` — both draw by popping). A filter rather than a zone, so it composes with the type filter through `All` and leaves the selection's zone the whole of R&D — which is what lets `PromptInstallCorpCard` find the card again by position, now that it accepts `OwnRAndD` beside HQ and Archives.
  - **Pure composition:** Top-Down Solutions (draw, then two one-at-a-time install prompts — Humanoid Resources' shape), Anthill Excavation Contract (Otto Campaign's shape at 8 credits and 4 a turn, drawing as it pays), and Byte!, which is Snare!'s `interactive_on_access` exactly. Byte! is the first such card *in a sample deck*, which took `PayAccessTrigger`/`DeclineAccessTrigger` off `ACTIONS_UNREACHABLE_WITH_SAMPLE_DECKS` — see the coverage-gate note below.
  - **The two sweeps stopped agreeing, and now say so separately.** The index-path sweep plays a fixed pair of System Gateway fixture decks (its card universe says as much) while the view-path sweep plays the sample pool, and they share the action allowlist: with Byte! in the pool, whichever list satisfied one sweep failed the other. `Coverage::gate_failures_excluding` lets a sweep name the actions *its own decks* cannot produce, and the index sweep declares the pay/decline pair there rather than in the shared list — no System Gateway card carries an interactive-on-access trigger. `PayAccessTrigger` stays in `ACTIONS_RARE_WITH_SAMPLE_DECKS` (768 games reach it; 96 sometimes do not).
  - **Known fidelity limits:** BANGUN installs faceup by rezzing later, which is strictly more information for the Corp than the printed timing; Byte!'s "while the Runner is accessing this asset in R&D they must reveal it" is a no-op here, since an accessed card is shown to the Runner anyway; the forfeit picks the cheapest agenda for the Corp; a Corp bot naming a card type for Touch-ups is choosing blind, because the masked view hides the grip it is about to reveal (the selection is by position, so the engine is right and only the *choice* is uninformed); Mycoweb resolving Ansel 1.0's or Brân 1.0's install subroutine finds no host — those pin themselves to the ice being encountered, which is Mycoweb — and no-ops; two rezzed Mycowebs can pass subroutine 4 back and forth, each hop a Corp decision.
  - **DSL ratio re-measured** over 157 card files: **31 of 71 `Effect` variants single-use, 6 unused** — flat against Stage 6's 30 of 68 while the pool grew by ten cards, and the best stage of the program by that measure. Three variants for ten cards (`ResolveSubroutineOfSelectedIce`, `GainClicksNextTurn`, `ForfeitAgendas`), plus one that *replaced* another (`RezInstalled` for `RezInstalledIgnoringCost`, now with two users). The rest is fields and small vocabularies: two `CardZoneRef`s, two `CardFilter`s, one `EffectRequirement`, one `Amount`, two `CardDefinition` fields and one `CorpState` field. `ForfeitAgendas` counts as unused above because it is authored in `engine::rez_ice` rather than in card JSON, the same way `ResolveSomeOf` is a composition primitive rather than a card's text.
  - **Tests:** ten per-card tests (`poetri_luxury_brands_installs_from_the_top_three_of_rd_on_a_score_and_from_hq_on_a_steal` … `measured_response_needs_threat_four_and_a_recent_run_then_taxes_eight_or_deals_four_meat`; the Mycoweb test walks all four subroutines in one encounter and watches subroutine 4 pick up the code gate subroutine 2 had just rezzed), the pinned table (Fashion Lab and Pork Chops, 49 cards / 15 influence / 20 points each — both transcribed totals agree with the published lists) and the set gate at 21.
  - **Measured** (96 games, seed 1, `target/coverage/elev-7-*.json`; two of nine Corp slots are these decks, so each sees 10 or 11 random games): random-vs-random installs Anthill Excavation Contract 43 times, rezzes 26 and pays out 40; Aggressive Trendsetting is installed 26 times and stolen 7; Byte! is rezzed 8 times and its access decision is taken both ways (1 paid, 1 declined); Mycoweb is rezzed twice for 8 subroutines and Biawak once for 8 (1 broken) — a 14-credit rez, forfeit or not, is rare in random play; Top-Down Solutions is played 3 times and Touch-ups twice; Poétrï's steal half fires 33 times. **BANGUN's access punishment fires 17 times in random play and 38 in heuristic play** — both bots do rez agendas once the identity allows it, so the mechanic is live on both paths. Heuristic-vs-heuristic scores Aggressive Trendsetting 3 times and fires Poétrï's *score* half 14 times (0 in random play — random Corps seldom score), and takes the Byte! decision 9 times (4 paid). **Measured Response was never played in either seating** (accessed 3 and 22 times — it sits in HQ): threat 4 plus a successful run last turn plus 5 credits is a narrow window in games that end at 7 points, recorded in the still-open list. End reasons random: 44 flatline / 43 Runner agenda / 9 deckout; heuristic-vs-heuristic: 26 Corp agenda / 9 flatline / 61 Runner agenda (Stage 6: 27 / 6 / 63). Bench (`heuristic,puct@32`, 12 games per pairing, seed 1): heuristic 1533 ± 177 Corp / 1582 ± 195 Runner, puct@32 1450 ± 180 / 1196 ± 166, no stalls.

- [x] **Stage 8 — Quick Returns (The Zwicky Group: Invisible Hands) and Glyph of Warding (AU Co.: The Gold Standard in Clones) — DONE** (`feat/elevation-stage-8-quick-returns-glyph-of-warding`, 3 September 2026). Nine cards: *AU Co.: The Gold Standard in Clones*, *Sericulture Expansion*, *Phật Gioan Baotixita*, *Empiricist*, *Peer Review*, *The Zwicky Group: Invisible Hands*, *Greenmail*, *Plutus*, *Lamplighter*. The pool is 11 Corp × 12 Runner = 132 matchups; `ELEV_UNIMPLEMENTED` holds 12.
  - **Counters on an identity** (AU Co.): `CorpState::identity_counters` is the first place counters sit that is not an install, a rig card or a scored agenda. `ability::modify_counters` and `counters_of` fall through to it when the resolving card *is* the Corp's identity, so `AddCounters`, `RemoveCounters`, `Cost::RemoveCounters` and `ThisCardCountersAtLeast` all reach it with no change on the card side, and the masking layer carries it as public — an identity is faceup, so its tokens are too.
  - **Three new things to react to, none of them a new `Effect`:** `Trigger::OnDamageDealt` (fired where the damage lands, since `GameEvent::DamageTaken` is returned by `damage::apply_damage` and never went through the dispatcher — only the prevention window did); `Trigger::OnCardsTrashedFromHq` with `GameEvent::CardsTrashedFromHq`, emitted once per selection batch because the printed text says "1 **or more** cards"; and `Trigger::OnAbilityGainedCredits` with `GameEvent::AbilityGainedCredits`, which every credit-gaining effect now emits alongside `CreditsGained` when a card is resolving. The Zwicky Group narrows that to "an agenda or operation" through the existing `TriggeringCardMatches`, so no requirement was needed. A field on `CreditsGained` would have been threaded through twenty-six construction sites, most of which have no card to name.
  - **Forfeit, generalised into a way of paying to rez** (Plutus, and Biawak re-authored): `CardDefinition::rez_alternatives` replaces Stage 7's `rez_forfeit_discount`. Each alternative is a `requirement`, an effect to `pay`, and a `discount`; `engine::rez_ice` keeps the ones whose requirement holds **and whose resulting price the Corp can actually meet**, resolves a single survivor directly and offers a `PresentChoice` when there are two. Biawak lists a no-op alternative beside its forfeit and Plutus does not, which is exactly the difference between an optional discount and an additional cost. `Trigger::OnForfeit` and `GameEvent::AgendaForfeited` give Greenmail its "when you forfeit this agenda, gain 4[c]" — and leave the "forfeit the cheapest" rule right, since Greenmail is both the cheapest and the one that pays.
  - **A loop the sweep found, and the pricing that closed it.** `Effect::RezInstalled` is deliberately lenient about affordability (Stage 7), so offering an alternative the Corp cannot finish paying is not a harmless dead end: the rez quietly does not happen while the action still counts as applied, and a random Corp re-rezzes the same ice forever. The view-path sweep hit it at seed 19 (Pork Chops, Biawak with nothing to forfeit) as `BudgetExhausted` after 10,000 actions. `engine::rez_price` is the split-out costing both `rez_install` and the alternative filter use, so an unaffordable rez is refused by the action and never offered by `legal_actions`. It also stops the Corp forfeiting an agenda for a rez it then cannot pay the rest of.
  - **A second loop, in the card text.** Biawak's "trash 1 installed program **or** end the run" was authored as two `EffectIf`s in a `Sequence`, and the `Sequence` continuation re-checked the condition *after* the trash — so trashing the only program also ended the run, and the next subroutine's `EndTheRun` then failed with `NoActiveRun`, failing the whole confirmation and stranding the selection that had parked. Two fixes: the clauses are ordered "end the run if there is nothing to trash" **first**, so the branch is decided before the board changes; and `Effect::EndTheRun` on an already-ended run is now a no-op rather than an error, because a card's own text can legitimately reach it twice and an `Err` there fails the action that got here.
  - **"Once per turn" now means once per turn.** `turn::enter_start_of_turn` clears *both* sides' `once_per_turn_used`, not just the starting side's. A Corp gate used on the Corp's own turn used to span the following Runner turn as one window; Phật Gioan Baotixita's "the first time each turn an agenda is scored or stolen" reads from both sides of that boundary and made the old behaviour visible.
  - **Everyone hears an agenda change hands.** `AgendaScored` and `AgendaStolen` now reach every rezzed Corp install (they reached the scored server's root, and nothing at all, respectively), because Phật Gioan Baotixita reacts from wherever it sits. The narrowing moved onto the cards that want it as `EffectRequirement::AgendaCameFromThisCardsServer` — Lamplighter's "from this server or its root", and *Malapert Data Vault*, which had been relying on the audience to say what its text says.
  - **Smaller pieces:** `EffectRequirement::ScoreAreaHasAtLeast` gates every forfeit alternative; `PromptInstallCorpCard::remote_only` is Peer Review's "in the root of a remote server", which is what keeps an upgrade out of a central's root; `Effect::PlayOperationFromHq` became `PlayOperation { from }` so Plutus can replay a transaction out of Archives (and have it removed from the game, which the Archives path already did for Petty Cash); the Corp's discard-phase-end audience gained the rezzed table, which it had been leaving out under a comment saying no card needed it yet. Sericulture Expansion, Empiricist and Peer Review's payout are pure composition.
  - **Known fidelity limits:** Sericulture Expansion's "you cannot score that card this turn" is vacuous where it resolves (the Corp's turn is already over); Peer Review's "reveal all but 1 card in HQ" is not modelled, like every other reveal; AU Co.'s dig trashes a card the Runner never sees, so it lands facedown in Archives; a Corp bot choosing which of Plutus's two costs to pay is choosing blind between two hidden zones.
  - **DSL ratio re-measured** over 166 card files: **30 of 71 `Effect` variants single-use, 5 unused** — the count of variants is *unchanged* from Stage 7 and the single-use count fell by one, over nine more cards. Stage 8 added **no new `Effect` variant at all**: `PlayOperation { from }` replaced `PlayOperationFromHq`, and `ForfeitAgendas` picked up its second author (Plutus) and left the unused list. The growth went into the vocabulary of *when* and *whether* instead — four `Trigger`s, two `EffectRequirement`s, three `GameEvent`s, one `CardDefinition` field replaced by the `RezAlternative` struct, and one `CorpState` field.
  - **Tests:** nine per-card tests (`au_co_counts_damage_and_hq_trashes_then_spends_two_counters_to_dig_three_deep` … `lamplighter_taxes_a_tag_or_three_credits_and_burns_out_when_its_server_gives_up_an_agenda`; the Plutus test walks both rez costs and the refusal when neither is payable, and the Biawak test now pins the priced-alternatives behaviour), the pinned table (Quick Returns 48 cards / 15 influence / 20 points; Glyph of Warding 49 / 15 / 20 — both totals agree with the published lists) and the set gate at 12.
  - **Measured** (132 games, seed 1, `target/coverage/elev-8-*.json`). **The report length changed with the pool**: `--all-matchups` walks `matchups[index % len]`, and at 11 Corp × 12 Runner a 96-game report stops eight Corp decks in — Quick Returns is the eleventh and never played. 132 games is one pass of the cross product, so this stage's numbers are one game per matchup rather than the old 96. Random-vs-random: Plutus is installed 18 times and **rezzed 15** (its additional cost is payable far more often than Biawak's), and its turn-start replay is offered 42 times; Phật Gioan Baotixita is rezzed 13 times and loads 37 counters at discard-phase end; AU Co. counts 22 damage instances and 10 HQ-trash batches, and offers its dig 22 times; The Zwicky Group draws off a credit gain 42 times out of 1,685 `AbilityGainedCredits`; Greenmail is scored 4 times and **forfeited once**, the first `AgendaForfeited` in agent play; Peer Review is played 6 times; Lamplighter is rezzed 7 times for 20 subroutines; Empiricist is rezzed twice for 9. Heuristic-vs-heuristic reaches the two agenda-driven triggers the random Corp mostly cannot: Phật Gioan fires on 12 scores and 11 steals, Lamplighter burns out once each way, Greenmail is forfeited 4 times, and AU Co. offers its dig 75 times. **Sericulture Expansion never paid a dividend** in either seating (scored 9 times by the heuristic), the Off the Books blindness a third time. End reasons random: 59 flatline / 59 Runner agenda / 14 deckout; heuristic-vs-heuristic: 42 Corp agenda / 8 flatline / 82 Runner agenda. Bench (`heuristic,puct@32`, 12 games per pairing, seed 1): heuristic 1533 ± 181 Corp / 1617 ± 211 Runner, puct@32 1339 ± 181 / 1238 ± 164, no stalls.

- [x] **Stage 9 — Hidden Funds (Jinteki: Restoring Humanity) and Peculiarity (PT Untaian: Life's Building Blocks) — DONE** (`feat/elevation-stage-9-hidden-funds-peculiarity`, 3 September 2026). Four cards: *PT Untaian: Life's Building Blocks*, *Proprionegation*, *Mitra Aman*, *Doomscroll*. The pool is 13 Corp × 12 Runner = 156 matchups; `ELEV_UNIMPLEMENTED` holds 8. Hidden Funds is the first Corp deck to run an *already-implemented* identity — Jinteki: Restoring Humanity, from System Gateway — so the stage is four cards for two decks.
  - **Abilities used from the score area** (Proprionegation's "hosted agenda counter: the Runner moves to the outermost position of Archives"): `PlayerAction::ActivateAbility` now resolves its target in `corp.scored_agendas` as well as on the table, and a scored agenda is always active — it is faceup in front of its owner with no rez state to check. `legal_actions` offers those abilities, `action_owner` places them (the one Corp card an installed-only lookup could not), and the `ActionSpace` addresses them from one shared list — installs, then the score area, identity still last — so encode and decode cannot disagree and the segment did not grow. `Cost::RemoveCounters` already spent agenda counters from there for Off the Books; this is the first card to *activate* from there.
  - **Moving a run backwards** (`Effect::MoveRunToOutermost`): the run's ice list is rebuilt for the new server with **nothing passed**, which is the whole difference from `RedirectRunOnApproach` — Maintenance Access arrives at a server having walked past everything, Proprionegation puts the Runner back at the front door. With no ice there the Runner lands on the server approach instead, and `ServerApproached` is emitted and dispatched so that server's own root reactions still fire. Gated by `EffectRequirement::DuringRun`, the wider sibling of `DuringEncounter`: any point from initiation to the server approach, which is where a card that moves a run can still do something.
  - **Swapping the ice being approached with one in hand** (`Effect::SwapApproachedIceWithCard`, Mitra Aman): the approached install keeps its position and handle and takes the new card, unrezzed as it arrives from a hidden zone, while the displaced ice goes back to the zone the new one came from — faceup in Archives if the Runner had seen it rezzed. `SwapInstalledIce` exchanges two cards already on the table and cannot reach a zone; `InstallFromZoneIgnoringCost` adds a card *beside* the approached ice with no way to send the displaced one back. "From Archives **or** HQ" is a `PresentChoice` between two prompts, since a selection reads one zone.
    - **And a stale-run bug it exposed:** `run::reconcile_ice` matched the run's ice to the table by `install_id` alone and kept its own copy of everything else, on the assumption that an install's *card* never changes. Mitra Aman is the first card that changes one, so the run went on approaching the ice that had just left the table. The match now requires the card to agree too.
  - **A trigger one step earlier than the server approach** (`Trigger::OnIceApproached`, fired off `GameEvent::IceApproached` for the approached server's rezzed root installs — the same audience `OnApproachServer` uses one step later). The event existed and was emitted at three places but had never been dispatched.
  - **Smaller pieces:** `CardFilter::Unrezzed`, the twin of `Rezzed`, for PT Untaian's "an unrezzed card you can advance" — which is an agenda or an asset as often as it is ice, so `UnrezzedIce` could not serve; `Amount::RunnerTags` for Doomscroll's "at least 2 tags", read through `AmountAtLeast` (`IsTagged` answers only "at least one"); and the `AgendaScored` dispatch now names the scored agenda by the install handle it kept in the score area, without which a "place a counter on this agenda" reaction had nothing to place onto.
  - **Known fidelity limits:** PT Untaian's "you cannot score that card this turn" is vacuous where it resolves, as Sericulture Expansion's is; Mitra Aman's swap keeps the install's advancement and counters off the new card by clearing them, which is right for a swap into an empty position but would lose an advanced piece of ice's tokens if a future card swapped one out; Proprionegation moves the Runner to Archives only, as printed, so `MoveRunToOutermost` has one authored target.
  - **DSL ratio re-measured** over 170 card files: **32 of 73 `Effect` variants single-use, 5 unused**. Two variants for four cards is the stage's own ratio, and both are mechanics the plan named at Stage 1 as this stage's reason to exist; the rest of the stage is a filter, an `Amount`, a `Trigger`, an `EffectRequirement` and two audience fixes.
  - **Tests:** four per-card tests (`pt_untaian_pays_one_to_advance_an_unrezzed_card_when_hq_is_down_to_three`, `proprionegation_scores_with_a_counter_that_sends_the_runner_back_to_archives`, `mitra_aman_trashes_itself_on_an_approach_to_pay_three_and_swap_the_ice_being_approached`, `doomscroll_tags_damages_and_damages_again_only_at_two_tags`) and the pinned table (Hidden Funds 44 cards / 15 influence / 18 points — the first 44-card Corp deck since System Gateway, on a 40-card minimum identity; Peculiarity 49 / 15 / 20).
  - **Measured** (156 games, seed 1, `target/coverage/elev-9-*.json` — one pass of the 13 × 12 cross product, since a report shorter than the pool leaves its tail unplayed). Random-vs-random: Proprionegation is installed 23 times, scored twice and **its score-area ability activated twice**, which is the first time an ability has been used from there in agent play; Mitra Aman is rezzed 10 times and reacts to 9 ice approaches; Doomscroll is rezzed 8 times for 47 subroutines; PT Untaian offers its advancement 68 times. Heuristic-vs-heuristic scores Proprionegation 18 times and **activates it 15**, reacts on 43 approaches with Mitra Aman, and fires 57 Doomscroll subroutines; PT Untaian offers 88 times. `RunRedirected` counts 10 in random play and 21 in heuristic play, where before this stage every one of them was Maintenance Access. End reasons random: 73 flatline / 67 Runner agenda / 16 deckout; heuristic-vs-heuristic: 48 Corp agenda / 17 flatline / 91 Runner agenda (Stage 8, at 132 games: 42 / 8 / 82). Bench (`heuristic,puct@32`, 12 games per pairing, seed 1): heuristic 1521 ± 178 Corp / 1645 ± 210 Runner, puct@32 1350 ± 184 / 1238 ± 164, no stalls.

- [x] **Stage 10 — Fine Print (NBN: Reality Plus), Gimbatul (Synapse Global) and Not so subtle (Nebula Talent Management) — DONE** (`feat/elevation-stage-10-fine-print-gimbatul-not-so-subtle`, 3 September 2026). Eight cards: *Nebula Talent Management: Making Stars*, *Synapse Global: Faster than Thought*, *Embedded Reporting*, *Next Big Thing*, *Public Access Plaza*, *N-Pot*, *Bigger Picture*, *IP Enforcement*. **`ELEV_UNIMPLEMENTED` is empty: every one of Elevation's 82 cards is implemented, and all 28 published sample decklists are in the training pool — 16 Corp × 12 Runner = 192 matchups, the number this program set out to reach.**
  - **A Corp identity with two sides** (Nebula Talent Management): the flip flag was the Runner's alone (Dewi Subrotoputri), so `CorpState::identity_flipped` is its twin and both `Effect::FlipIdentity` and `EffectRequirement::IdentityFlipped` became side-aware — the effect flips whichever identity is resolving, the requirement reads the asking side's flag. One effect and one requirement still serve both sides. The flip *back* needed no new trigger at all: the Corp identity joined `RunSucceeded`'s audience, which the arm's own comment had been waiting for ("both Runner-side today … until a Corp-side reactor exists"), and the per-server variants already tell HQ and R&D from Archives. `Trigger::OnOperationPlayed` is the front side's, fired for every operation rather than only the transactions `OnTransactionPlayed` already covered, and `EffectRequirement::PlayedOperationThisTurn` reads a flag rather than consuming a once-per-turn tag — the question is asked at the end of the action phase and must not spend anything.
  - **An ability the *other* side uses** (`AbilityDef::used_by`, N-Pot's "3[c]: break 1 subroutine on this ice. Only the Runner can use this ability."): the Corp's card, the Runner's decision, the Runner's credits. `engine::activate_ability` resolves it before the priority and phase checks so those apply to the side that will actually spend, and `legal_actions::action_owner` reads it too, so the offer reaches the seat that can pay. That lookup is why `action_owner` now takes the registry.
  - **Buying a stolen agenda back** (`Effect::InstallAgendaFromRunnerScoreArea`, IP Enforcement): the agenda leaves the Runner's score area, their score drops, tags equal to its printed points come off, and it installs unrezzed in a fresh remote. One effect rather than a cost plus an install because X *is* the chosen agenda's point value — the cost cannot be known until the card is picked, and `ability::pay_cost` never sees the selection. `CardZoneRef::OpponentScoreArea` is the source and `CardFilter::AgendaPointsAtMostRunnerTags` keeps the offer to agendas the tags can actually pay for.
  - **Reacting to a tag coming off** (`Trigger::OnTagRemoved`, Synapse Global): fired for the Corp's identity by every route a tag leaves — the basic action, a cost, a card effect — which meant dispatching `TagsRemoved`/`TagRemoved` where they are produced, exactly as `DamageTaken` needed at Stage 8. `Effect::RemoveTags` now reports what actually came off rather than what was asked for, so a request that removed nothing fires nothing; it also picked up its first two authors and left the unused list. Synapse's own remove-a-tag ability triggers its own install, which is the card working as printed.
  - **"While it is rezzed", asked after the card has gone** (Public Access Plaza): `AccessState::pending_install_rezzed` records the rez state when the card is *presented*, because the one card that asks — "when the Runner trashes this asset (while it is rezzed)" — is dispatched from `OnTrashedFromAccess`, by which point the install has left the table. `CurrentlyAccessingInstalledCard { rezzed_only }` reads the record instead of the table, which is also what BANGUN was doing at the earlier moment.
  - **Pure composition, four ways:** Embedded Reporting is Off the Books' shape with `dividends: 2` (the multiplier was already there); Next Big Thing pairs `Cost::AllOf` with the score-area activation Stage 9 built; Bigger Picture drains 5 credits a tag as five `LoseCreditsAmount`/`GainCreditsAmount` pairs over `Amount::RunnerTags` and `CreditsLostThisResolution`, which is exact even when the Runner runs out; N-Pot's threat-gated subroutines are `EffectIf` over `Amount::ThreatLevel`.
  - **Known fidelity limits:** Next Big Thing's "the Corp can use this ability even if this agenda is in the Runner's score area" is not modelled — the Runner's score area holds bare `CardId`s with no counters or handles, so the ability is available only from the Corp's own; Bigger Picture removes *all* tags rather than letting the Corp name a number, which is the dominant choice and the only one this DSL can express without a numeric prompt; Embedded Reporting's search does not shuffle R&D, because `shuffle_after` fires after the destination push and would undo the "add it to the top" the card is for (the Corp already sees its own R&D order in this engine); IP Enforcement installs into a fresh remote without asking, since a returning agenda has one sensible home and installing over an occupant would trash it; Next Big Thing's "any number of cards" is capped at ten.
  - **DSL ratio re-measured** over 178 card files: **29 of 74 `Effect` variants single-use, 5 unused** — the single-use count *fell* by three while the pool grew by eight cards, because `RemoveTags` and two others picked up authors. One new `Effect` for eight cards. The rest of the stage is two `Trigger`s, one `EffectRequirement`, one `CardZoneRef`, one `CardFilter`, one `AbilityDef` field and two state fields.
  - **Tests:** eight per-card tests (`nebula_talent_management_flips_on_an_operation_refunds_a_click_and_flips_back_on_a_central_run` … `ip_enforcement_buys_a_stolen_agenda_back_for_its_own_points_in_tags`), and the pinned table completed at 28 decks (Fine Print 44 / 15 / 18; Gimbatul 49 / 15 / 20; Not so subtle 49 / 15 / 20).
  - **Measured** (192 games, seed 1, `target/coverage/elev-10-*.json` — one pass of the finished cross product). **Every card in the report's universe was seen in play: 139 of 139 in random-vs-random**, which is the strongest form of the coverage claim this harness can make. Random play plays IP Enforcement twice and Bigger Picture three times (both need a tagged Runner and a specific board); activates N-Pot's Runner-only break 12 times for 13 subroutines broken; rezzes Public Access Plaza 19 times and tags a trasher once; and flips Nebula Talent Management 43 times, 18 of them off an operation and 18 off a successful central run. Synapse Global's tag-removal trigger fires 30 times against 46 `TagsRemoved` and 25 `TagRemoved`. Heuristic-vs-heuristic scores Embedded Reporting 25 times and Next Big Thing 22, rezzes Public Access Plaza 20 times for 238 turn-start credits, and flips Nebula 75 times — but never plays Bigger Picture or IP Enforcement (accessed 70 and 18 times: they sit in HQ), the same tagged-Runner blindness the still-open list records. End reasons random: 89 flatline / 82 Runner agenda / 21 deckout; heuristic-vs-heuristic: 74 Corp agenda / 12 flatline / 106 Runner agenda (Stage 9, at 156 games: 48 / 17 / 91). Bench (`heuristic,puct@32`, 12 games per pairing, seed 1): heuristic 1454 ± 179 Corp / 1617 ± 207 Runner, puct@32 1383 ± 181 / 1357 ± 164, no stalls — the closest the two have been on any pool, which is what a Corp side with sixteen real decks does to a heuristic tuned on four.


---

## 🔍 Rules Audit — August 2026

Phase 1 §3's memory-cost entry ("no program could be installed through `legal_actions` at all") prompted an audit for more bugs of that shape: a rule silently non-functional while every test stays green. Three read-only passes — the candidate-generation/handler seam, rules fidelity against Null Signal Games' rules, and what the bots and self-play actually measure — found **twelve**, each re-verified by reading the code path. This section is their single source of status. The order below is the fix order; each entry records its branch and the before/after numbers from the coverage harness when it lands.

**How they stayed hidden, in one paragraph.** `legal_actions` keeps only candidates `apply_action` accepts, which makes false positives impossible and false *negatives* invisible: a candidate never generated is never probed, and an under-strict handler is laundered into "legal, with the engine's authority". Every sweep asserted only reachability — `GameOver`, a non-empty action list. Self-play recorded tensors and never a `PlayerAction`, `GameEvent` or `CardId`. And `HeuristicAgent`'s evaluator has no run, draw or advancement term, so heuristic-vs-random play — both sweeps' only seatings — **never produced a single `IceEncountered` event**: the encounter machinery had never once run under test.

**Status: all twelve Tier-1 findings fixed, in ten branches, each measured before and after through the harness (§0) and merged only with both deep sweeps clean; Tier 2 (§2) is closed the same way in seven more branches (September 2026), with its already-correct items re-described rather than "fixed", plus one loose-ends branch; the adjacent-hazards pass (§4) closed six more, two of them game-deciding in ordinary play.** Every game recorded before this section, and every exported policy, is stale twice over — the rules changed under them, and `ActionSpace::SIZE` went 1357 → 1646.

### 0. The rules-coverage harness — DONE

- [x] **`netrunner_session::Coverage`** aggregates a `MatchHistory` — which `Session::apply` already records — into counts per `PlayerAction` variant (and per side), per `GameEvent` variant, per card (installed / rezzed / played / activated / accessed / stolen / scored / trashed / subroutines fired / broken / click-broken), per server run outcome, `Effect` variants seen (exact for subroutines, inferred through `Effect::for_each_effect` for activated abilities) and triggers that fired (`triggers_fired`, read off `GameEvent::TriggerFired` — originally an inference, `triggers_eligible`, from the event that would have offered the trigger; replaced by the exact event in Tier 2, see §2). All `BTreeMap<String, _>`, so `to_json()` is sorted and two reports `diff`. Variant names come off the `Debug` rendering, so a new variant is counted the day it is added — the same trick `no_client_view_or_log_entry_ever_names_a_card_it_conceals` uses.
  - Homed in `netrunner_session`, not `netrunner_core` (rules, not reports) or `netrunner_bots` (players): the session owns the match record and this aggregates it. `netrunner_core` gained only `Effect::for_each_effect`/`variant_name` and `PlayerAction::VARIANT_NAMES`/`variant_name` — the walker because both this and the coming `ActionSpace` cap gate need it, the const kept honest by `variant_names_match_the_enum`.
- [x] **`netrunner_cli --headless` is the driver**, rewritten: it plays the real sample decks (`--all-matchups` rotates all twelve like the sweeps; `--corp-deck`/`--runner-deck` are honoured now, where before it hardcoded a Kate-vs-HB filler matchup and ignored them), seats `random`/`heuristic`/`mcts`/`puct` (new `BotKind::Puct` over the uniform policy — the shape self-play trains with), `--index-path` drives the `ActionSpace` round trip instead of `Seat::Agent`, `--report <path>` writes the JSON, `--verbose` prints one line per game. A stalled game is *counted* under `end_reasons`, not an error: the sweeps assert, the driver reports. The old mode's one line of output — "N games completed without panics" — is exactly the kind of check under which nothing was ever found.
- [x] **Mechanical gates in both sweeps** (`Coverage::gate_failures`): every `PlayerAction` variant applied at least once, every sample-deck card seen in play, every `LOAD_BEARING_EVENTS` entry emitted. Exclusions are claims with reasons, on the `SG_UNIMPLEMENTED` model: `ACTIONS_UNREACHABLE_WITH_SAMPLE_DECKS` (the two trace bids — no card in the pool has a `Trace`; the two access-trigger actions — Snare! is in no sample deck) and `ACTIONS_RARE_WITH_SAMPLE_DECKS`, which names a game count below which a zero is tolerated (`TrashResource` 512, `BreakSubroutineWithClick` 256, `RemoveTag` 128 — measured at 4, 10 and 27 per 192 random games). The default 32-seed run therefore never false-alarms on rarity, and the 256-seed run AGENTS.md already mandates demands everything. `every_player_action_variant_is_applied` is the assertion that would have failed while `InstallProgram` was unreachable.
  - **Both sweeps gained a random-vs-random seating**, because it is the only one that reaches runs. It tolerates `StallReason::BudgetExhausted` (measured: 1 game in 192 outlasts `MAX_STEPS` under two random players — slow play, not a deadlock) and never `NoLegalActions`, which is the invariant the sweeps pin. `every_sample_deck_matchup_finishes` went from three seeds per matchup to four, with the last two random-vs-random: a 2-of in a 40-card deck (Docklands Pass) went unseen with one.
- [x] **What it found on its first run, before any rules fix:**
  - **A deadlock, fixed in the same branch.** Index-path sweep, random-vs-random, seed 85 on the mechanic-coverage decks: *Anoetic Void* and *Manegarm Skunkworks* protecting one remote. Anoetic's `OnApproachServer` parked a card selection and, once confirmed, ended the run; the queued Skunkworks trigger from the same `RunSucceeded` dispatch then fired against **no run** and parked a paid choice the Runner could neither afford (`AnyOf([Clicks(2), Credits(5)])` with 1 click and 2 credits) nor decline (`if_declined: EndTheRun` → `NoActiveRun`). No legal action, deterministically. `dispatcher::still_applies` now drops a planned or deferred trigger whose event was run-scoped (`RunSucceeded`/`IceEncountered`/`RunInitiated`) once `active_run` is gone — in `fire_one`, the single function both `fire_plan` and `drain_deferred_triggers` go through, since the immediate path has the same exposure when the run-ending card does not park. `OnRunEnded` is deliberately outside the guard: it fires *from* the run-ended events. Pinned by `the_anoetic_void_then_skunkworks_position_plays_out`; two dispatcher fixtures that dispatched `RunSucceeded` with no run at all — a state the engine cannot produce — now carry one.
  - **Baseline numbers, 96 games per seating, seed 1, all matchups** (`target/coverage/main-*.json`; regenerate with `cargo run --release -p netrunner_cli -- --headless --all-matchups --games 96 --corp <k> --runner <k> --seed 1 --report <path>`):
    | seating | `IceEncountered` | `SubroutineFired` | `AgendaStolen` | `AgendaScored` | outcome split |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | random / random | 427 | 348 | 215 | 21 | 37 Corp flatline, 48 Runner points, 11 Runner deckout |
    | heuristic Corp / random Runner | **0** | **0** | 285 | 0 | 73 Runner points, 13 deckout, 10 flatline |
    | random Corp / heuristic Runner | **0** | **0** | 0 | 80 | **88 of 96 end by Corp deck-out** — the heuristic Runner never runs, so the game is decided by the Corp emptying R&D |
    Those zeros are the two seatings the sweeps had. Against them, the audit's findings below were unobservable by construction.

### 0.5 The heuristic can now play — DONE (`feat/heuristic-values-advancement-and-breakers`)

Not a rules fix, and a prerequisite for the rest: with the sweeps' heuristic seatings unable to reach an encounter, deleting the free break (T1) would have turned every heuristic game into a Runner that installs nothing and a Corp that never rezzes. `bots::eval::evaluate_state` now takes the `CardRegistry` and scores what only a card's text can say:

- **Corp:** an unrezzed install is worth 0.6 (installing beats clicking for a credit), a rezzed ICE 2.0 (rezzing Palisade at approach beats passing; a 6-cost bioroid still waits for credits), and each advancement token 1.5 *up to the card's requirement* (advance, then score — never pile on).
- **Runner:** 2.0 per ICE subtype the rig can break (via `Effect::for_each_effect` over the rig's abilities — a first Barrier breaker beats a credit, a second adds nothing), +0.6 while a run is in progress (starting one beats a credit; jacking out forfeits it), −1.0 per unbroken subroutine on the encountered ICE (breaking for up to two credits is worth it; three unbroken subs with no breaker means jack out), and −0.5 while a decision of one's own is parked.
- **The last term fixed a livelock the change exposed:** *Tāo Salonga*'s two-Barrier swap parks a `min: 2, max: 2` selection; toggles and confirm all scored 0, so the tie-break jitter random-walked `ToggleCardSelection` to `MAX_STEPS` (view sweep, seed 82). Pricing the parked state makes confirming progress the moment it is legal.
- **Measured, 96 games per seating, seed 1.** Heuristic Corp vs random Runner: `IceEncountered` 0 → 723, `AgendaScored` 0 → 257, outcome 73 Runner wins → 55 Corp point wins + 37 flatlines. Random Corp vs heuristic Runner: `RunInitiated` 0 → 1858, `AgendaStolen` 0 → 265, 88 games ending by Corp deck-out → 0. Heuristic vs heuristic — previously 93 Corp wins with no run ever made — is now 20 Corp point wins, 36 flatlines, 40 Runner point wins over 45,203 steps, with 32 confirmations to 31 toggles. Phase 2 §5's first item is closed by this.
- **And the conservation invariant found an engine bug the moment the heuristic Runner installed programs:** `pending_choice::remove_installed_card` pushed a selected install into its owner's discard pile *and* the destination branch pushed it again, so every card trashed through a selection — Ansel 1.0, Ballista, Retribution, Above the Law — arrived in the Heap or Archives twice (`marjanah` ×3 against a deck of 2 at seed 11). Removal now returns the card and the destination push happens once; it is keyed by `InstallId`, closing the Tier-2 first-match aliasing noted below. `trashing_an_installed_card_through_a_selection_moves_it_exactly_once_by_install_id` pins it.

### 1. Tier 1 — a core rule is non-functional in ordinary play

Verified by reading the code path; line numbers as of the audit.

- [x] **T1 — Any subroutine can be broken for free, with no icebreaker — DONE** (`fix/no-free-subroutine-break`). `engine::break_subroutine` → `run::transition_subroutine` checked phase, index and `Pending` and nothing else: no rig lookup, no strength, no subtype, no cost, and `legal_actions` offered it for every pending subroutine at every encounter. ICE was a no-op card class and an icebreaker was worthless — the real reason behind Phase 2 §5's "the evaluator cannot value an icebreaker". `legal_actions.rs`'s own test asserted a strength-2 Corroder may break strength-3 Wall of Static.
  - **Deleted, not gated.** `PlayerAction::BreakSubroutine`, its handler, its candidate generator, its `ActionSpace` arms and its TUI label are gone, along with `Effect::BreakSubroutine(usize)` — a DSL variant used by zero cards. A subroutine is now broken in exactly two ways: `Effect::BreakSubroutines` through an icebreaker's paid ability (subtype- and strength-checked), or `BreakSubroutineWithClick` on a bioroid. `RunAction::BreakSubroutine` stays: it is the internal transition both share. The six engine tests that pinned the free break are ported onto the click-break; `legal_actions.rs`'s test now asserts the pump is offered and no break is.
  - **The `ActionSpace` segment stays as a documented hole**, never legal, so no index shifts — the append-never-shift rule applied to a deletion. Reclaim it in B.10.
  - **That hole exposed the `in_segment` underflow** recorded in the stale-comment list: `then_some(index - start)` subtracted eagerly, so indices in the hole fell through to later, higher-`start` arms and panicked in debug. It is `then(|| index - start)` now, and the "must stay the LAST check" ordering constraint is gone with it.
  - **Measured (96 games, seed 1).** Random-vs-random: `SubroutineBroken` 747 → 130 (only real breaks remain), `SubroutineFired` 670 → 1230, `RunEndedByEffect` 298 → 476, `RunSucceeded` 1817 → 1494, flatlines 45 → 55. Heuristic-vs-heuristic: `SubroutineFired` 115 → 1151 and `RunEndedByEffect` 0 → 535 — ICE stops runs for the first time in this engine's history. The Runner side of that is a bot problem (a one-ply Runner never pumped, because strength had no value), addressed in the same branch: the evaluator now prices the shortfall between the encountered ICE and the best matching breaker, so pumping to break is preferred to walking into the subroutines.
  - **`every_sample_deck_matchup_finishes` lost its card gate.** Forty-eight games is too small a sample for "every card seen" — it flagged Docklands Pass, then Echelon, on consecutive branches purely from trajectories shifting. The sample-pool card gate is the 96-game view sweep and the deep runs.
- [x] **T2 — A stolen agenda never leaves the Corp's zone — DONE** (`fix/run-and-access-bookkeeping`). `run::access::resolve_steal` pushed to `runner.scored_agendas` and removed nothing; `move_to_archives` was reached only by the trash paths, so the same agenda on top of R&D was stolen again on the next run and an installed agenda stayed scorable after being stolen. Both paths now share `remove_from_corp_zone(state, card, server)`, which prefers the zone the access named — the run's remote for an install (two copies in two remotes are told apart by `pending.server`), the *top* copy for R&D, and Archives itself for an agenda stolen there. Pinned per zone by `a_stolen_agenda_leaves_the_corp_zone_it_was_accessed_from`.
  - **The real gate is the card-conservation invariant** now checked at every step of `no_client_view_or_log_entry_ever_names_a_card_it_conceals`: every card a deck started with is in exactly one zone. It fails on the pre-fix engine for two reasons at once — a stolen agenda in two places, and a played Event in none — and would have failed on the day either bug was written.
  - Measured (random-vs-random, 96 games, seed 1): `AgendaStolen` 215 → 203 with the same `GameOver` count, and `RunEndedByEffect` 126 → 169 as the T8 fix below stopped letting pumped strength carry into the next encounter.
- [x] **T3 — The Corp pays a card's rez cost at install *and* at rez, and ICE has no install cost — DONE** (`fix/corp-install-economy`). `CardDefinition::cost` is NetrunnerDB's `cost`, i.e. the rez cost (Palisade 3); `install_card` paid it and `rez_ice` paid it again. Now: agendas, assets and upgrades install free, ICE costs 1[c] per piece of ICE already protecting *that* server (`engine::ice_protecting`), and only `rez_ice` pays the printed cost. `Effect::InstallFromZoneIgnoringCost` was already outside this path. Seven test assertions had pinned the double charge — one in `engine.rs`, one in `legal_actions.rs` (a broke Corp *can* install its first ICE now; the second on the same server is what it cannot afford), five in the HB/PAD/Nico/Regolith card tests — each corrected with the arithmetic spelled out. New: `ice_install_costs_one_credit_per_ice_already_protecting_the_server`, `root_installs_cost_no_credits_regardless_of_printed_cost`.
  - **Measured (random-vs-random, 96 games, seed 1):** `CardInstalled` 1123 → 1678, `IceRezzed` 1033 → 1206, `IceEncountered` 533 → 890, `SubroutineFired` 449 → 670, `AgendaStolen` 201 → 160; Runner point wins 39 → 29. The Corp can afford its board for the first time, and it shows in every run statistic at once.
- [x] **T4 — The Corp has no click-to-draw — DONE** (`feat/action-space-v2`). `draw_card_click` hardcoded `Side::Runner`, `PlayerAction::DrawCardClick` carried no side, and `action.rs:18` said "Runner-only for now". It is `DrawCardClick { side }` now: the Corp draws the top of R&D into HQ for a click, fires `BasicDrawActionTaken { side: Corp }`, and is refused (and not offered) on an empty R&D — a click that would have to draw from nothing is not resolved as a self-inflicted deck-out. Pinned by `corp_can_draw_with_a_click_but_not_from_an_empty_rnd`. Measured: `Corp/DrawCardClick` 0 → 334 in 96 random-vs-random games on both agent shapes; the heuristic never draws by choice (no hand-size term) — a bot matter.
- [x] **T5 — No one-agenda/asset-per-remote rule, no install-over — DONE** (`fix/one-card-per-remote`). `install_card` pushed unconditionally and the candidates offered every existing remote, so a remote could hold three agendas and a breach handed over all of them. Now a Root install of an agenda or asset into a remote that already holds one **trashes the occupant** as part of the install — to Archives, faceup if it was rezzed (the Runner had seen it), facedown otherwise, with a `CardTrashed` event; upgrades stack and do not count as occupants. `fresh_remote_id` offers the smallest *unused* id instead of `max + 1`, so an emptied remote's number comes back and ids no longer ratchet past the `MAX_REMOTE_SERVERS = 10` the `ActionSpace` can index. Pinned by `installing_an_agenda_or_asset_over_a_remotes_occupant_trashes_the_occupant` and `fresh_remote_ids_are_recycled`.
  - **Measured (random-vs-random, 96 games, seed 1):** `CardTrashed` 87 → 279 (installs over), `CardAccessed` 814 → 687 with `RunSucceeded` unchanged (585 → 577) — a breach of a remote now finds one card in its root, not a pile.
- [x] **T6 — Scoring an agenda costs a click — DONE** (`fix/scoring-is-free`). `score_agenda` spent one, and `PlayerAction::ScoreAgenda`'s doc recorded it as intended ("matches `AdvanceCard`'s just-a-click shape"). Real rule: scoring is free, is not an action, and is legal on zero clicks — the Corp advances with the turn's last click and scores before ending it, which a click cost made impossible. `ScoreAgenda` stays in `classify_action`'s click-action group for the two guards it genuinely shares (no scoring mid-run; a Runner window afterwards), with a comment saying so. Only *Luminal Transubstantiation*'s test had pinned the click (3 − 1 + 3 = 5 → 6); `scoring_costs_no_click_and_is_legal_on_zero_clicks` pins the rule. Random-vs-random: `AgendaScored` 15 → 17 — small, since a random Corp rarely advances to completion; the heuristic Corp (B.4) is where this will show.
- [x] **T7 — Uniqueness (◆) is not modeled — DONE** (`feat/uniqueness`). `card/dto.rs` parsed `uniqueness`; `cards/netrunnerdb.rs` dropped it; nothing on `CardDefinition`. Now `CardDefinition::unique` is joined from the catalog on `numeric_id` — never authored in card JSON, so it cannot drift from the printed card — and every install handler calls `engine::trash_earlier_unique_copy` just before the new copy lands: the Runner's earlier copy goes to the Heap, the Corp's to Archives (faceup if it was rezzed). It is an **install-time** rule, not a deckbuilding one, so the sample decks' 3× Spin Doctor and 3× Cookbook stay legal as data. The console limit stays as its own rule (a subtype, and it rejects rather than replaces). `system_gateway_unique_cards_are_flagged_from_the_catalog` pins the eleven ◆ cards; `installing_a_second_copy_of_a_unique_card_trashes_the_first` pins both sides.
  - Measured (random-vs-random, 96 games): small, as duplicates in play are rare — Cookbook trashes 1 → 3, Docklands Pass 0 → 2, Anoetic Void 4 → 8. The heuristic never installs a second copy over a first (an install-over of a unique scores exactly zero against its own board), so it shows nothing there; that is the bot, not the rule.
- [x] **T8 — "Until the end of this encounter" strength buffs survive an end-the-run — DONE** (`fix/run-and-access-bookkeeping`). `reset_encounter_strength_buffs` was called only when ICE was passed normally; `Effect::EndTheRun` cleared `active_run` without it, so a Runner bounced by ICE kept every pumped point for the rest of the turn. **`run::end_run` is now the only way a run leaves `GameState`**: it snapshots `last_completed_run`, clears `active_run` and resets the encounter buffs, and the six sites that used to do one or two of those by hand (`jack_out`, `Effect::EndTheRun`, and four in `run::access`) call it. Same one-choke-point move as `drain_deferred_triggers` and `memory::refresh`.
  - **Found alongside it:** `paid_ability::close_window`'s `Success` arm *pushed* `RunCompleted` for a run on an empty server but never *dispatched* it, so `Trigger::OnRunEnded` — Mayfly's "when this run ends, trash this program" — never fired on the most ordinary run there is, a run at an empty Archives. It dispatches now (`closing_the_success_window_on_an_empty_server_dispatches_run_ended`), guarded against a flatline mid-access having already emitted one.
- [x] **T9 — `RunSucceeded` fired at the approach-server step, before the breach — DONE** (`fix/approach-server-before-success`). `OnApproachServer` (*Manegarm Skunkworks*, *Anoetic Void*) was dispatched from the *same* event, so a run those cards ended had already counted as successful, paid out every "when your run is successful" trigger and set `made_successful_run_this_turn`. NSG's order is approach server (jack-out legal, non-ICE rez) → run is successful → breach.
