Now `GameEvent::ServerApproached` fires where `RunSucceeded` used to and carries `OnApproachServer`; `RunSucceeded` and everything keyed to it (Jailbreak's rider, Docklands Pass, Zahya, the flag) fire in `engine::complete_run` — the Runner's commit — before the pre-access window opens. `dispatcher::still_applies` treats the new event as run-scoped.
  - **A second rule was hiding under the merge:** with approach and success one step, the Runner could jack out *after* the run was successful — `JackOut` was legal from the pre-access window, and a random Runner did it on half its successful runs (`RunSucceeded` 1619 against `RunCompleted` 409). `complete_run` now closes the jack-out window; the approach step is the last chance to leave.
  - Pinned by `a_run_ended_at_approach_is_not_successful_and_success_arrives_on_commit`. Seven card tests (Red Team ×2, Pennyshaver, Conduit, Jailbreak ×2, Docklands Pass) had asserted "when successful" effects at the approach step and now assert them on the commit; nine event-list assertions name `ServerApproached`.
  - **Measured (96 games, seed 1).** Random-vs-random: `ServerApproached` 1280, `RunSucceeded` 1619 → 585 and `RunCompleted` 409 → **585** — every successful run now breaches — with `RunJackedOut` 1410 → 832 (the illegal post-success ones gone) and `AgendaStolen` 145 → 202 as commits reach the cards. Heuristic-vs-heuristic: 1204 approached, 1204 succeeded, 1204 completed; `RunEndedByEffect` 533 → 440 now that an approach-step end is an end and not also a success.
- [x] **T10 — ICE can be rezzed at any paid-ability window, and on the Corp's own turn — DONE** (`fix/ice-rezzes-only-when-approached`). `rez_ice` permitted the Corp's action phase or any open window for every card alike. Now the rule is by card type: ICE only while the Runner is approaching *that install* (`RulesError::IceNotBeingApproached` otherwise), assets and upgrades at any priority — their own turn or any open window. `RunIce` carries `install_id`, threaded from `build_run_ice` and surfaced on `PublicRunIce` (public: it is the same handle `PublicInstalledCard` already shows), so two copies of one ICE on a server cannot be confused and `determinize` rebuilds the run with the right handles. A `serde(default)` keeps pre-existing histories loading.
  - **What flipped:** `corp_rez_ice_for_ice_not_at_current_position_does_not_affect_run_ice` had pinned "pre-emptive" rezzing of an inner ICE mid-run as legal; it is now `corp_cannot_rez_ice_the_run_has_not_reached`. Three own-turn rez tests became asset tests (their cost/turn semantics are unchanged for assets) and `ice_cannot_be_rezzed_on_the_corps_own_turn` pins the ICE half, including that `legal_actions` does not offer it.
  - **Measured (96 games, seed 1).** Random-vs-random: `IceRezzed` 1229 → 1097 and `RezIce` 1212 → 1075 — the rezzes that vanished are the at-home ones — while `AgendaScored` 22 → 54 and `RunSucceeded` 1494 → 1619, since the Corp keeps the credits it used to burn rezzing unthreatened ICE. Heuristic-vs-heuristic moves the same way (`IceRezzed` 716 → 660, `AgendaScored` 126 → 144).
- [x] **T11 — `MAX_PENDING_CHOICE_OPTIONS = 2`, but *Ansel 1.0* and *Brân 1.0* present three options — DONE** (`feat/action-space-v2`). The third is the decline; `index_of` returned `None` for it, so on the index path — gym, PUCT, the index sweep — the Corp could never decline and was forced to install off Ansel/Brân whenever a target existed. The comment claimed 2 was "the largest any System Gateway card offers"; nothing checked. Now 4, and **`every_card_fits_the_action_space_caps` walks every embedded card's JSON** (via `Effect::for_each_effect`) against `MAX_PENDING_CHOICE_OPTIONS`, `MAX_COST_CHOICE_OPTIONS`, `MAX_ABILITIES_PER_CARD` and `MAX_SUBROUTINES`, so the next set cannot silently exceed a cap. `a_three_option_pending_choice_can_be_declined_on_the_index_path` pins the concrete case.
  - **`ActionSpace::SIZE` 1357 → 1646 — layout v2, taken as one deliberate break.** Retraining was already required (Phase 2 §5), so every layout change landed together: the sided draw's two-wide segment; `MAX_HAND_SIZE` 12 → 16; `MAX_ACCESS_SELECTION` 12 → 32 (an Archives breach); a new `MAX_DECK_ZONE = 50` bounding `ToggleCardSelection` (Malapert Data Vault selects from all of R&D — the old comment's "every selectable zone is hand-sized" was false); the deleted free break's hole reclaimed; `PurgeVirusCounters` folded into the unit block. `MAX_TRACE_BID` stays 30 (no card in the pool has a Trace). An exported policy's head must be resized *and* retrained; `checkpoints/` was empty. The view-path and index-path coverage reports now agree action for action on the same seeds.
- [x] **T12 — An upgrade installed on Archives is never accessed — DONE** (`fix/run-and-access-bookkeeping`). `compute_accessed_cards`' Archives arm was the only server arm that omitted `root_installs_on`, while `install_card_candidates` explicitly offered upgrades onto Archives. Two-line fix, pinned by `an_upgrade_in_archives_root_is_accessed_alongside_the_archived_cards`.
- [x] **Played Events now go to the Heap — DONE** (same branch; was a Tier-2 item). `play_event` took the card from the grip and put it nowhere, so every Event the Runner played left the game and the Heap under-counted by one per Event. Caught by the conservation invariant the moment it existed.

### 2. Tier 2 — narrower gaps, index-path caps, latent aliasing — DONE

**Status: every Tier-2 item is either fixed (seven branches, September 2026, each measured through the harness and merged with both 256-seed sweeps clean) or re-verified as already correct and re-described below.** Re-reading the list against the code before starting changed its shape: three items were live rules bugs, not latent ones (the run's ICE snapshot — Brân 1.0's install was never encountered; the memory limit — a trashed console left every program installed; `ChooseTriggerToResolve` — a player could not order their own triggers), and four were already fixed or mis-described. What remains open is bot quality, recorded in Phase 2 §5.

- **Index caps — already raised by T11's layout v2**, which this bullet predated: `MAX_ACCESS_SELECTION` 12 → 32, `TOGGLE_CARD_SELECTION_LEN` → `MAX_DECK_ZONE` = 50, `MAX_HAND_SIZE` 12 → 16, and the false "all ≤ MAX_HAND_SIZE" comment corrected. `MAX_TRACE_BID` stays 30: `Effect::Trace` and `rules/trace.rs` are live and tested but **card-dead** — no card in the pool has a trace (System Gateway's *Public Trail* genuinely has none). `complete_run`'s missing `require_no_window`, also listed here once, was fixed by T9.
- [x] **Programs were never trashed when memory dropped — DONE** (`fix/memory-limit-enforced`). `available_memory` saturated at zero and its doc claimed a negative budget was "not reachable through `apply_action`"; it was, the moment *Retribution* or *Ansel 1.0* trashed a console under a full rig — the Runner kept 5 MU of programs on 4 MU indefinitely. `memory::memory_balance` is now the signed ledger and `memory::enforce_limit`, run at the `apply_action` choke point right after `refresh`, is the Netrunner checkpoint: while over, it parks a 1-of-N `ChooseCards` over the Runner's own programs (through `Effect::PromptChooseCards`, so the fewer-than-`min` guard is inherited) and emits `GameEvent::MemoryLimitExceeded { over_by }`; the next action re-checks and re-parks until the rig fits. One program per action on purpose — *Mayfly* is 2 MU, so "how many" has no answer until the Runner has chosen which. The Runner decides mid-Corp-turn (`current_actor` gives a parked chooser precedence). Random-vs-random, 96 games: `MemoryLimitExceeded` 0 — no console left play under a full rig in that sample (a random Runner rarely reaches 4 MU in use), every other counter identical; it is a totality fix, pinned by four card tests. Note for the harness: a selection-trash emits `CardsSelected`, not `CardTrashed`, so `CardCoverage::trashed` does not see it (pre-existing; *Retribution*'s victims are invisible the same way).
- [x] **The run's ICE list was a snapshot — DONE** (`fix/run-ice-reconciliation`). `start_run` built `run.ice` once and nothing revisited it, so *Brân 1.0*'s "install a piece of ice directly inward from this ice" put the new ICE into `corp.installed` where the run never looked: it was never approached, and the run reached the server one ICE early. `Effect::DerezCard` left the snapshot's `rezzed` stale, and `Effect::SwapInstalledIce` (*Tāo Salonga*) had to refuse outright during a run. `run::reconcile_ice` now rebuilds the list from `corp.installed` in install order, keyed by `InstallId` — a surviving entry keeps its per-run state (subroutine statuses, `current_strength` with this run's `ModifyStrength` deltas) and re-reads only `rezzed`; a new install gets a fresh `RunIce` — then re-anchors `position` on the install the run was standing on. If that install is gone the encounter ends without `IcePassed`, the jack-out window opens, and the run stands on the first ICE not yet passed (or the server); a stale `WindowCheckpoint::Run` window is cleared and `ServerApproached` dispatched. Called at the `apply_action` choke point after the deferred drain, in `advance_run` before a `Continue` (Brân's install lands in the same handler that then passes Brân), and in `resolve_encounter_ice` before firing subroutines; `resolve_unbroken_subroutines` stops once the phase is no longer `EncounterIce`. `CannotSwapIceDuringActiveRun` is deleted — a swap is simply reflected at the run's next step. `determinize` reuses the card sampled for the same install rather than drawing twice for one `InstallId`. Random-vs-random, 96 games: `IceEncountered` 529 → 539, `IceApproached` 1517 → 1530, `SubroutineFired` 759 → 775, Brân's `subroutines_fired` 56 → 58 (its install path is rare in random play; the end-to-end card test is the proof). 27 test fixtures hand-built a run with no ICE on the board — a state the engine reads as "that ICE was trashed" — and now call `test_support::install_the_runs_ice`.
- [x] **Access bookkeeping — DONE** (`fix/access-bookkeeping`), three small rules fixes: (1) **`can_trash` was frozen at presentation.** `AccessPhase::PendingChoice` carried a hint computed when the card was shown and `legal_actions` gated `TrashAccessedCard` on it, so a Runner who gained credits in the paid-ability window before deciding was never offered the trash. The field is gone from the state, the public view and `determinize`; `resolve_trash` reads live credits and the `apply_action` probe is the only gate. (2) **Facedown Archives cards never turned faceup.** A breach now flips every card in Archives (`compute_accessed_cards`' Archives arm, so a replaced access reveals nothing); before, the Runner's view named a card mid-access and masked it again the moment the run ended, and *Jinteki: Restoring Humanity* kept paying for "facedown" cards the Runner had read. (3) **`AdditionalAccessGranted` was emitted for a no-op** — an extra access on Archives or a remote is meaningless by the rules (the whole zone/root is accessed anyway), so the two-counter design is correct and the effect now records nothing for those servers. Random-vs-random, 96 games: RNG drift only (`TrashAccessedCard` 65 → 63) — the window between being shown a card and paying for it is one a random Runner rarely gains credits in; pinned by tests.
- [x] **Link was structurally 0 — DONE** (`feat/printed-link`). `RunnerState::link_strength` was read by `trace.rs` and written by nothing. `CardDefinition::base_link` is now joined from the catalog's `base_link` exactly as `unique` is (`netrunnerdb::convert_one`, `embedded::fill_catalog_metadata`, and a row in `printed_values_agree_with_the_netrunnerdb_catalog`), and `GameState::setup` seeds `link_strength` from the Runner identity beside `max_hand_size_bonus`. Static, not derived: only identities carry link in the implemented pool — *Kate "Mac" McCaffrey* (1) is the sole non-zero case, every System Gateway identity prints 0 — so a seed suffices; hardware that grants link would need the `memory`-style derivation, noted on the field. Card-dead until a Trace card exists (`MAX_TRACE_BID` stays 30). Random-vs-random, 96 games: identical to the previous report, as it must be.
- [x] **Harness v2: trigger coverage is observed, not inferred — DONE** (`feat/trigger-fired-event`). `GameEvent::TriggerFired { card, trigger }` is emitted by `ability::fire_card_triggers` — the one loop behind `process_card_triggers` and every firing in `dispatcher` — after a `TriggeredEffect`'s requirement passes and before its effects. `Coverage::triggers_eligible`, which inferred "had its moment" from the event that would have offered the trigger, is replaced by `triggers_fired`, read straight off the event; the inference table is deleted. Random-vs-random, 96 games: 2,991 firings across **61** card/trigger pairs where the inference could name 34 — it could not see board-wide triggers (`OnTurnStart`: Smartware Distributor 765, Tranquilizer 260, Botulus 177), `OnRunEnded`, `OnApproachServer`, `OnDiscardPhaseEnd`, or the identities at all. Of the 58 triggers the sample-pool cards declare, 53 fired; the 5 that never did are every agenda's own `OnAgendaScored` (Above the Law, Longevity Serum, Malapert Data Vault, Orbital Superiority, Send a Message) — a random Corp scores almost nothing, which is a fact about the seating, not the engine (heuristic play scores them). Actions and every other event count are identical: the event is a pure record. The two plain entry points (`process_card_triggers` and the unit tests that drive it) do not announce, so the 28 exact-vector `ability.rs` tests were untouched; 18 dispatch-path assertions gained the marker.
- **Recurring credits — correct as coded, but identity-only.** The old bullet ("refill only on the Corp's turn", `turn.rs`) described the code, not a bug: the one recurring-credit card in the pool is the Corp identity *NBN: Making News*, and its pool refilling at the start of the Corp's turn is exactly "at the start of their controller's turn". The real limit is capacity — `recurring_credits`/`recurring_credits_max` live on `CorpState` and are seeded from the Corp identity alone; there is no per-install recurring pool on either side, and the "also when rezzed/installed" half of the refill rule is unmodelled. It becomes a bug the day a Runner or non-identity recurring-credit card is implemented, and that card's branch is where the per-install pool belongs.
- **Multi-access — the two-counter design is correct for the pool.** `RunState::additional_hq_access`/`additional_rd_access` exist because HQ and R&D are the only servers whose access is capped at one card; an "additional access" on Archives or a remote is meaningless by the rules (the whole zone or root is breached anyway). Four cards grant extra accesses (*The Maker's Eye*, *Docklands Pass*, *Jailbreak*, *Conduit*), all on centrals. The only defect was the dishonest `AdditionalAccessGranted` event for the no-op case — fixed above.
- **Forfeit and Region — absent from System Gateway, so nothing is approximated.** Zero occurrences in `data/corp`, `data/runner` or the engine; `SG_UNIMPLEMENTED` needs no entry for them. The cards that need them are Core's *Data Dealer*, *Posted Bounty*, *Archer*, *SanSan City Grid* and Elevation's *Greenmail*, *Plutus*, *Biawak*, *Mahkota Langit Grid* — none implemented. `keywords` already arrives from the catalog, so "Region" would be readable for free; the "limit 1 region per server" install rule has no home yet and would go in `install_card` beside the one-card-per-remote rule. *Both halves closed at Elevation Stages 6 and 7 (3 September 2026): `CardSubtype::Region`, enforced in `engine::place_corp_card`, and `Effect::ForfeitAgendas`, offered as a `PresentChoice` by `engine::rez_ice` for Biawak's "forfeit 1 agenda as you rez this ice" — see Phase 1 §8.*
- [x] **Loose ends — DONE** (`fix/audit-loose-ends`), four items noticed during Tier 2 and closed together. (1) **`install_program` accepted a trojan**: `legal_actions` never offered `InstallProgram` for an `installs_on_ice` card, but the handler did not check, so a submitted one landed in the rig with no host — Botulus inert, and *Tranquilizer* failing its own third turn-start when `DerezCard(HostIce)` found no host to resolve and the error propagated out of the dispatch. `RulesError::TrojanMustBeHostedOnIce`. (2) **A selection-trash of a piece of ICE stranded its trojans**: `pending_choice::remove_installed_card` was the one removal site that never called `cascade_trash_hosted_programs`, leaving Botulus/Tranquilizer in the rig pointing at ICE that no longer existed (the same `DerezCard(HostIce)` failure). It cascades now. (3) **Selection-trashes recorded nothing**: the path emitted only `CardsSelected`, so Ansel 1.0, Ballista, Retribution, Above the Law, Carnivore, Longevity Serum, Hansei Review, Anoetic Void and the memory-limit trash were all invisible to the harness's per-card `trashed` count. A move into a discard pile (`OwnHeap`/`OwnArchives`/`OpponentDiscard`) now emits `CardTrashed` naming the card's owner; nothing dispatches on it, so no rules change. Random-vs-random, 96 games: `CardTrashed` 372 → 446, **20 cards newly seen as trashed** (Botulus, Carnivore, Hansei Review, Palisade, Tranquilizer…), every other counter identical. (4) **Two hand-rolled `run.ice` rez syncs deleted** — `rez_ice`'s and `Effect::RezInstalledIgnoringCost`'s — as redundant with `run::reconcile_ice`; the latter matched by `CardId` and was unreachable (Send a Message fires with no run or mid-access). The follow-up recorded here — `InstalledRunnerCard::hosted_on_ice` was a `CardId`, so the cascade could not tell two copies of one ICE apart — is closed in §4 (`feat/install-id-resolution-context`). `CHOOSE_TRIGGER_LEN` stays a `debug_assert`; widening is a pure append when a sweep trips it.
- **Not a rules item, and closed in Phase 2 §5:** the heuristic bots' evaluator blindnesses (run term, savings, draw, Corp draw and asset rez, Carmen, agenda placement).
- [x] **`ChooseTriggerToResolve` aliased by `CardId` — DONE** (`fix/trigger-order-by-index`). It resolved by first-match `CardId`, which failed on two axes: two copies of one card, and — reachable in the pool — one card owing several triggers to one event (a successful run on HQ queues `OnSuccessfulRun` and `OnSuccessfulRunOnHq` as separate `DeferredTrigger`s, `dispatcher.rs`). The second entry could never be ordered first; `legal_actions` listed it twice and `ActionSpace` mapped both to one slot. The action now carries `index: usize` — a position into the parked `pending` list, which is what the `ActionSpace` segment had always encoded (no layout change; `SIZE` stays 1646), the same shape as `ToggleCardSelection`. `TriggerOrderChosen` names the `trigger` too, and the TUI label reads "Resolve Docklands Pass (OnSuccessfulRunOnHq) first" off `view.pending_decision`. `offer_trigger_order` debug-asserts the list fits `CHOOSE_TRIGGER_LEN`. Random-vs-random, 96 games: `ChooseTriggerToResolve` 403 → 415 (the previously unreachable second entries), no stall. `HistoryEntry.action` shape changed; no compatibility promise exists for recorded actions. (`ConfirmCardSelection`'s first-match removal was fixed earlier — see §0.5.)
- [x] **`apply_action` did not own the card-type rule — DONE** (`fix/apply-action-owns-card-type-guards`). Only `legal_actions`' candidate generators checked that a card fit the action installing or playing it, contradicting "`apply_action`'s guards are the only authority" — and a remote client submits straight into `apply_action`. An agenda submitted with `InstallSlot::Ice` was installed as ICE and encountered as a 0-strength barrier (`run/engine.rs`' `_ => IceType::Barrier`); any grip card could be "played" as an event into the heap. Every install/play handler now checks the type (`RulesError::CardTypeMismatch`; agendas/assets refuse a central's root with `NotInstallableInCentralServer`), `Effect::InstallFromZoneIgnoringCost` refuses a non-ICE in an ICE slot, `build_run_ice` yields nothing for a non-ICE instead of a barrier, and the two `_ => {}` type dispatches in `legal_actions` are spelled out so a new `CardType` is a compile error rather than a silently unoffered class — the shape the memory-cost bug had. Same branch, two adjacent aliasing bugs: **Brân 1.0/Ansel 1.0's "this ice" resolved by first-match `CardId`** — with two Brâns on two servers the new ICE went inward of the wrong one — and **fell back to `ServerId::Archives` when Brân was gone**, which equalled the JSON placeholder and so was never noticed. The host is now the ICE *being encountered* (by `InstallId`, which `insert_after` now carries; Brân's placeholder is `0`), and if it has left play nothing is installed and the chosen card stays in HQ. `has_usable_paid_ability` no longer counts the identity: `ActivateAbility` names an `InstallId` an identity does not have, so it would have opened a window whose only legal move was `PassPriority`. Random-vs-random, 96 games: no counter moved beyond RNG drift (`CardInstalled` 1633 → 1635) — every guard is unreachable through `legal_actions`, which is the point; pinned by `install_and_play_handlers_reject_a_card_of_the_wrong_type` and two Brân tests. `complete_run`'s missing `require_no_window`, also listed here, was fixed by T9.

### 3. Stale comments — the failure mode that hid the memory-cost bug — DONE

True when written, false by the time of the audit; every one below is corrected (`docs/rules-audit` and the fix branches), most replaced by a sentence saying what is true *and* what the old comment claimed, so the next reader knows the shape. `action.rs:18` (draw is Runner-only — still true, and the only record of T4), `:22-26` ("Runner grip/rig aren't modeled with card identity"), `:41-44` (`RunState::ice` "is left empty"), `:79-82`/`:88-92` ("no credit cost yet" — paid at `engine.rs:636/811/883`), `:204-206` (scoring "not-yet-modeled"); `setup.rs:187-191` claims the Corp's first-turn mandatory draw *diverges* from the real rules — it is backwards, NSG's Corp draws on turn one, and the comment invites someone to break a correct implementation; `action_mask.rs:19-21` ("real games essentially never reach these caps"), `:51-55`, `:141-143`, (the `:493-499` "must stay the LAST check" comment is gone with T1's `in_segment` fix). AGENTS.md's DSL Growth Rule cited "14 of 66 `Effect` variants"; the enum has 47 (see the corrected figure there).

### 4. Adjacent hazards — the *classes* the Tier-1/2 bugs belonged to (September 2026)

Three read-only passes over the whole engine for the shapes above — "kept resolving after the game ended", "first match by `CardId`", "silent fallback / catch-all arm" — rather than for specific rules. Same discipline as §1/§2: one branch per class, random-vs-random report before and after, both 256-seed sweeps before merging.

**Status: closed, six branches.** Two findings were reachable and game-deciding in the sample decks (a win silently reverted after a Clearinghouse flatline; a central steal removing the Corp's installed copy), one was a structural root cause behind eleven multi-copy bugs (`InstallId` never reached effect resolution), and the rest were authority gaps, catch-all arms and five card definitions that had drifted from print. **Follow-ups, recorded here so they are not re-found — the first two closed September 2026 (`fix/audit-followups`):**
- **An access now pins the installed instance it is resolving.** `AccessState` stays `CardId`-keyed in its lists and phase — two copies of one upgrade are the same printed card, both get accessed in the same breach, and `SelectCardToAccess`'s action slot is a position, so the Runner's *choice* between them is immaterial — but `AccessState::pending_install` names the instance the pending card is (set at presentation, the first root copy not in `resolved_installs`), `GameEvent::CardAccessed` carries it, `remove_from_corp_zone` takes exactly that install for a trash or steal, and the dispatcher fires `OnAccessed` against it. Both by-`CardId` first-match sites were live: two copies of an upgrade in one root both resolved to the lower-indexed install, so the second trash removed nothing and a trap's `AddCounters` landed on the wrong copy. Kept on `AccessState` rather than the phase variants (one pending card; the variants are built in dozens of fixtures), public and unmasked in the view (an install id says *which*, not *what*), carried by `determinize`. Tests pin two-instance trashing, the per-instance trigger, and the zone-card `None` case.
- **Red Team offers only centrals not yet run this turn.** `RunnerState::servers_run_this_turn` records every run at `start_run` (click, event or Red Team's own) and clears when the Runner's turn starts — state, not `ResolutionContext`, because it must outlive every action of the turn; public in the view. `Effect::PromptChooseServer` gained the field `exclude_servers_run_this_turn` (a field, not a variant or requirement: nothing composable narrows a server *offer*), applied at park time; with nothing left to offer the effect fails with `RulesError::NoServerLeftToRun` *before* parking, the `check_run_may_begin` pattern, so `legal_actions`' probe withholds the click instead of sinking it into an unresolvable decision. The narrowed list is what the decision carries, so resolution's re-check and the candidate filter are unchanged.
- **Measured (96 games, seed 1):** random-vs-random and heuristic-vs-heuristic move by a handful of games (steps 38,517 → 38,460 and 49,623 → 49,584; `AgendaStolen` 204 → 205 and 200 → 196), Red Team activations 15 → 17 — two correctness fixes on rare paths, as expected; both 256-seed sweeps clean.
- Still open by design: `CardTarget::CorpInstalled { card, server }` is DSL-authored by card and server and stays so; `EffectRequirement::OncePerTurn` on an identity or event is tag-only by design. The one item that was not a rules item — the heuristic Runner's eagerness — is closed in Phase 2 §5.

- [x] **A card accessed in a central removed the Corp's *installed* copy — DONE** (`fix/access-removal-zone-gate`). `access::remove_from_corp_zone`'s server preference (§1 T2) fell back to "any root install with that `CardId`" for *every* server, so stealing Offworld Office off the top of R&D — or trashing a Nico Campaign out of HQ — removed the advanced/rezzed copy from its remote and left the accessed copy in the hidden zone to be accessed again. Card conservation held, so the sweep invariant could not see it; Offworld Office ships ×3 in two sample decks, so it happened in ordinary play. The fallback now applies to remotes only; a central access matches an install only in that central's own root (an upgrade). Random-vs-random, 96 games: Offworld Office `scored` 3 → 5 with `stolen` 53 → 52 — installed copies now survive a central steal — and every downstream counter drifts off that, which is the signature of a bug that was live. Follow-up: `AccessState` is still `CardId`-keyed, so two copies of one upgrade in one root remain indistinguishable.
- [x] **A win could be silently reverted — DONE** (`fix/game-over-hardening`). `GamePhase::GameOver` was assigned at four sites and none cleared what was mid-resolution: `paid_ability_window`, `pending_*`, `active_trace`, `deferred_triggers`, or (outside access) `active_run`. `is_resolution_blocked()` ignored `is_over()`, `apply_action` had no GameOver guard, and `close_window`'s `StartOfTurn`/`PostAction` arms wrote `phase = Action(side)` unconditionally. Reachable with the sample decks: *Clearinghouse*'s `OnTurnStart` choice is parked under the start-of-turn window; when it flatlined the Runner the window survived, `current_actor` named the Corp, `PassPriority` was legal, and two passes later the phase was `Action(Corp)` again — play continued with the Runner at zero cards. The same leftovers let the rest of a trigger plan, a `Sequence`, or the deferred queue keep resolving into a finished game, and an `Err` from any of it (e.g. `reconcile_ice` dispatching `ServerApproached`) rejected the very action that had ended the game, so `legal_actions` never offered the kill. Now: `win::end_game` is the single transition (sets the phase, ends the run, clears every parked field, emits `GameOver` exactly once — the duplicate on the Jinteki PE steal-flatline path is gone); `apply_action` rejects everything with `RulesError::GameIsOver` first, so `legal_actions` is empty after a win whatever the state holds; `GameState::resolution_halted()` (= over ∨ blocked; kept separate because `fire_plan` *queues* when blocked and must not refill the queue `end_game` emptied) stops `Sequence`, the trigger-effects loop (which had no stop at all), `fire_plan`, `drain_deferred_triggers`, `still_applies`, `offer_trigger_order`, `reconcile_ice`, `close_window`, `resolve_encounter_ice` and `enter_start_of_turn` (also guarded after the `TurnStarted` dispatch, before its window opens); `complete_run` no longer reaches `open_window`'s `unreachable!` after a flatlining `RunSucceeded` trigger — the original crash shape at its last unguarded site (`Success` is deliberately not a checkpoint, so it could not be routed through `open_window_if_at_checkpoint`). Random-vs-random, 96 games: `GameOver` events 104 → **96** (one per game), actions identical, `RunnerFlatlined` 42 → 39 and `CardDerezzed` 212 → 191 (post-game firings that no longer happen). This closes the "`Effect::Sequence` keeps resolving after the game ends" hazard recorded under Phase 1.5.
- [x] **`InstallId` never reached effect resolution — DONE** (`feat/install-id-resolution-context`, three commits). The `InstallId` work reached the action boundary (`PlayerAction`, `ActionSpace`, `legal_actions`, the view) and the selection/swap/rez/advance/score paths, but `ability::ResolutionContext` still carried only `acting_card: &CardId`, so every "this card" resolution — counters, advancement, self-trash, host, strength, `OncePerTurn` — found the **first installed copy**. Three feeders knew the install and threw it away: `activate_ability`, trigger dispatch (`DeferredTrigger { card: CardId }`), and `PromptChooseCards::then`. Every counter card in the sample decks ships at ×3, so this was live everywhere: two Fermenters shared one pool and cashing #2 trashed #1; Nico Campaign #2 loaded its nine counters onto #1 (possibly unrezzed, where masking hides counters); Seamless Launch advanced the first Offworld Office while `ScoreAgenda` scored the chosen one; a decoy Urtica Cipher dealt the advanced copy's damage; three Telework Contracts shared one once-per-turn; two Botulus could break on the wrong host or neither. Now: (1) `ResolutionContext::acting_install`, with `acting_corp_install`/`acting_rig_card` as the only way resolution looks up "this card" — by install when there is one (an install that has left play resolves to *nothing*, never a sibling), by first-match `CardId` only for identities, events, operations and subroutines; every parked struct (`PendingPaidChoice`, `PendingPrevention`, `TraceState`, all `PendingDecision`s) carries `source_install` across the `PlayerAction` boundary (State Hygiene: it survives a parked decision); `OncePerTurn` is keyed by `(tag, install)`. (2) `DeferredTrigger { install, target_install }`; every dispatcher plan builder iterates installs, `IceRezzed` names its install, `fire_card_triggers` takes the record. (3) `hosted_on_ice: Option<InstallId>` — closes the follow-up left by the loose-ends branch. No `ActionSpace` change; additive `serde(default)` fields plus one view type change. Random-vs-random, 96 games: `CardDerezzed` 191 → 153 (each Tranquilizer accrues its own counters rather than all three feeding copy #1), Regolith Mining License `activated` 46 → 50, Telework Contract 49 → 42, `TriggerFired` 2974 → 2879 — copies now spend and fire independently. Pinned by eight two-copy card tests. Follow-up: `AccessState` is still `CardId`-keyed (two copies of one upgrade in one root), and `EffectRequirement::OncePerTurn` for an identity/event stays tag-only by design.
- [x] **Catch-all decode arms and a no-op that consumed a once-per-turn — DONE** (`fix/decode-catch-alls-and-noop-costs`). `action_mask`'s unit block decoded *every* surplus index as `DeclinePendingPaidChoice` (`_ =>`), so the next appended unit action — the ROADMAP's own append-never-shift rule — would have shared its slot and been unreachable while `get_action_mask` called it legal; `decode_install_slot` made any non-zero index `Root`. Both are exhaustive now (`_ => None`), the `_ => IceType::Barrier` shape again. Carnivore's ability was offered with one card in the grip: `PromptChooseCards { min: 2 }` silently parked nothing, the accessed card stayed, and the click and once-per-turn were spent. New `EffectRequirement::ZoneHasAtLeast { zone, count }` gates the *offer* (an `EffectIf` could only have skipped the effect after the cost was paid — the one-line reason for a variant). Random-vs-random, 96 games: Carnivore `activated` 28 → 12 — sixteen of its activations were the empty ones. Three stale comments corrected (`agenda_points`' "hardcoded lookup" that was already data-driven; `end_turn`'s "no card has an end-of-turn trigger" with *Jinteki: Restoring Humanity*'s `OnDiscardPhaseEnd` live; the Runner's click-draw on an empty stack, which is correct and reads as an oversight).
- [x] **Five card JSONs diverged from print — DONE** (`fix/card-fidelity`). Found by the same pass; none had a `_note`, so nothing announced them. **Anoetic Void**: the 2[c] was `LoseCredits` (saturating — a Corp on 0 credits ended the run free) followed by a card selection that silently no-oped with fewer than two cards in HQ (the Corp paid, the run went on). Now `EffectIf { ZoneHasAtLeast(OwnHq, 2) → OfferPaidChoice { Cost::Credits(2), … then EndTheRun } }`: a real cost, declinable, unaffordable when unaffordable, not offered with nothing to buy. **Jinteki: Restoring Humanity**: paid a flat 1[c] instead of 1 *per* facedown card — new `Amount::FacedownCardsInArchives` and `Effect::GainCreditsAmount(Side, Amount)` (no primitive counts; `EffectIf` branches). **Red Team**: modelled as `Trigger::OnSuccessfulRun` — paid on *every* successful run, remotes included, and its click ran any server. The payout is now the `on_success` rider of its own `PromptChooseServer` (Jailbreak's mechanism), which needed the run to carry the rider's *source*: `RunState::on_success_card`/`on_success_install`, so "take 3[c] from this resource" resolves as the Red Team that started the run; `allowed_servers` is the three centrals. "…you have not run this turn" stays unmodelled (no per-turn server-run record). **Hansei Review**: the Corp chose which HQ card to trash; new `CardTarget::RandomFromHq` rolls on the state's PRNG (deterministic under the seed). **Above the Law**: an opt-out `PresentChoice` wrapped a mandatory trash; unwrapped. Random-vs-random, 96 games: `red_team/OnSuccessfulRun` 27 → 0 as a trigger with `activated` 8 → 30 and `trashed` 8 → 1 (it drains only through its own runs now), `RunEndedByEffect` 356 → 383 and `PendingPaidChoiceOffered` 88 → 107 (Anoetic Void as a paid choice), `CreditsGained` 2484 → 2319 (the free payouts gone). DSL growth: one `Effect`, one `Amount`, one `CardTarget`, one `EffectRequirement` variant across H.4/H.5, each with its one-line "why composition didn't work".

---

## 🔍 System Gateway Card Fidelity Audit — September 2026

`fix/card-fidelity` found five cards drifted from print by *sampling*; this pass read **all 75 playable System Gateway cards** against NetrunnerDB's `stripped_text`, the engine code behind each primitive they use, and their play evidence (per-card tests + the 96-game coverage report). **The per-card record — verdict, evidence and the documented-approximations list — is `docs/system-gateway-card-audit.md`; this section is status.**

**Tally: 64 faithful (11 of them through documented approximations), 11 deviations — all fixed** (branch `fix/sg-card-fidelity-audit`, four commits), **plus one engine-wide finding.** Both 256-seed sweeps green with all coverage gates; measured random-vs-random, 96 games (`h5-…` → `sg-audit-random-random.json`). Two of the eleven were introduced *by the previous fidelity pass itself* (Hansei Review made random, Above the Law made mandatory) — the lesson recorded there stands reversed here: verify against the printed card and the rules document, never against what "removes a drawback".

- [x] **Hansei Review** — the HQ trash is the Corp's choice (no "at random" is printed; a player trashing from their own hand picks). Reverted to a Corp `PromptChooseCards`; `CardTarget::RandomFromHq` deleted with it.
- [x] **Above the Law** — printed "you **may** trash 1 installed resource"; the opt-out is restored.
- [x] **Predictive Planogram** — tagged, "you **may** resolve both instead" is a three-option choice, not a forced both. `predictive_planogram/OnPlay` 112 → 105 with `PresentChoice` 420 → 582 across the pool.
- [x] **Mayfly** — was trashed at the end of *every* run; the rider belongs to its break ability. The break leaves a hosted counter as the used-this-run marker (no `counter_kind`, so purges ignore it); `OnRunEnded` requires it. `mayfly/trashed` 11 → 3 and `activated` 0 → 15 — it survives long enough to be used at all.
- [x] **Karunā** — "The Runner may jack out" was `Effect::PermitJackOut`, a flag no loop paused for: both subroutines always fired in one batch. Subroutine 1 now parks a Runner choice between the two; `PermitJackOut` and `GameEvent::JackOutPermitted` deleted (`effects_seen/PermitJackOut` 8 → 0).
- [x] **Zahya Sadeghi** — her once-per-turn is consumed at trigger fire, and `OnRunEnded` fires for bounced runs: a 0-access central run burned it for 0[c]. New `EffectRequirement::AccessedAnyCardDuringLastRun` gates her: `zahya_sadeghi/OnRunEnded` 191 → 75 — the other 116 firings were paying nothing and eating the turn's use.
- [x] **Urtica Cipher** — `OnAccessed` fired from R&D/HQ too (flat 2 net from a deck access; a first-match token read could size the hit by an installed copy). New `EffectRequirement::ThisCardIsInstalled`, answered strictly off the dispatched install: `urtica_cipher/OnAccessed` 38 → 29 while its accesses *rose* 38 → 53.
- [x] **NBN: Reality Plus** — a tag taken as a **cost** (`Cost::TakeTags`, Funhouse) emitted `TagsGiven` undispatched, so the printed NBN+Funhouse pairing never fired. The cost's tag events now dispatch after the choice's own effect, deferring via `fire_each` if something parked.
- [x] **Mutual Favor / Pantograph** — both install clauses were unmodelled. New `Effect::InstallRunnerCardFromGrip` (pays cost, memory, console limit, unique rule) + `CardFilter::InstallableRunnerCard` (offer ≡ resolution via `engine::can_install_runner_card_from_grip`); Trojans stay click-installs. `effects_seen/InstallRunnerCardFromGrip` 0 → 31.
- [x] **Ansel 1.0** — its install used Brân's cost-ignoring fixed-destination effect: free, always into Ansel's own server, agendas into central roots, Operations selectable. New `Effect::PromptInstallCorpCard` reuses `PendingDecision::ChooseServer` (same action, `ActionSpace` slot, bots, masking) with a `PendingInstallFromZone` naming the card by *position* (an id would leak the HQ pick through the unmasked decision); `engine::corp_install_destinations` offers agendas/assets remotes only and ICE only where the 1[c]-per-protecting-ICE tax is affordable. `effects_seen/PromptInstallCorpCard` 0 → 26. **No `ActionSpace` change.**
- [x] **Engine-wide: new ICE landed *innermost*.** Every ICE install appended to `corp.installed`, whose per-server vec order is the run's approach order — reversing the approach order of every stacked server against Null Signal Games' outermost-install rule. Fixed in `engine::place_corp_card` (extracted from `install_card`, shared with Ansel's path); Brân's "directly inward" keeps its own `insert_after`. Visible pool-wide: `IcePassed` 949 → 1045 with `IceEncountered` 561 → 516 — a new, unrezzed piece now sits outside and is passed instead of the inner rezzed one being met first.

**DSL growth:** two `Effect` variants deleted (`PermitJackOut`, `RandomFromHq`), two added (`PromptInstallCorpCard`, `InstallRunnerCardFromGrip`), two `EffectRequirement`s (`AccessedAnyCardDuringLastRun`, `ThisCardIsInstalled`) and one `CardFilter` (`InstallableRunnerCard`) added, each with its one-line "why composition didn't work". Ratio re-measured in AGENTS.md.

**Still open, deliberately** (recorded in the audit doc's approximations list): Red Team's "a central you have not run this turn" (needs a per-turn record of servers run), Docklands Pass's "breach" ≈ successful run, Zahya's residual "may", Trojan effect-installs, and the pre-existing capacity items (§4 above).

## 🔍 Core Set (implemented subset) Fidelity Audit — September 2026

The SG audit's rubric repeated over the **19 implemented Core Set cards** — the hand-authored baseline set. **Per-card record: `docs/core-set-card-audit.md`.** Tally: **15 faithful (3 through documented approximations), 4 findings — all fixed** (branch `fix/core-set-card-fidelity-audit`, one commit). Both 256-seed sweeps green; the 96-game random-vs-random report is **bit-identical** to the SG-audit baseline (0 changed counters, same step count) — no sample deck fields a Core card, so nothing else could move.

- [x] **Weyland: Building a Better World never fired on Hedge Fund, Hansei Review or Predictive Planogram** — all printed Transactions, none carrying the `subtypes` field `Trigger::OnTransactionPlayed` dispatches on (a cross-set data gap the SG audit missed: subtypes are type-line, not rules text). Three JSON fixes; pinned by `building_a_better_world_gains_a_credit_when_hedge_fund_is_played`.
- [x] **Ice Wall modelled neither of its clauses** — not advanceable (no `advancement_requirement: 0` marker) and no per-counter strength. New `StrengthModifier::PerHostedAdvancement` (a rate; Pharos' variant is a threshold), baked in `build_run_ice` like its siblings.
- [x] **Gordian Blade's pump expired with the encounter** against printed "for the remainder of this run." New `BoostDuration::Run` backed by `InstalledRunnerCard::run_strength_buff`, cleared only in `run::engine::end_run` — one pump now carries across every encounter of a run and no further.
- [x] **Account Siphon forced its replacement and paid a flat 10** against "you **may** force the Corp to lose **up to 5**[c], then you gain 2[c] **for each credit lost**." `SetAccessReplacement` gains `optional` (serde-default `false`, wire format unchanged): `try_replace_access` parks the Runner's siphon-or-breach choice, and declining consumes the replacement so the next `CompleteRun` breaches normally. The gain is two `GainCreditsAmount(Amount::CreditsLostThisResolution)`s — the new `Amount` reads `ResolutionContext::credits_lost`, recorded by `Effect::LoseCredits`, whose `CreditsLost` event now reports what was actually removed (a Whitespace against 2[c] logs 2, not 3).

**Follow-up, recorded once here:** no sample deck fields a Core card, so the two agent sweeps and the coverage harness never touch these 19 — their play evidence is per-card tests only. A Core-flavored sample deck pair (behind the existing deck-legality gates) would put them under the same sweep pressure as System Gateway; sized as its own task, not started.

## 🔗 Phase 1.5: Session Unification (the single-player → network bridge) — DONE

Five places independently re-implemented the same match loop — `current_actor` → get action → `apply_action` → check `GameOver` — each with its own step budget. There is now **one `MAX_STEPS`, in `netrunner_session`**, and every caller pumps the same driver.

- [x] **Extracted the shared loop** into `netrunner_session` (new crate, deps `netrunner_core` + `netrunner_bots` + `serde` + `thiserror`), sitting between `netrunner_bots` and every consumer. Homed in its own crate rather than `netrunner_core`: the seat enum holds a `Box<dyn BotAgent>`, which core cannot name.
- [x] **Unified the seat interface on `ClientView` + `PlayerAction`.** The local human seat is now `Seat::External` and *structurally* cannot see `GameState` — the client-politeness hole is closed. `PlayerDriver` was **deleted outright**, not adapted: it was a blanket-impl'd clone of `netrunner_bots::Agent` with an identical signature, and every `Box<dyn PlayerDriver>` in the tree was already an `Indexed*Agent`. The index-based shape survives only inside `SinglePlayerSession`, which is the RL path.
- [x] **Two seat variants, not four.** The plan called for `Bot`/`LocalHuman`/`Channel`/`Indexed`. Under a **pull-shaped** loop (`fn step(&mut self) -> SessionStep`, which yields `Awaiting { side, view }` and stops) the last three collapse into one: a seat the session cannot resolve itself. They differ only in who pumps and in the `PlayerAction` ↔ index conversion at the boundary, which is `ActionSpace::action_at`'s job. **Do not re-split them.**
- [x] **`MatchHistory` moved into the driver**, so every path records actions and events — which is what gave the network path a game log (`ServerMessage::ActionLog`, one message per action; `App::action_log` renders it). Both TUI paths now share one four-region layout; the log-less `build_layout` is gone.
- [x] **`GameEndReason`/`classify_end_reason` moved to `netrunner_session::outcome`**, re-exported from `netrunner_server` so the wire protocol is unchanged. `netrunner_cli` used to depend on the whole server crate purely to classify end reasons on its *offline* path.

**Ported:** `netrunner_server::MatchSession` (now purely the async pump), `SinglePlayerSession` (thin index adapter), `netrunner_cli`'s local TUI, `netrunner_gym::env`, `netrunner_selfplay`, and the fifth copy that lived in `post_action_windows_stay_rare`.

**Two behavioural changes worth knowing:**
- `netrunner_gym`'s budget was `1_000` *per fast-forward call*, reset on every call — so a pathological episode was effectively unbounded. It is now one `MAX_STEPS` for the whole episode. `max_episode_steps` truncation stays in the env; it is an RL concern.
- `StallReason` splits `NoCurrentActor` / `NoLegalActions { side }` / `BudgetExhausted`, which the old `else { break }` conflated. `NoLegalActions` also stops a deadlocked position from *panicking*: every `BotAgent` asserts a non-empty `legal_actions`.

### Deadlock and crash: "a run can outlive the game it belongs to" — BOTH FIXED

Two bugs, one invariant. `damage::apply_damage` flatlines the Runner by setting `phase = GameOver` **without clearing `active_run`**, so a run can sit parked at `EncounterIce` after the game has ended. Two places then acted as though it were still live:

- [x] **Crash.** `open_window_if_at_checkpoint` asked `open_window` for a window, which reads the priority side straight off `phase` and hit its `unreachable!()`. It surfaced inside `build_client_view` — `legal_actions` probes candidates through `apply_action` — so merely *rendering* such a position brought the process down, putting `MatchSession::broadcast_state_updates` on the same hook. Guarded on `phase` being `Action(_)`.
- [x] **Deadlock.** One statement earlier, `resolve_encounter_ice`'s advance guard checked `active_run` and the four parked-state fields but never `phase`. Since `resolve_unbroken_subroutines` breaks its loop at `GameOver`, the rest of a multi-subroutine ICE stays `Pending`; advancing then handed `continue_run` the one thing it refuses (`SubroutinesStillPending`), and that `Err` propagated through `close_window` into `pass_priority`. Because `legal_actions` keeps only candidates `apply_action` accepts, that made the priority holder's **own `PassPriority`** illegal while `current_actor` still named them — no legal action at all, deterministically, forever. Reproduced at `decks::matchups()[0]` seed 2.

**The real lesson is the detection gap, now closed.** Both were trivially reachable on ordinary sample decks at low seeds, and both were invisible to every existing sweep, because those drive *index-based* agents whose `ActionSpace` round trip does not reach the path. `crates/netrunner_session/tests/no_deadlock_sweep.rs` now drives view-based `Seat::Agent`s, so each side chooses only from `legal_actions_for` — the per-seat `ClientView` slice a real client gets. See AGENTS.md's Testing Rule for the division of labour between the two sweeps.

### Adjacent hazard, since closed: `Effect::Sequence` kept resolving after the game ended

`Sequence` broke only on `is_resolution_blocked()`, not on `GameOver` — the same "kept going after the world ended" class as the two above — and was left as "harmless, revisit if a card needs it". The Rules Audit's adjacent-hazards pass (§4 there) found the class was not harmless at all: nothing cleared parked state at `GameOver`, and a leftover window could revert a win. `win::end_game` and `GameState::resolution_halted()` now stop every resolution loop, `Sequence` included.

---

## 🎓 Phase 1.75: Learn to Play

**The repo can play Netrunner but cannot teach it.** A new player launching `netrunner_cli` gets a flat list of legal actions and no explanation of clicks, servers, runs, or why any of it matters. Netrunner is famously hard to learn cold, and its asymmetry means learning it twice. This phase closes that, from both sides.

Two stages, in order:

1. **Scripted lessons** — gated, one-concept-at-a-time scenarios, a Corp track and a Runner track.
2. **A faithful NSG starter game** — the official preset starter decks, 6 agenda points to win, booster-pack staging — so a graduate gets a feel for a real game unguided.

> **The scripted lessons are our addition, not Null Signal's.** [Learn to Play](https://nullsignal.games/players/learn-to-play/) is a rulebook with training wheels, not a tutorial: fixed preset decks, a lowered 6-point win threshold, mechanics deferred to a booster pack (viruses, tags, meat damage, modal ice), and per-card clarifications keyed to the fixed deck. There are no scripted turns and no puzzles. Stage 2 is faithful to that; stage 1 is scaffolding NSG leaves to a human teacher. Don't "correct" stage 1 toward the source material later — the divergence is the point.

**The card pool is already done.** All 32 starter-deck cards and all 11 booster cards are implemented, playable System Gateway cards. Nothing needs authoring — verified card by card against `data/{corp,runner}/`.

**Placement, and why it is here rather than in Phase 1:** it does not need Phase 1's deck import, and its one hard prerequisite — Phase 1 §2's counter visibility, needed because the booster stage teaches viruses — **is now satisfied**. More importantly, the lesson driver was to be a `PlayerDriver` — the trait Phase 1.5 has now deleted. That ordering rationale has been discharged: the lesson driver is `netrunner_session::lesson::LessonSession`, a `Seat::External` learner pumped by the lesson script, the same shape the local TUI uses.

### 1. Configurable match rules (`netrunner_core`)

- [x] **Replaced the `WINNING_AGENDA_POINTS: u32 = 7` const (`rules/win.rs`) with a `MatchRules { winning_agenda_points: u32 }` struct on `GameState`** — `Default` = 7, `#[serde(default)]` so recorded histories still deserialize (pinned by a test), read by `win::check_win_conditions`; also on `ClientView` (a client has to be able to say how many points win) and carried by `determinize`. `GameState::setup_with(…, MatchRules, DeckOrder)` is the constructor that sets it; `setup` delegates with Standard rules and a shuffle, so no call site moved. **Done, `feat/phase-175-foundations`, September 2026.**
  - **A struct rather than a bare `u32` field, deliberately.** This is the State Hygiene Rule applied *before* the debt accrues rather than after: the starter game already varies one rule, and the second variant knob should extend a struct instead of becoming another loose field. Note that a match rule is not cross-effect context — it is fixed at setup and never threaded between effects — so this is not the scratchpad pattern that rule bans.
- [x] **`deck::agenda_point_range` did not need the win threshold after all.** The range was `20 + 2·⌊(size−45)/5⌋` above 45 with a flat 18 below — the same function as Null Signal's published `2 + 2·⌊size/5⌋` on every legal Standard size, and wrong only under 40, where the flatten sat. The unflattened rule reproduces every existing example and gives 34 cards → 14–16, exactly the starter Corp deck's 14; `agenda_point_range_matches_size_derived_examples` gained the 34 and 30 cases. Size alone determines the range; the 6-point starter game changes only `MatchRules::winning_agenda_points`.
- This touched `win.rs`, so it was measured as engine-level work: random-vs-random (96 games, seed 1) is byte-identical before and after — Standard rules are the default and every sample deck is 40–49 cards, where the range rule is unchanged. Heuristic-vs-heuristic moved (steps 49,584 → 53,398, `AgendaStolen` 196 → 215), which was attributed at the time to `determinize` sampling the two new identities as hidden cards. **That attribution is unproven**: the next branch found the heuristic seating is not reproducible run to run at all (Phase 2 §5), so the shift may be noise. Both 256-seed sweeps ran clean. *(Phase 2 §5 has since made the seating reproducible and then removed every identity from the pools; that measurement moved the same metrics the other way — steps −4,354, `AgendaStolen` −9 for sixteen identities leaving, against +3,814 and +19 here for two entering — so the sign agrees — but both shifts sit inside the seed-spread band recorded there, so neither is evidence on its own and the attribution stays a plausible guess.)*

### 2. Make the starter identities playable (`netrunner_core`)

Reverses the decision recorded under Phase 1 §7 — see there for why.

- [x] **Authored `data/corp/the_syndicate.json` and `data/runner/the_catalyst.json`** as hand-authored blank identities carrying `numeric_id` 30077/30076. `min_deck_size` is **30 for both — the catalog's printed value**, not the 34 this bullet guessed (the 34-card Corp starter satisfies 30; the printed-values gate does not compare the field, so print wins). Both carry the new `CardDefinition::unlimited_influence` flag — the catalog's `influence_limit: null`, which nothing modelled before — and `deck::validator` skips its flat 15-influence check for such an identity; without it the every-faction starter decks could not validate. The catalog dump is untouched.
- [x] **Rewritten as `starter_identities_are_playable_and_blank`** (`cards/mod.rs`): playable, no triggers, no abilities, `min_deck_size` 30, `unlimited_influence` — read from the embedded playable cards rather than the catalog, which still says `is_playable: false` for its own printing.
- [x] **Both `SG_UNIMPLEMENTED` entries removed** (`cards/embedded.rs`); the gate's arithmetic reads 77 of 77.
  - Its count assertion is written as `sg_total - SG_UNIMPLEMENTED.len()`, so the arithmetic self-adjusts — but only if **both** identities actually land. Implementing one and excluding the other fails it, which is the desired behaviour.
  - **System Gateway then reads 77 of 77 rather than 75 of 77.** Update Phase 1 §7's headline count when this ships; it is the first set in the repo to reach genuinely complete coverage.

### 3. Tutorial decks, without polluting self-play (`netrunner_core`)

- [x] **Four decklists under `data/decks/`** from NSG's published Learn to Play guides: `the_syndicate_starter` (34, 14 agenda points), `the_catalyst_starter` (30), `the_syndicate_boosted` (44, 18 points), `the_catalyst_boosted` (40) — the first place the 32 starter and 11 booster cards are actually enumerated in the repo. Pinned by `starter_decks_match_the_published_lists`; the sample-deck tests filter to `Sample`, and `every_embedded_deck_is_labelled_a_sample` became `…_is_labelled_and_none_is_custom`.
- [x] **The category field and its `matchups()` filter — DONE, landed early with Phase 1 §1.** `DeckCategory` (`Sample` | `Starter` | `Boosted` | `Custom`) is on `decks::DeckFile`, `matchups()` filters to `Sample`, and the trap this bullet warned about — starter decks silently **training the policy network on tutorial decks** — is closed. `Starter` and `Boosted` already exist as variants; the four decklists just need authoring against them.
- [x] **`every_sample_deck_is_legal` validates the starter decks too** (it iterates every embedded deck); the threshold is carried by the record as `DeckCategory::match_rules()` — `Starter` → 6 points, everything else Standard. The `influence_limit: null` worry was right: see `unlimited_influence` in §2.

### 4. Deterministic stacked openings (`netrunner_core`)

- [x] **`GameState::setup_with(…, DeckOrder::{Shuffled, Fixed { corp, runner }})`**, with `setup` delegating to `Shuffled`. `Fixed` is authored **top-first** — `corp[0]` is the first card the Corp draws, the order a lesson author thinks in — and reversed onto `r_and_d`/`stack`, whose draws pop from the end.
  - [x] **`Fixed` is validated as a permutation of the expanded deck** after `validate_deck` — `RulesError::FixedOrderNotAPermutation { side }` for an extra card, a missing one, or the other side's deck — so the decklist stays authoritative.
  - Draw convention, for whoever implements it: `corp.r_and_d` / `runner.stack` are plain `Vec<CardId>` and draws `pop()` from the **end** — the last element is the top card.

### 5. Lesson content as data (`netrunner_core::tutorial`) — DONE (`feat/phase-175-lessons`, September 2026)

- [x] **Lessons live in `data/lessons/{corp,runner}/*.json`**, embedded by `build.rs` exactly like cards and decks (one directory per side, because sorted file order *is* the track order), parsed with `#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]` and pinned by `a_misspelled_lesson_key_is_rejected`. Never hardcoded in Rust.
- [x] **A lesson is `{ stacked deck order, scripted opening, ordered steps }`** — `tutorial::Lesson { order: StackedOrder, opening: Vec<PlayerAction>, opponent: Vec<ScriptedAction>, steps }`. Three decisions differ from the sketch above:
  - **The stacked order is a top-first *prefix*, not a permutation.** `DeckOrder::Fixed` still demands the whole deck, so `Lesson::deck_order` completes the prefix with the remaining copies in decklist order; a prefix card the deck has no further copy of is `TutorialError::OrderNotInDeck`. Listing 34 cards to pin six was the chore this bullet feared.
  - **The opening is `PlayerAction`s, not `ActionSpace` indices, and there is no `ScriptedDriver: PlayerDriver`** — that trait is gone. The learner's canned actions are submitted through the same `Seat::External` a human uses; the opponent's are a `netrunner_bots::ScriptedAgent`, a `BotAgent` that plays its script when legal (optionally gated on `turn`) and otherwise the quietest legal thing — keep hand, pass priority, click for credits, end turn — so a lesson's board is the same on every play-through.
  - **A step also carries `solution: Vec<PlayerAction>`**, the author's intended play. The driver never reads it; the lesson gate (§9) plays it to prove the step completable through its own `allow`.
- [x] **A step is `{ prose, hint, allow: ActionPredicate, advance_when: EventPredicate, solution }`.** `ActionPredicate::{Any, Kind, Card, Server, AnyOf}` matches by variant name (validated against `PlayerAction::VARIANT_NAMES`), with `Card` resolving an `InstallId` through the learner's own view so no lesson hardcodes an install handle; `EventPredicate::{Any, Kind, Card, Server, Side, AnyOf}` likewise, unvalidated by name because `GameEvent` has no name table — a misspelt kind is a step that never advances, which the gate catches. Events reach the lesson through the history-diff idiom (`entries()[mark..]`), which replaced `SinglePlayerSession::with_observer` in Phase 1.5; `HistoryEntry::events` is the same `Vec<GameEvent>`.
- [x] **`LessonProgress`** is the pure state machine (`observe(events) -> advanced`, `allowed(view) -> filtered legal actions`), shared by the TUI and the gate so they cannot disagree.
- Homed in `netrunner_core` because it depends only on `GameEvent`/`PlayerAction`/`ClientView` and must be reachable by any client, not just the TUI — the same reasoning that already puts `decks` there. It adds no dependencies. The *loop* is not here: `netrunner_session::lesson::LessonSession` pumps one `Session` (learner external, opponent scripted) and lives in the driver crate because the gates need it from a test and `netrunner_cli` has no library target.

### 6. Gating that does not break the client contract (`netrunner_cli`)

**This is the item most likely to be got wrong, so state the rule up front:**

> **A lesson step narrows `view.legal_actions`; it never widens them.** Presenting a subset is a UI affordance, like sorting — it cannot make an illegal action legal, so it does not violate AGENTS.md §3's ban on a client re-deriving legality. Gating logic must never call `apply_action` and must never reimplement a legality check.

- [x] **Gate on a predicate plus an escape hatch, never on a fixed action index.** `LessonStep::Prompt { view, allowed }` carries both the filtered list and the full view; `tui::LocalUiState::offered_actions` shows the subset, `a` toggles the full list, and a step whose filter matches nothing falls back to the full list with the coaching panel saying so. The doc comment on `LocalUiState::allowed` states the rule above verbatim, and `begin_gated_decision` debug-asserts the subset is one.
- **Two decisions are made for the learner, both documented on `LessonSession::step`:** an opening action is submitted on their behalf (that is what an opening is), and priority is passed for them when it is their only legal action or when the live step allows none of their legal actions but passing is one. Without the second, every lesson would have to spell out "pass priority" around each turn start's paid-ability window.

### 7. TUI work (`netrunner_cli`)

- [x] **A coaching/prose panel.** `layout.rs` had one builder by then, not two (the log-less one went with Phase 1.5); it is now `build_layout(area, with_coach)`, the panel taking 40% of the board row. It shows step *n* of *N*, the prose and hint, `explain_action` for the highlighted action, the escape-hatch state, and the run-phase strip during a run.
- [x] **A modal system.** `app::Modal { title, body, footer }` on `LocalUiState`; `prompt_human` routes keys to an open modal first (Enter/Space/Esc dismiss, `q` quits, everything else swallowed); `draw_modal` generalises the game-over popup, which now uses it. Intro before the first step, outro on `Complete`.
- [x] **`explain_action(action, registry, view) -> String`** — three arguments, like `describe_action` it mirrors — one sentence per variant on what the action does and costs, exhaustive over all 41 so a new action without an explanation is a compile error; `every_action_has_an_explanation_that_says_more_than_its_label` builds one of each.
- [x] **A run-phase strip** — `run_phase_strip`, Null Signal's six names with the current one lit, drawn under every active run (ordinary play included) and in the coaching panel. *Movement* is listed but never lit: the engine's `RunPhase` has no such decision point.
- [x] **`last_rejection` is rendered** — red, in the action list's title, on both paths. The local path used to `?`-propagate a rules rejection out of the TUI; `prompt_human` now shows it and keeps prompting, taking the submit as a closure so the same prompt serves `Session` and `LessonSession`.
- [x] **A `learn` subcommand** — `learn list | lesson <id> | track corp|runner | game corp|runner [--boosted]`. `track` plays a side's lessons in order and, on completing them all, hands off to `game`: the preset decks under `DeckCategory::match_rules()` against the heuristic — the only path that plays a starter deck at 6 points, since a saved deck carries no category.
- A `TestBackend` render test (`a_lesson_prompt_renders_with_coaching_modal_and_escape_hatch`) draws every widget the lesson path adds, since nothing else exercises the TUI.
- **Measurement:** the engine change is additive (a `GameEvent::variant_name` method and a new module), and random-vs-random (96 games, seed 1) is byte-identical to the foundations report. Heuristic-vs-heuristic differed — and differed again against *itself* on a second run of the same binary, which is how the run-to-run nondeterminism recorded under Phase 2 §5 was found. Both 256-seed sweeps ran clean.

### 8. The lesson tracks — DONE (`feat/phase-175-tracks`, September 2026)

Both sides, following NSG's own concept order; seven lessons each under `data/lessons/{corp,runner}/`, every one passing both §9 gates.

- [x] **Corp** — 01 clicks & the mandatory draw (and discarding to hand size) → 02 *Hedge Fund* → 03 servers & installing, rezzing *Nico Campaign* → 04 ice & rezzing: *Palisade* in front of the remote, rezzed as the scripted Runner approaches, subroutine ends the run → 05 advancing & scoring *Offworld Office* → 06 defending against a breaker: the scripted Runner installs *Cleaver*, boosts, breaks Palisade and steals the agenda — ice taxes, it does not wall → 07 *Urtica Cipher*, advanced twice and left unrezzed, 4 net damage on access → graduation.
- [x] **Runner** — 01 clicks & credits (*Sure Gamble*) → 02 *Cleaver* & MU → 03 a first run on Archives, walking initiation → approach → complete → access with the strip lit → 04 breaking *Palisade* on R&D (the scripted Corp installs turn 1, rezzes on approach) → 05 stealing *Offworld Office* off the top of R&D — R&D rather than HQ because a stacked deck pins the access where HQ's is random → 06 *Jailbreak* on R&D, two accesses, choosing the order → 07 *Tread Lightly*: the Corp rezzes for 6, Cleaver breaks, and the Runner jacks out at the server — the last point at which `JackOut` is legal, since `CompleteRun` is the commit.
- [x] **Graduation hands off to the starter game:** `learn track <side>` ends by launching `learn game <side>` — starter decks, 6 points to win, no gating, against the heuristic. The booster unlock (`--boosted`) plays the 44/40-card lists to 7; it introduces viruses, tags, meat damage and modal ice, the four things NSG withholds from a first game, and has no lessons of its own.
- **Authoring notes, learned from the gate:** remotes are numbered from `Remote(0)`; an `InstallId` counts from 1 in install order across both sides, so an opening that advances its own install can name it; a run is `InitiateRun` → `ContinueRun` (to `ServerApproached`) → `CompleteRun` (the commit, `RunSucceeded`) → `SelectCardToAccess`/`StealAgenda`/`PassAccessedCard` → `RunCompleted`; the mandatory draw is the sixth card of the Corp prefix, so a card the opening needs in hand goes in the first five. The lesson opening advanced a step that was meant to teach the same thing, which is why `LessonSession` now observes events only from the first hand-over, and why `Step::solution` became a preference list rather than a sequence: the number of priority-window and continue-run prompts between two intended plays is not something an author should count.

### 9. Test gates

- [x] **Every lesson is completable** — `every_lesson_is_completable_through_its_own_gates` (`crates/netrunner_session/tests/lessons.rs`) plays each step's `solution` through `LessonSession`, asserting each action is in the step's own `allowed` list and the step advances before its solution runs out; `Ended`/`Stalled` before `Complete` fails naming the lesson, step and legal actions. `every_embedded_lesson_parses_and_validates` covers what can be checked without playing.
- [x] **Every gated step offers at least one action** — the same test asserts `allowed` is non-empty at every prompt.
- [x] **Starter decks validate**, and the starter matchup plays to a result at 6 points — `the_starter_matchup_plays_to_a_result_at_six_points` (`netrunner_single_player`), eight seeds, every game ends and at least one by points.
- [x] **Replay determinism survives** the new `MatchRules` field — both bit-identical-replay assertions still hold, and a state recorded without the field deserializes to Standard.

---

## 🧠 Phase 2: Bot Intelligence, Replay & Gym Harness

### 1. Determinization — DONE
- [x] **State determinization in `netrunner_bots`.** `determinize` samples a plausible concrete `GameState` from a masked `ClientView` and is wired into `HeuristicAgent`, `MctsAgent`, and `PuctAgent`. `MctsAgent` resamples independently per parallel tree, making it genuine (if basic) Information Set MCTS.

### 2. Action Replay Protocol & Match Logging — mostly done
- [x] **Action/event recording and verified replay.** `MatchHistory`/`HistoryEntry` record `(turn_number, side, action, events)`; an integration test re-applies a recorded history and asserts a bit-identical final state.
- [x] **Emit it as JSON-Lines — DONE** (`feat/jsonl-history`, September 2026). `MatchHistory::write_jsonl(header, writer)` / `read_jsonl(reader)`: line 1 is a `MatchRecordHeader { seed, corp_deck, runner_deck, rules }` — the exact inputs of `GameState::setup_with`, so `header.setup(registry)` reproduces the opening position and replaying the entries reproduces the final one — then one `HistoryEntry` per line. Decks are recorded whole, not by id (a saved custom deck may not exist by that name tomorrow); there is no footer (the outcome is derivable, and a record cut short is a valid prefix); `MatchHistory` is `serde(transparent)`. `netrunner_cli --headless --record <dir>` writes `game_NNNNN.jsonl` per game on both the `Session` and `--index-path` branches. The record is the **raw** history — a replay viewer masks it per side when rendering, through the same `HistoryEntry::for_viewer` the live paths use. Pinned by `a_recorded_history_round_trips_through_jsonl_and_replays_to_the_same_state` and `record_writes_one_replayable_file_per_game`.
- [x] **TUI Replay Viewer — DONE** (`feat/replay-viewer`, September 2026). `netrunner_cli replay <game_NNNNN.jsonl> [--side corp|runner]`. Not a second engine path: `Replay::load` recomputes every position from the header through `apply_action` — the same replay the `Session` tests pin as bit-identical — and caches them, so stepping is indexing; a record the current engine no longer accepts fails on load naming the entry (`ReplayError::Diverged`), never showing a wrong board. The viewer picks a **chair**: every frame is the `ClientView` that seat would have received, the match log is that seat's `HistoryEntry::for_viewer` copy masked against each entry's own post-state, and the actions pane lists the step's masked events (their `Debug` rendering — a per-variant prose renderer is the narration work Phase 4 §1 leaves open). `s` swaps chairs. Renders through the same `draw_frame` as live play with one new `RenderableView::actions_title` hook. Pinned by a walk-every-position test, a Runner's-chair test asserting the Corp's installs read "Install a card into …", the diverged-record test, and a `TestBackend` render test.

### 3. Training Decks & Self-Play Data — DONE
Null Signal Games' published sample decklists are embedded as data (`data/decks/*.json`, compiled in by `build.rs`) and reachable via `netrunner_core::decks`: the seven System Gateway-only lists and the twenty-one System Gateway + *Elevation* lists Phase 1 §8 landed — all 28 on the published page. `matchups()` is the full Corp × Runner cross product of the `Sample` pool, 16 × 12 = 192; self-play rotates through it, local play selects with `--corp-deck`/`--runner-deck`, and the `--serve` daemon deals a matchup per match (Phase 4 §3). `every_sample_deck_is_legal` asserts every embedded deck validates; `every_sample_deck_matchup_finishes` plays the deck-covering schedule to a result.
- [x] **Real decks replaced the blank-filler fixture.** Self-play previously used a synthetic pair — an 18-blank-agenda Corp deck behind 6 ICE. All 5,000 recorded games ended in a Corp loss, so the value head trained against a constant and no System Gateway card ever appeared.
- [x] **Card-identity observation.** `OBS_SIZE = 990`: scalars plus five card-identity planes over a fixed 192-slot vocabulary ordered by `numeric_id`, so a future set appends rather than shifting slots out from under an exported model. The old 30-scalar encoding had no card-identity features at all — two different hands of the same size were literally the same input.
- [x] **A trained policy is playable.** `netrunner_cli --corp onnx --model <path>` (feature `onnx`) seats a trained network via `SinglePlayerSession`.
- [x] **Engine and search bugs found by running real decks** — none reachable with the old fixture: *Send a Message* offering already-rezzed ice as rez targets (fixed with `CardFilter::UnrezzedIce`); `ToggleCardSelection` keyed by `CardId` so two copies of a card could never both be selected, deadlocking any "choose N" over a zone with fewer than N *distinct* cards (*Carnivore* against two identical grip cards) — fixed then by cycling through copy counts, and since superseded: the action carries a zone *position*, so two copies are simply two entries and the cycling is gone (see Phase 1 §3's `InstallId` entry); `determinize` resampling the stack out from under a parked card-selection; and greedy self-play action selection being a perfect two-cycle when toggling one card on and off (now falls back to sampling after `MAX_GREEDY_REPEATS`).
- Combined effect on a 12-game smoke run: games hitting the 10,000-step budget went **8 of 12 → 0 of 12**, median game length **6,674 → 171 steps**, and outcomes went from a single constant value to a real win/loss split.

**Running a training loop** (self-play → PyTorch → ONNX → promote, repeat):
```bash
source scripts/venv/bin/activate    # torch + onnx already installed
python3 scripts/run_iteration_loop.py --iterations 50 --games-per-iter 100 --simulations 200
```
Each iteration ends with an **arena**: the new checkpoint plays `--arena-games` (48) against the incumbent — the uniform search until something has been promoted — from both chairs, and is promoted only at `--promote-threshold` (0.55, wins plus half the stalls) or better. Verdicts accumulate in `checkpoints/promotions.log`; a refused checkpoint is kept as `rejected_iter_NNN.onnx`. `--skip-arena` is the old unconditional behaviour. The arena is `netrunner_selfplay --arena-candidate <onnx> [--arena-incumbent <onnx>] -n 48 -s 64`, usable on its own.
Then measure it the way self-play plays it — PUCT searching over the network (`puct-onnx`), against the uniform search it replaced:
```bash
cargo run --release -p netrunner_cli --features onnx -- --headless --all-matchups --games 24 --seed 1 \
  --corp puct-onnx --runner puct --model checkpoints/latest_policy.onnx --report target/coverage/<run>-trained-corp.json
```
Or play it:
```bash
cargo run -p netrunner_cli --features onnx -- --runner human --corp onnx \
  --corp-deck discretion_advised --runner-deck stolen_goods
```

### 4. Gym & Self-Play Harness (`netrunner_gym`)
- [x] **Fog-of-war-respecting observations.** `encode_observation` builds a `ClientView` internally and encodes only that — training already runs under masking. (`env.rs` still holds a raw `GameState` field for stepping; cosmetic, and subsumed by Phase 1.5.)
- [x] **Vectorized feature extraction.** Dense `OBS_SIZE`-length observations plus action masks over the fixed `ActionSpace`, exposed to Python via PyO3.
- [x] **Headless benchmark suite — DONE** (`feat/rating-and-bench`, 2 September 2026). `netrunner_cli bench --bots random,heuristic,puct --games 12 --seed 1 --simulations 32 [--threads N] [--report x.json] [--ratings book.json] [--label sha]`: every *ordered* pairing of the kinds — a kind against itself included, since that pairing is what says which chair is the stronger one for a given bot — plays `--games` matches on a rayon pool, rotating the twelve sample matchups, and the results are rated on `netrunner_rating`'s bot-benchmark track (Phase 3 §2). Plays through `netrunner_session::Session` like every other driver.
  - **Rated in play order, after all games finish**, so the same arguments give the same ladder however many threads ran it (a Glicko-2 update is order-dependent) — pinned by `a_bench_rates_every_seating_and_reproduces_from_its_seed`, which runs the same seed on two threads and then one. A stall is counted in the pairing's tally and not rated; the book has no "nobody won" outcome on purpose. Search kinds carry their budget in the participant id (`puct@32`), and `--label` appends a suffix so two versions of one bot rate as different participants in a saved book.
  - **Why a ladder and not a win rate:** the arena scores a candidate against one incumbent with no error bar. `--bots puct,puct-onnx --model X` is the arena with the heuristic and random bots on the same table and an interval on every number.
  - **First ladder** (108 games in 13.9 s on 20 threads, seed 1, 32 simulations, no stalls): heuristic 1905 ± 154 / 1904 ± 154 (Corp / Runner, 26-10 and 24-12), `puct@32` 1838 ± 145 / 1759 ± 150, random 1219 ± 206 / 1384 ± 184. Uniform PUCT at 32 simulations loses to the heuristic 4-8 as Runner and draws 6-6 as Corp, which matches Phase 2 §5's 48-game figure (7+3 wins of 48 against the heuristic) and is now a number with an interval. `target/coverage/bench-random-heuristic-puct.json`.

*Elevation* is being implemented deck by deck — Phase 1 §8 — so the 21 System Gateway + Elevation decklists on the same NSG page join the pool as their cards land.

### 5. Bot and Training-Data Quality

Both items below were surfaced by Phase 1 §3's memory-cost fix, which let a bot
Runner install a program for the first time. Neither is a bug in the engine —
they are consequences of one having existed.

- [x] **`HeuristicAgent` almost never installs a program, and the arithmetic says why — DONE, see the Rules Audit §0.5.** The evaluator now credits *being able to break each ICE subtype* rather than owning a card, exactly the "honest fix" this entry asked for, plus the run and advancement terms without which a one-ply agent on either side never reaches an encounter. Original analysis kept below.** Measured immediately after the fix: **3 installs across 24 heuristic-vs-heuristic games**, up from a structural 0. `eval::evaluate_state` scores `+1.0` per rig card (`BOARD_PRESENCE_WEIGHT`), `+0.5` per free memory unit (`MEMORY_WEIGHT`) and `+0.4` per credit (`OWN_CREDIT_WEIGHT`), so installing a 1-MU program of printed cost *C* moves the score by **`0.5 − 0.4C`** — against a flat **`+0.4`** for `GainCreditClick`, which is legal on essentially every click. Installing therefore wins only when `C = 0`: *Marjanah* is the single program in the pool that clears the bar, plus a cost-1 program behind a first-install discount (DZMZ Optimizer, Kate-style identities).
  - **The weights are defensible individually and wrong together.** A rig card is worth 2.5 credits and a free MU is worth 1.25, which is not obviously mispriced; the problem is that a breaker's *value* is entirely in the runs it enables, and a one-ply greedy evaluator cannot see a run it has not taken. Re-tuning `BOARD_PRESENCE_WEIGHT` upward would paper over that — the honest fixes are a deeper search (`MctsAgent`/`PuctAgent` already look further) or an evaluation term that credits *being able to break ICE* rather than *owning a card*.
  - **Distinct from Phase 3 §1's "Biased Evaluation Function"**, which is about giving bots deliberate personalities. This is about the default evaluator being unable to value an icebreaker at all.
  - Worth re-measuring against `MctsAgent` and `PuctAgent` before changing any weight: the 3-in-24 figure is `HeuristicAgent`'s, and the search agents may already clear the bar without help.
