One new `Weights` term at 2.0, read in two places so the two halves of one mechanic share a number: an advancement token *past* a Dividends agenda's requirement is worth the `dividends` counters it will become (`corp_install_value`, replacing the flat zero `advancement_tokens.min(required)` gave it), and `ScoredAgenda::agenda_counters` is worth the same after the agenda is scored (`scored_agenda_counters`). Both halves are load-bearing: the second is what stops the value evaporating at the moment it pays off, since a search comparing "score now" with "advance once more, then score" sees the install gone either way.
  - **Sized against the decision it has to win, not tuned to a result.** Scoring costs no click (`engine::score_agenda`), so both lines end with the agenda scored and the second is one click and one credit dearer: a counter has to beat a credit-click plus the credit spent (0.8) and stay far under a point of agenda (20.0), so a Dividends agenda never looks better held than taken. 2.0 is what a counter buys — *Sericulture Expansion* spends one for two advancement tokens (+3.0), *Off the Books* for a searched card installed free.
  - **It works, and the budget it needs is the finding.** On the isolated position — a 3/2 at its requirement, three clicks, eight credits — the term moves the decision at **512 search iterations and not at 128**, and never moves it at all on an agenda with no `dividends` (deep search banks a credit there instead, which is also sound: scoring can wait until the turn ends). Three tests pin exactly that, including the budget, because the alternative was raising the weight until 32 iterations found it — which would mean valuing a counter above a point of agenda, and would be wrong.
  - **Measured, and the honest result is "no change in play".** Random-vs-random (192 games, seed 1) is **byte-identical** to `elev-10-random-random.json` — the random bot has no evaluator, so the only acceptable delta was none, and it is the control that says the report pipeline did not move under us. Heuristic-vs-heuristic and `puct@32` (192 games each) still fire the dividend triggers **zero** times, with deltas everywhere else inside the seed-spread band (`AdvanceCard` 1,828 → 1,816 heuristic, 3,270 → 3,250 puct; `AgendaScored` 374 → 372 and 422 → 424). A `puct@512` run over just the six Corp decks that play these agendas — 70 of its 72 games; the other two were still short of the step budget after the rest had finished and were stopped, since at 512 iterations a *stalling* game is effectively unbounded — scored them 48 times and fired the trigger zero times too. `target/coverage/dividends-{random-random,heuristic-heuristic,puct-puct,puct512-sample}.json`.
  - **Why the isolated position and the real ones disagree, stated rather than guessed at:** PUCT expands by leaf value, and the "advance" child is worth ~2 against the "score" child's ~40, so it is explored last; in a constructed position with five legal actions 512 iterations still reach past it, and in a real one with a full board they do not. That is a property of a search guided by *this* evaluator, and no setting of this weight fixes it — the honest next lever is a policy whose priors are not derived from the evaluator (the ONNX head, Phase 2 §5), or a two-ply-aware rule, which `personality.rs` deliberately forbids ("nothing here adds a rule, a card preference by name, or a script").
  - **What the branch is worth, then**: the term is *necessary* — with the zero in place no search budget and no trained policy could ever prefer the play, because the payoff was defined as nothing — and it is now the search, not the valuation, that is the barrier. Recorded as a capability with a stated budget rather than claimed as a behaviour change. Both 256-seed sweeps clean, 1,189 tests green, clippy silent.
- [x] **The ambush lever — the term `Trap` was recorded as missing — DONE** (`feat/eval-ambush-lever`, 3 September 2026). This section's `Trap` entry closed with "a real trap archetype needs a lever that recognises an ambush by its text (an `OnAccess` `DealDamage`, an advanceable non-agenda), which is card-inspection the evaluator does not do yet". It does now, and the first half of it turned out to be a plain bug in the balanced evaluator.
  - **An ambush's advancement tokens were worth exactly zero.** *Clearinghouse* and *Urtica Cipher* carry `advancement_requirement: Some(0)`, so `advancement_tokens.min(required)` counted every token as nothing — while each card's damage **is** that token count (`DealDamageAmount(_, HostedAdvancementTokens)`). Clearinghouse's turn-start trigger fired 247 times in a 192-game heuristic report and dealt no damage at all: the Corp was offered "deal 0 meat and trash this" every turn and correctly declined, forever. The same shape as the Dividends zero one entry above, but **without its horizon problem** — no competing action banks the same value one ply later, so a one-ply Corp can see it. `ambush_advancement_weight` 1.0 (beats the 0.8 a click and a credit cost, never rivals an agenda token) with `ambush_advancement_cap` 3, because unlike an agenda there is no requirement to stop at and an uncapped weight above the credit-click would have the Corp advancing one forever.
  - **Recognised by text, never by name.** `damage_grows_with_advancement` and `punishes_access_with_damage` walk the card's declared effects through `Effect::for_each_effect` — triggers, abilities and `interactive_on_access` — so the next card with the same shape is valued for free and `personality.rs`'s rule holds ("nothing here adds a rule, a card preference by name, or a script"). In today's pool the first matches *Clearinghouse* and *Urtica Cipher*, the second *Snare!*, *Byte!* and *Urtica Cipher*.
  - **Measured across five seeds, because one would only have been attribution.** Heuristic-vs-heuristic, 192 games, seeds 1–5, Corp flatline wins **before 12/16/20/16/23 → after 26/31/31/31/31**: up on every seed, and the two ranges do not overlap, which is what clearing the seed-spread band looks like. Total Corp wins up on every seed too — 83→89, 82→91, 89→94, 78→85, 75→80 — bought by trading scored agendas for kills (Corp agenda wins 71→63 at seed 1). Random-vs-random is **byte-identical** to `elev-10-random-random.json`, the control. `target/coverage/band-{before,after}-hh-seed{1..5}.json`.
  - **`Trap` is no longer broken for the reason recorded, and still does not win.** With `ambush_weight` 1.0 (a face-down ambush is worth more than a plain install, *to this profile only* — zero by default, like `opponent_grip_weight`, since the balanced Corp does not play for the flatline) and its tokens at 1.4 capped at 4: trap-Corp vs balanced-Runner goes **73 → 84 Corp wins, flatlines 13 → 33**. It now out-kills the balanced Corp (33 to 26) — the mechanism the profile was always asking for. But it still loses to balanced overall (84 to 89), because its other biases give up more scoring than the damage buys (51 agenda wins to balanced's 63). **The missing capability is closed; what is left is profile tuning, which is a different kind of open item.**
  - Counter-intuitive, and recorded so it is not mistaken for a regression later: total `DamageTaken` *falls* (489 → 392) while flatlines double. Clearinghouse trashes itself when it fires, so a Corp that advances it holds it and fires once for real damage instead of declining a 0-damage offer every turn — 247 turn-start firings become 167, and games end sooner (112,829 steps → 107,666). Fewer, larger, lethal.
  - Pinned by `an_ambush_is_recognised_by_its_text_and_a_plain_damage_card_is_not` (a growing ambush, a flat one, damage on the wrong trigger, and an agenda), `advancing_an_ambush_is_worth_more_than_the_click_it_costs_and_stops_at_the_cap`, and `a_face_down_ambush_is_worth_more_only_to_a_corp_that_plays_for_damage` (zero for balanced, and nothing once rezzed). Both 256-seed sweeps clean, 1,192 tests green, clippy silent.
- [x] **`Trap` retuned until it beats balanced — DONE** (`feat/trap-profile-tuning`, 3 September 2026). The lever existed; the profile around it was the problem. **The whole profile is now three terms, all card-inspecting, and nothing else** — `ambush_weight` 3.0, `ambush_advancement_weight` 2.8, `ambush_advancement_cap` 7, `..base`. Trap-Corp vs balanced-Runner, 192 games at each of five seeds: **460 Corp wins against balanced's 439, ahead on all five seeds** (91/97/96/93/83 against 89/91/94/85/80), where it started this branch at 366 and started the day at 331.
  - **Every generic knob it carried was costing it.** The first cut was six of them — hoard credits (`own_credit_weight` 0.5), install more (1.3), hold a bigger hand (`hq_floor` 4), and protect agendas *less* (0.3) — around a wish that the Runner's hand be thin. The mechanical tell was `IceRezzed` 841 against balanced's 1375: its agendas sat naked and it lost them, 205 agenda wins to balanced's ~290. Restoring the two most suspicious knobs bought nothing (361). **Deleting all of them at once and keeping only the ambush terms went straight to 443**, and turning those terms up took it to 460.
  - **Attributed, not just tuned.** `ambush_advancement_weight` is the engine: alone it is worth +14 over balanced and wins on all five seeds. `ambush_weight` **alone is worth −2** — it loses to balanced — and only +7 on top of the other. A face-down ambush is a threat *because it is loaded*; valuing the card without valuing what fills it buys nothing. Past these values it turns over (a 5.0/4.0/10 profile drops back to 457) as the clicks stop being worth the damage.
  - **`opponent_grip_weight` is gone from the profile, and that is a measurement.** It is the term the archetype was *named* for, and it does nothing: 443 against 444 at the old ambush settings, **460 against 460 at these**, with `DamageTaken` 356 against 353 and identical trigger counts. The reason is that the Corp never chooses to deal this damage — the Runner walks into *Urtica Cipher* on access. No profile uses the term now; it should either find a user or be deleted, and that is a standing item rather than something to leave quietly dead.
  - **On the benchmark ladder** (4 profiles, 12 games a pairing, seed 1, `target/coverage/trap-tuned-bench.json`): trap Corp **1569 ± 140** (25-23) over balanced Corp **1446 ± 153** (15-33), and trap beats balanced head-to-head 8-4 as Corp. Glacier still tops the Corp column at 1510 and the ladder overall at 1641 on its Runner side; twelve games a pairing is ±140 of deviation, so the five-seed 192-game runs above are the load-bearing measurement and the ladder is corroboration.
  - Balanced-vs-balanced is **byte-identical** to the previous report — only the `Trap` arm of `Personality::weights` changed — and `profiles_move_the_terms_their_doc_comments_name_in_the_direction_they_say` now asserts the profile equals `Weights::default()` once its three ambush terms are put back, so a future knob has to justify itself. Both 256-seed sweeps clean, 1,192 tests green, clippy silent.

- [x] **The Corp accepted no paid choice at all, and the reason was the prompt behind it — DONE** (`feat/eval-paid-choice`, 3 September 2026). The `puct@32` baseline above recorded "paid choices accepted / declined — random 124/213, heuristic 65/451, `puct@32` 33/672" and called the decline-by-reflex "a separable second term, worth its own before/after". Attribution came off the five `band-after-hh-seed*` reports without a new run: the Runner accepts 43% and is not reflexive at all, while the **Corp accepted 0 of 332 at every one of the five seeds**, and four cards are all of it — *Anoetic Void* (~120 offers), *AU Co.* (~110), *PT Untaian* (~65), *Phật Gioan Baotixita* (~20).
  - **A zero out of 332 is an arithmetic, not a preference, and it is the asymmetry between the two answers.** Both `AcceptPendingPaidChoice` and `DeclinePendingPaidChoice` clear the paid choice, so `UNRESOLVED_DECISION_WEIGHT` cancels between them — except that every one of these accepts hands the payer a *further* `PromptChooseCards` while every decline resolves to `Sequence []`. PT Untaian's "pay 1[c], put an advancement token on an installed card" therefore scored −0.4 − 2.0 = **−2.4 against declining's 0.0**, with the +1.5 token one ply the other side of the prompt. Lowering the penalty does not reach it: at 1.0 the accept is still −1.4 against 0.0, because the fruit is not counted at all.
  - **`pending_decision_upside_weight` 1.5, credited beside a lower bound on what resolving the prompt will deliver** (`eval::pending_decision_upside`). Writing `P` for the penalty, `C` for the accept cost and `F` for what resolving really adds: accepting wins when `upside + this > P + C`, and resolving still beats sitting in the prompt when `F + P > upside + this`. Because the upside is a **bound and not an estimate**, the second holds for every card whenever this constant is below `P` — safe by construction rather than by measurement, which is the entire reason the bound is a bound. A flat bonus big enough to flip an accept on its own would have to exceed `P` and would re-create the wander.
  - **The bound is over the *worst* resolution the decision allows, because the evaluator cannot see which card a toggle will pick** — `ToggleCardSelection` changes `selected`, which nothing in `evaluate_state_with` scores. An advancement token is therefore priced at its marginal `corp_install_value` on the least rewarding eligible target, read through `card_matches_filter` (the definition-level half of the filter, so the cards walked are a *superset* of the eligible ones — which is what keeps the minimum a lower bound); a card arriving in HQ or the grip at the exact shortfall delta the live term would pay, `rd_draw_reserve` guard included. Recognised by shape, never by card name, like the ambush lever.
  - **A catch-all cost a 10,000-action stall, and that is why the recogniser is a whitelist.** With unpriced effects contributing zero to a sum rather than poisoning it, *Touch-ups* — advance a card by two (priced, +3.0) and *then* park a `PresentChoice` (unpriced, but −`UNRESOLVED_DECISION_WEIGHT` the moment it lands) — made confirming the selection 1.5 *worse* than sitting in it, and the heuristic Corp toggled position 4 on and off for the full budget (view sweep, seed 7, gimbatul vs professional_opportunities; found by the sweep, before merge). A continuation that parks a further decision has to be unpriceable, and a whitelist gets that right for every future `Effect` variant without anyone remembering to classify it. Pinned by `a_continuation_that_parks_a_further_decision_is_not_priced_at_all`.
  - **Measured, 192 games at each of five seeds. Corp accepts 0 → 12 / 12 / 11 / 12 / 15**, from ~20% of PT Untaian's own offers; *Seamless Launch*, never played in 192 games at any seed, is played once at seeds 4 and 5 — the same blindness, since its payoff also sits behind a prompt. **Everything else is inside the seed-spread band and is reported as such**: steps ±1%, `CardAdvanced` 1,740 → 1,731 / 1,653 → 1,652 / 1,753 → 1,751 / 1,591 → 1,602 / 1,635 → 1,632, `AgendaScored` within 2 at every seed, the outcome split within 4 games. Random-vs-random (192 games, seed 1) is **byte-identical** to `elev-10-random-random.json`, the control. `target/coverage/paidchoice-{after-hh-seed{1..5},random-random}.json`.
  - **A capability, not a strength gain** — the same honest shape as the Dividends entry above. The other three cards are each blocked on a *different* missing term, and the arithmetic for each is now on record: **Anoetic Void** (pay 2[c], discard 2 from HQ, end the run) needs the Corp to value a run in progress at all — it has no counterpart to `ACTIVE_RUN_WEIGHT`, so the run it ends is worth zero; **AU Co.** chains a prompt into a prompt and is unpriceable by the rule above; **Phật Gioan** spends counters for net damage, worth nothing to a Corp whose `opponent_grip_weight` is 0.0. That last one is the damage/pressure lever this section already names twice (the *Measured Response* reading, and the `Trap` retune's standing "`opponent_grip_weight` should either find a user or be deleted"), and it is the next branch.
  - Also fixed in passing: `HQ_FLOOR`'s doc comment said "Two, not the Runner's three" over a constant of 3 — stale since the branch that measured it at 3 (Rules Audit §3's failure mode). Both 256-seed sweeps clean, 1,198 tests green, clippy silent.

### 2. Rating & Elo Engine — DONE
- [x] **`netrunner_rating` — Glicko-2, three tracks, a rating per role — DONE** (`feat/rating-and-bench`, 2 September 2026). A new pure crate (deps: `serde`, `serde_json`; no engine, no I/O): `Glicko2::update` is Glickman's 2013 paper line for line and reproduces his worked example (1500 ± 200 → 1464.06 ± 151.52, σ 0.05999), and `RatingBook` holds one `Standing` — a Corp `RoleRecord` and a Runner one, each a `Rating { rating, deviation, volatility }` plus W-L-D — per `Track` (`HumanVsHuman`, `HumanVsBot`, `BotBenchmark`) per participant, serializable whole into one sorted JSON file.
  - **Glicko-2 rather than Elo** because every consumer has few games per participant, and a deviation is what lets a ladder say `1612 ± 120` honestly and lets a newcomer move fast without a hand-tuned K-factor. **A rating period of one game**: the Corp's Corp rating is updated against the Runner's Runner rating as both stood before the game, and vice versa, so the order of the two updates cannot matter and a participant may play itself (a bot benchmarked against its own kind is two ratings). Glickman suggests 10-15 games a period; per-game is what online play does anyway, and the cost is volatility reacting a little faster.
  - **Tracks never mix**, and the roles never mix: a human's evenings against the heuristic do not touch their human-vs-human standing, and a Corp rating is never updated from a Runner result. `overall()` is the mean of the two roles, for sorting a ladder only — nothing is ever updated from it.
  - **Persistence is the consumer's**, the split `netrunner_core::decks` keeps with `deck_store`: the CLI's `bench --ratings` loads and saves a book; the daemon's `--ratings-file` loads at bind and rewrites (temp file plus rename) after every rated match, under the registry lock. Nothing is rated without the flag, so every existing test's daemon is stateless. A human is rated under its `player_name`; a bot under `bot:heuristic`/`bot:mcts`, prefixed so no human name collides with it. The track follows the daemon: human-vs-bot under a bot opponent, human-vs-human under `--bot-runner none`.
  - **A forfeit is a loss.** `MatchSession::run_with_outcome` returns the verdict the seats were sent — surrender, disconnect and clock included, none of which the final `GameState` records (it never reached `GameOver`) — and a stall (`None`) goes unrated. Pinned over sockets by `a_surrender_against_the_bot_is_rated_on_the_human_vs_bot_track` and `human_matches_are_rated_on_their_own_track_and_the_book_survives_a_restart` (the daemon restarted on the same file, standings accumulating), and by eleven unit tests in the crate.
  - **The idle seat is read too — DONE** (`fix/idle-seat-surrender`, 2 September 2026). Found while testing the human track: `MatchSession::await_seat` read only the *awaited* seat's channel, so the non-acting seat's `Surrender` — and any out-of-turn `SubmitAction`, which was then applied when its turn came if it happened to be legal — waited until that seat was next awaited. A third `select!` arm now reads the idle seat: a `Surrender` ends the match at once (`SeatEvent::Surrendered`), and a `SubmitAction` is **checked against that seat's own `legal_actions_for` slice** — the one its view offered — then submitted, applied if the engine accepts it (`RezIce` during the Runner's approach is the Corp's whatever the window's priority) and answered with `ActionRejected` otherwise. That check is the one place the host re-derives legality, and the reason is on `SeatEvent::IdleActionApplied`: a `PlayerAction` carries no submitter, and the first version of this arm — "try it against the engine, its guards are the only authority" — applied the idle Runner's `KeepHand` as the Corp's mulligan. The awaited seat's path is unchanged and still re-derives nothing (AGENTS.md's Session Rule). **The reconnect grace clock moved onto the session** (`grace_deadline`, keyed by the seat it is for) for the reason `decision_deadline` already lived there: an applied idle action re-enters `await_seat`, and a clock local to one call would restart then, letting an opponent keep a vanished player's seat alive by acting. Pinned by `the_idle_seat_can_surrender_without_waiting_for_its_turn`, `an_out_of_turn_action_is_refused_at_once_and_not_replayed_later` and `the_opponents_activity_does_not_restart_a_detached_seats_grace_clock` (`start_paused`); the human-track socket test now surrenders from the idle seat.
  - Not built: the client is not told its new rating (`GameEnded` goes out before the session task rates the match; a `ListRatings`/`Rated` message is the obvious shape when a client wants to show it), and there is no rating decay for inactivity beyond what an idle Glicko-2 period would give if someone applied one.

## 🌐 Phase 4: Network Resilience & Server Infrastructure

### 1. Event Stream to Clients — DONE
- [x] **Send `GameEvent`s to clients at all.** Done since Phase 1.5 and never ticked here: `ServerMessage::ActionLog` carries one `HistoryEntry` — the action and its full `Vec<GameEvent>` — after every `StateUpdate`, and `App::action_log` renders it. This entry sat open for a release while the bullet below described a hazard that was already live.
- [x] **Then mask them per viewer — DONE** (`fix/masked-action-log`, September 2026). `MatchSession::broadcast_applied` sent the *same* raw entry to both seats, so the Runner read the Corp's `InstallCard { card_id }` and `CardInstalled` for a card its own `StateUpdate` had just rendered as `card: None`, the Corp read which HQ/R&D card the Runner had `SelectCardToAccess`ed or `PassAccessedCard`ed, and `CardAdvanced`/`CountersAdded`/`IceSwapped`/facedown `CardTrashed`/`CardDiscarded` all named unrezzed or facedown Corp cards. The local TUI had the same hole from the other direction: `describe_action` rendered the bot Corp's installs by title to a human Runner. Now:
  - **`rules::masking::mask_action_for_player(action, actor, viewer) -> PublicAction`** — `Visible(PlayerAction)` or `Concealed(ConcealedAction)`, a small typed enum of exactly the six card-naming shapes (`InstallCard { zone, slot }`, `DiscardCard`, the four access actions). A viewer always sees their own actions whole. A concealed action keeps its shape because "the Corp installed a card into Remote 0" is information the Runner *must* have; an `Option<CardId>` on `PlayerAction` itself was rejected as a view concern on a type with an `ActionSpace` layout and forty-one-arm exhaustive matches.
  - **`rules::masking::mask_event_for_player(event, state, viewer) -> Option<GameEvent>`** — drops an event whose information *is* a concealed identity (nothing renders events yet, and the `StateUpdate` before it carries the structural change); strikes the card out in place for `TraceInitiated` and `VirusCountersPurged`, the two where it can. **Concealment is read from the post-action state, conservatively**: a Corp card is off-limits to the Runner while any installed copy is unrezzed or any Archives copy is facedown — the same facts the view reads, so log and view cannot disagree, and no event grew a field or changed an emit site. It errs toward hiding (a rezzed Nico's counters are concealed while a second Nico sits unrezzed; a derezzed card the Runner *had* seen is concealed because the view has no "seen before"), which is the accepted direction. Both functions are exhaustive with no catch-all arm, so a new card-bearing variant must be classified before it compiles.
  - **`netrunner_session::PublicHistoryEntry`** is what crosses the boundary: `HistoryEntry::for_viewer(state, side)`, reached only through **`Session::last_entry_for(side)`** — valid right after the `Applied`/`submit` that produced it, and deliberately with no batch form, since masking an old entry against a later state is the mistake that would reveal a card trashed since. The server sends each seat its own copy; the TUI's `drive_local` now pumps `step` one action at a time instead of `run` for the same reason (and, as a side effect, logs the human's own actions, which the mark-after-`submit` idiom had silently omitted); `LessonSession` keeps the learner's copy as it pumps and hands it over via `drain_log`. `describe_public_action` renders a `Concealed` shape without consulting the registry.
  - **The gate is the sweep's own**: `no_client_view_or_log_entry_ever_names_a_card_it_conceals` (renamed) checks, after every action of every seed, each seat's `PublicHistoryEntry` against a concealed set widened past hidden installs to the opponent's HQ, R&D, facedown Archives, grip and stack. It was non-vacuous on its first run: it caught the derez case above and a false positive (the Runner passing on an HQ card it had just seen) that fixed the ground truth, not the mask. Its limit is recorded on the test: a card in the viewer's *own* hidden zone is invisible to it, so the HQ/R&D-access rules are pinned by `masking`'s unit tests. Twelve of those, one per rule; `the_action_log_never_names_the_other_sides_hidden_cards` pins the server path end to end.
  - **Measured (96 games, seed 1):** random-vs-random byte-identical to `trk-random-random.json` — the mask is read-only over the engine's events, so the only acceptable delta was none. Both 256-seed sweeps clean.
  - Still open by design: the log names cards by `CardId` as the engine emits them; an `InstallId` on `CardInstalled`/`CardAdvanced`/`IceSwapped` would let a masked event keep *which* install rather than being dropped, and is the thing to add when a client wants to narrate events (the replay viewer, Phase 2 §2). A spectator's log (§3) has exactly the same limitation, since it is the Runner's mask on Corp events and the Corp's on the Runner's.

### 2. Reconnection & Session Recovery — DONE
- [x] **Session Token Handshake — DONE** (`feat/reconnect-handshake`, September 2026). `ServerMessage::MatchJoined` now carries a per-*seat* `session_token` (a v4 `Uuid`; the Corp's token can never reseat the Runner), and a new `ClientMessage::Resume { session_token }` presents it on a fresh socket. The server answers `MatchJoined` again (same side, same token) followed by a fresh `StateUpdate` — the resync is the full masked snapshot the design already sends after every action, so nothing new crosses the boundary — or `ServerMessage::ResumeRejected { reason }` when no live match holds the token. Three decisions, each recorded on the type or constant:
  - **The seat outlives its channel pair.** `MatchSession`'s `ChannelSeat` gains a `detached` flag, set when either `mpsc` half is seen closed; a `ReattachHandle` (cloneable, addressed by `Side`) hands the seat a new pair while `run` is in progress, and the session resends that seat its view immediately — *including while the other side is being awaited*, which is why `await_seat` is a `select!` over the awaited seat's `rx`, the reattach channel and the deadline rather than a bare `recv`. The alternative, a relay task swapping sockets under a fixed channel, was rejected because only the session holds the state a resync needs. **Newest connection wins**: reattaching a still-attached seat drops the old halves, and `net::bridge_websocket` now closes its socket when the session lets go (a `Close` frame, not a silent stop) and tears down its other half instead of leaving a detached task holding the socket until the match ends.
  - **The grace clock (`DEFAULT_RECONNECT_GRACE`, 30 s; `--reconnect-grace-secs`) starts when the seat is *needed*, not when it dropped**, and a reattach resets it. A Corp that dropped during a slow Runner turn has cost nobody anything; forfeiting it because the *other* player took ten minutes would be a strange rule. When it does run out the pump reports `GameEndReason::Disconnected` to the other side — a transport outcome like `Surrender`, never produced by `classify_end_reason`, and the final `GameState` is not `GameOver`.
  - **Tokens are the transport's business, not the pump's.** `netrunner_server::serve` (moved out of `main.rs` so a test can bind port 0 and drive real sockets) keeps a `HashMap<Uuid, SeatTicket { match_id, side, handle }>`; `start_match` is the one place that builds the session, registers a ticket per channel seat, sends `MatchJoined`, and removes the tickets when `run` returns — so the bot and lobby paths cannot disagree on the order, and a resume after the game ended (including one forfeited by this seat's absence) is refused rather than answered with a `MatchJoined` for a match that no longer exists.
  - **Client:** `netrunner_cli::remote::Reconnector` makes one bounded attempt per render tick (`block_in_place` on the multi-threaded runtime, 3 s per attempt, 1 s apart, 60 s total — longer than the server's grace on purpose, so a forfeited seat learns its fate from `ResumeRejected` rather than giving up first), while the board keeps drawing the last view under a red notice and `q` still quits. `App` distinguishes `TryRecvError::Disconnected` from `Empty` (they were treated alike, so a dropped socket looked like a very quiet opponent), holds submissions while down, and notes the log gap on reconnect: `ActionLog` entries that fell on the floor are not replayed, because an entry is masked against the state its action produced and never a later one (`Session::last_entry_for`), and the resync `StateUpdate` carries every structural consequence anyway.
  - **Fixed in passing:** the server bridge treated any non-text frame — a ping — as the end of the stream. Harmless before; a forfeit now.
  - Pinned by four channel-level tests in `match_session` (fresh view on reattach; non-acting seat reattaches while the other is awaited; forfeit at the deadline under `start_paused`; the clock resets on reattach), four socket-level tests in `tests/reconnect.rs` (resume, unknown token, resume after forfeit, newest-connection-wins closes the displaced socket), and the CLI's `Reconnector` driven against a real ephemeral-port server. Random-vs-random is untouched — no engine or bot code changed.
  - Both of the gaps this left — the lobby holding a `PendingHuman` whose socket was already dead, so the next human paired with a ghost, and no `Resume` during the lobby wait — are closed by §3's match registry below.

### 3. Multi-Match Daemon & Matchmaking

Three branches, each mergeable on its own: the registry and lobby (concurrent matches as a first-class thing), a turn clock, and spectators. The order is forced — the last two both edit `MatchSession::await_seat`'s `select!` and `ServeOptions`, so they are sequential rather than parallel.

- [x] **Match registry, lobby queue, rooms, cap and per-match seed — DONE** (`feat/match-registry`, 2 September 2026). `serve.rs` used to hold a one-element lobby (`Option<PendingHuman>`) and a token map, with a `match_id` minted per spawn and stored nowhere but each ticket; bot matches ran concurrently by accident (every connection spawned its own `session.run()`) and human matches were bounded by the one slot. Now:
  - **One `Registry` under one `std` mutex** — running matches (names, start time, `ReattachHandle`), seat tickets, the lobby queue and the match counter. Separate locks were rejected because the operations that matter are compound: a pairing must sweep the lobby, check the cap, claim a match index and register two tickets as one step, or two simultaneous pairings both pass the cap. Nothing awaits under it; the longest critical section is a `GameState::setup`.
  - **The lobby is a queue, not a slot, and a waiter's token is the seat's token.** `Connect` under `--bot-runner none` pairs with the first waiter in the same `room` (`None` is the public queue; a name pairs only with itself — the whole of "play against my friend") or joins the queue and is told so by the new `ServerMessage::Queued { session_token, position }`. `Resume` with that token while still queued swaps the socket under the queue entry and answers `Queued` again; the same token then arrives in `MatchJoined`. A separate lobby token was rejected (two credentials, and the CLI's `Reconnector` keys on one). An explicit `CreateMatch`/`JoinMatch` was rejected: a match only ever comes into being by pairing two waiters, so there is no open-match object for a second message to join.
  - **Ghosts are swept, not watched.** `bridge_websocket` tears both its halves down together, so a dead waiter's `tx.is_closed()` is true; the queue is filtered on every pairing and every `ListMatches` (so `waiting_in_lobby` never counts a ghost, which is what a client — and the test — polls to see one go). A swept waiter simply connects again: a queue position is not a seat with a game in it, so it gets no grace.
  - **`ClientMessage::ListMatches` → `ServerMessage::MatchList`**, answered before the handshake commits the socket to anything: `MatchSummary { match_id, corp, runner, started_secs_ago }`, the live waiter count and the cap. Player names finally go somewhere (`Connect.player_name` was only ever logged). **The seed is never on the wire**: with the fixed decklist it reproduces R&D's order.
  - **`--max-matches N`** refuses a `Connect` at the cap with the new `ServerMessage::ConnectRejected` and closes the socket, in both modes — a human `Connect` is refused *before* it is queued, since a queue entry that cannot be paired is a promise the daemon cannot keep. Queueing past the cap was rejected: it needs a wake-up from the session-exit task. Setup failure now uses the same variant (it was misreported as `ActionRejected`).
  - **`--seed` works again.** It was defeated: the accept loop added `rand::random()` per *connection*, and a human pairing used whichever connection arrived second. Match `n` now plays on `seed + n`, the headless driver's policy, and the index is claimed under the registry lock and never reused.
  - Pinned by `tests/lobby.rs` over real sockets: `two_humans_are_paired_into_one_match_on_opposite_sides`, `a_waiting_player_whose_socket_closed_is_never_paired`, `four_humans_make_two_concurrent_matches`, `a_queued_player_resumes_its_place_with_the_token`, `the_seed_policy_is_deterministic_and_per_match` (two daemons on `--seed 1` deal the same first match; the second match differs), `connect_is_refused_at_the_match_cap`, `rooms_only_pair_within_themselves`. `tests/reconnect.rs` is unchanged in substance. **No engine or bot code changed, so random-vs-random is untouched.**
  - CLI: `--room <name>` in remote mode; `connect_remote` reports `Queued` on stderr (it runs before `ratatui::init`) and turns `ConnectRejected` into the final `RemoteError::Rejected`. Still open: resuming a *lobby* place from the CLI (the token arrives before `MatchJoined`, and the retry loop keys on a seat), and a graceful shutdown (SIGINT already yields a clean client-visible disconnect; a graceful version needs a `ServerShutdown` message and a policy for live matches, which have no winner to award).
- [x] **Turn timers — DONE** (`feat/turn-timers`, 2 September 2026). `--turn-timeout-secs` (`ServeOptions.turn_timeout`, `MatchSession::with_turn_timeout`; off by default) is a **per-decision** clock beside the reconnect grace: a player who stays connected and simply never answers forfeits with the new `GameEndReason::TimedOut` — the third transport outcome after `Surrender` and `Disconnected`, reported by the pump with the other side as winner and a final state that is not `GameOver`; `classify_end_reason` never produces it. Four decisions, all on the `TurnTimeout` doc comment:
  - **Per decision, not per turn.** `await_seat` is entered once per `SessionStep::Awaiting`, which is exactly "this player was asked something and has not answered". A per-turn chess clock needs accounting across steps and a rule for the paid-ability windows the *opponent* opens during your turn; if anyone wants tournament pacing that is the open item, and the flag keeps its discoverable name meanwhile.
  - **A rejected action does not restart it.** The deadline lives on the session (`decision_deadline`) and is cleared only when an action *applies*, because a rejected submission re-enters `await_seat` for the same decision — otherwise a client could submit garbage to buy time. Not in the original design; found while writing the tests.
  - **A reattach does not restart it** (only the reconnect grace is reset then), or dropping the socket would. Both clocks can run for a detached seat and whichever fires first names the reason.
  - **It forfeits, never auto-plays.** There is no universally legal action to play — `EndTurn` is illegal under a pending decision — and choosing one would make the host a player.
  - `ServerMessage::DecisionClock { side, remaining }` goes to both seats when a clock starts and to a reattaching seat right after its resync `StateUpdate`, with what is left. A message rather than a `ClientView` field (the view is the engine's, and `netrunner_core` knows no wall clock); one per decision rather than ticks (the client counts down). Never sent when the clock is off, so a clock-less match's message sequence is exactly what it was. The TUI header shows `Clock: Corp 17s`.
  - Pinned under `start_paused` in `match_session.rs`: `a_connected_seat_that_never_moves_forfeits_at_the_turn_timeout`, `the_turn_clock_is_not_reset_by_a_reattach` (back with ten seconds left, told exactly that, still out), `a_submitted_action_starts_a_fresh_clock`, `a_rejected_action_does_not_restart_the_clock`, `the_reconnect_grace_still_wins_when_it_is_shorter`, `no_clock_message_is_sent_when_the_timeout_is_off`; over a socket, `a_client_that_sits_on_its_mulligan_is_timed_out`. **No engine or bot code changed** (`netrunner_session` gained a variant), so random-vs-random is untouched.
- [x] **Spectator channels — DONE** (`feat/spectators`, 2 September 2026). `ClientMessage::Spectate { match_id }` (an id from `MatchList`) is answered with `ServerMessage::Spectating` and then every `StateUpdate`, `ActionLog`, `DecisionClock` and `GameEnded` the seats get, masked for a spectator. The perspective is the engine's, not the host's:
  - **`rules::masking::Viewer { Player(Side), Spectator }` — a spectator sees the intersection of what the two players see.** Every gate in `masking` already had the shape "visible iff the viewer owns it, or it is public" (`mask_zone`, `mask_installed_card`, `mask_archived_card`, `mask_run_state`, the event and action maskers); each is now phrased as `viewer.is(owner)`, so a spectator — owner of neither — takes the hidden branch everywhere and **there is no third code path** to keep honest: neither hand, no deck, unrezzed ICE as the Runner sees it, an accessed HQ card as the Corp sees it, a Corp install in the log as the Runner reads it. Rejected: an omniscient "caster" mode — anyone may spectate, including a player of that very match, so a union view is a fog-of-war bypass; a delayed omniscient stream is a different feature with its own gate.
  - **Every masking entry point takes `impl Into<Viewer>`** (`build_client_view`, `mask_state_for_player`, `mask_action_for_player`, `mask_event_for_player`, `HistoryEntry::for_viewer`, `Session::{last_entry_for, view_for}`), so the ~45 callers that mask for a `Side` — every bot, the RL encoder, both TUIs — compile unchanged; wrapping each in `Viewer::Player` would have documented nothing. A parallel `build_spectator_view` was rejected: two entry points into one policy is how they drift. The one wire change: `ClientView.side: Side` → `viewer: Viewer`, and `legal_actions` is empty for a spectator (`legal_actions_for` is not even called). `observation.rs` reads `view.viewer.side()` once and panics on a spectator's view, which no training path can produce.
  - **The fog gate is strictly stronger now.** `no_client_view_or_log_entry_ever_names_a_card_it_conceals` checks the spectator's view after every `Awaiting` and its log entry after every action against the **union** of both seats' concealed sets — the own-zone limit the test records for a seat does not apply to a viewer that owns no zone. Plus four `masking` unit tests (`a_spectator_sees_neither_hand_nor_deck_and_each_board_as_the_opponent_does`, `…unrezzed_run_ice_as_the_runner_does_and_accessed_cards_as_the_corp_does`, `a_spectators_log_conceals_both_sides_card_naming_actions`, `a_spectator_gets_every_event_the_more_restricted_seat_gets_and_no_other`) and a `view` test.
  - **Server:** the session's one control channel carries `SeatControl::{Reattach, AddSpectator}` — a second channel would be a second `select!` arm doing the same thing — and `ReattachHandle::add_spectator` sends the current spectator view at once. `MatchSession.spectators` is a `Vec` of sinks: one spectator view is built per broadcast however many there are, and a closed sink is pruned at the send that finds it. A spectator holds no seat and no token: the daemon **drains and discards** what it sends — the receiving half has to stay open because `bridge_websocket` closes the socket when its send into a dropped receiver fails, so a dropped `rx` would have kicked the spectator on its first keypress. Reconnecting is `Spectate` again; `MatchJoined` with an optional side was rejected because its `assigned_side`/`session_token` are pinned by every reconnect test and the CLI's `Reconnector`.
  - Pinned by `a_spectator_gets_the_current_view_on_join_and_every_update_after`, `a_spectator_that_drops_is_pruned_without_disturbing_play`, `a_spectators_log_entry_conceals_both_sides` (the spectator's copy of a Corp install *equals* the Runner's) at the channel level; `a_spectator_joins_a_running_match_by_id` (which also sends a `Surrender` from the spectator and shows it costs nothing) and `spectating_an_unknown_match_is_refused` over sockets.
  - **CLI:** `netrunner_cli matches` lists the daemon's matches; `--spectate <id>` watches one; the header reads `You: Spectator`, and a lost spectator connection is not resumed (no token) — the notice says to spectate again. `App::human_side` → `viewer`.
  - **Measured:** random-vs-random (96 games, seed 1) byte-identical to `trk-random-random.json` — the masking change is read-only over the engine's events, so the only acceptable delta was none. Both 256-seed sweeps clean.
  - Still open, all deferred rather than forgotten: spectator counts/caps in `MatchSummary`; a delayed omniscient stream; the same `InstallId`-on-events limitation §1 records — a spectator's log drops the events a Runner's does.
- [x] **The daemon deals the published pool — DONE** (`feat/serve-plays-the-sample-pool`, 3 September 2026). Every match `--serve` hosted was seated on `fixtures::kate_vs_hb_decks()`: a Core-set-era Kate vs. Haas-Bioroid pair padded to legal size with blank `CardDefinition`s — no triggers, no abilities, cost 0 — so **24 of the Corp's 45 cards and 27 of the Runner's 45 did nothing at all**. Every human game the daemon ran, and every Glicko-2 rating it wrote to `--ratings-file` on both the human-vs-bot and human-vs-human tracks, was played on that board. Phase 2 §3 records what the same fixture did to self-play: 5,000 games, every one a Corp loss, no System Gateway card ever drawn. It was the last place in the workspace not playing the 192-matchup pool (Phase 1 §8's still-open list).
  - **`start_match` is the one seating decision**, so the change is one line there and one at `bind`: `fixtures::sample_registry()` is `register_playable_cards` and nothing else, and `fixtures::sample_decks_for_seed(seed)` is `decks::matchups()[seed % len]` — the same rotation `netrunner_cli --all-matchups` and the gym already use, over the seed `Registry::allocate` already derives per match. `--seed` now fixes *which matchups are dealt* as well as how each shuffles, which is a strictly stronger version of the reproducibility `the_seed_policy_is_deterministic_and_per_match` already asserted. `--corp-deck`/`--runner-deck` pin a side by published id, resolved once at `bind` so a misspelling is a daemon that refuses to start rather than one that refuses every client; each side pins independently.
  - **Deleting the fixture deleted a cross-crate coupling.** The wire protocol transmits no `CardRegistry`, so `netrunner_cli --mode remote` builds its own to resolve card titles — and while the filler existed, `netrunner_cli::decks` had to mirror `netrunner_server::fixtures`' seventeen synthetic ids constant for constant ("the filler card counts must match … exactly"). Both copies are gone; the two crates now agree by construction, because every card the daemon can deal is in the embedded pool. `netrunner_server --headless` moved onto the same rotation, for the same reason `netrunner_cli --headless` did: a driver that plays one fixed pairing measures that pairing, not the pool.
  - **The decklist ids are on the wire** (`MatchJoined`, `MatchSummary`, both `#[serde(default)]` so an older client still parses): with the pool rotating, two running matches are no longer the same game with different people in it, and a lobby that cannot say what a match is cannot be read. `netrunner_cli matches` prints them. The seed stays off the wire — it reproduces R&D's order — and that comment's old justification ("with the fixed decklist") was itself part of why this went unnoticed.
  - **No sweep was run and none was needed**: no engine or bot code changed, and the workspace's other drivers were already on the pool. `cargo test --workspace` green, clippy silent, plus two new socket tests — `each_match_is_dealt_a_published_matchup_and_the_pool_rotates` (both dealt matchups are in `decks::matchups()`, and consecutive matches differ) and `a_pinned_deck_is_dealt_to_every_match_and_a_bad_id_fails_to_bind` (the unpinned side still rotates; a bad id and a wrong-side id each refuse at `bind`). The eight `match_session` tests that used the fixture passed on real decks at their existing seeds, unchanged. **Verified live** against a running daemon: a `--mode remote` client seated as Corp was dealt `advanced_yomi` vs `dashing_mad`, the next client `advanced_yomi` vs `enthusiasm`, and `netrunner_cli matches` named both — 1,184 tests green.
  - Still open: `netrunner_single_player/tests/common/mod.rs` holds the last near-copy of the filler fixture — independent of the daemon and harmless, since it is a test's own scaffolding rather than a pool anything is rated on. The TUI header shows the phase and both economies but not the matchup, in local or remote mode; the identities are on the board.

### 4. Transport Efficiency
- [ ] **State deltas instead of full snapshots** — only once profiling shows full `ClientView` broadcasts are actually a bottleneck. Full snapshots are simple and correct; do not trade that away speculatively.
